# v0.3 implementation plan

> **Status:** plan, not contract. The binding contract is
> [`cli-design.md`](./cli-design.md). This document sequences the
> work needed to ship v0.3 of that contract; it doesn't redefine it.
> If a conflict arises, `cli-design.md` wins and this plan is updated.

## 1. Scope summary

v0.3 is "**Monday Dev + multi-profile** — agents bring their own
identity, narrate their work against sprint/epic/release/task
artefacts, and stop iterating boards by hand to find the things
they own." v0.2 gave agents the full mutating CRUD surface (item
lifecycle, board structure, comment threads); v0.3 layers four
distinct surfaces on top: (1) the slipped tentative-row writer
expansion (`tags` / `board_relation` / `dependency`) plus the
verb-shaped `time_tracking` extension that closes the
column-type roadmap for v0.3-target slots, (2) multi-profile
credentials cache (`monday auth login` + `~/.monday-cli/config.toml`)
plus the diagnostics cluster (`monday status` + `monday usage`)
that probes its state, (3) the cross-board read upgrades
(cross-board `item search`, `board favorites`, `item history`,
`item update --continue-on-error`) that lift the v0.1 single-board
read assumption, and (4) the `monday dev` namespace + outbound
write surface (notifications, webhooks). v0.3 is the first version
where an agent can fully self-onboard: log in, probe its
configuration, discover what's available, plan against a sprint,
and narrate progress without ever touching a Monday UI tab.

**In scope (from `cli-design.md` §13 v0.3 + §5.3 writer-expansion
roadmap):**

| Group | Commands |
|-------|----------|
| **column writers (slipped from v0.2)** | Friendly translators for `tags`, `board_relation`, `dependency` (the v0.2 tentative row that didn't converge in v0.2's window). `--set-raw` accepts these today; M19 lifts them into the friendly translator. |
| **column verbs (`time_tracking`)** | `item time-track start <iid>` / `item time-track stop <iid>` (verb-shaped, distinct from value writes — `time_tracking` is start/stop, not a settable JSON value). |
| **auth + profiles** | `monday auth login` (OAuth flow + credentials cache at `~/.monday-cli/credentials`, mode `0600`); `~/.monday-cli/config.toml` (multi-profile config — per-profile `api_token_env` reference + workspace + timezone overrides; tokens are NEVER stored in the config file per cli-design §7.2); `--profile <name>` global flag (selection order per cli-design §7.2: `--profile` flag > `MONDAY_PROFILE` env > `default_profile` in config > `MONDAY_API_TOKEN` env). |
| **diagnostics** | `monday status` (DNS / TCP / TLS / auth probe + cache writability + redaction self-test + env-var pickup summary; `--no-probe` skips the four network probes); `monday usage` (daily Monday API **operation** budget remaining via `platform_api.daily_limit` + `daily_analytics.by_day` — empirical-probe-confirmed at M22 pre-flight as operations, NOT complexity points; the per-minute complexity surface stays on v0.1 `account complexity`). |
| **cross-board reads** | `item search` cross-board (omit `--board`); `board favorites` (current user's starred boards); `item history <iid>` (per-item activity log — new §6 envelope shape). |
| **partial-success bulk** | `item update --continue-on-error` (new §6.4 sub-section — per-item `{ok, error?}` records vs the v0.2 fail-fast bulk path). |
| **`dev` namespace** | `dev sprint/epic/release/task` workflow shortcuts; `dev discover/configure/doctor` (cli-design §5.2 carve-out 1 — workflow namespaces). |
| **outbound writes** | `notification send --user <uid> --target <iid|bid> --target-type item|board --text <t>` (single-recipient per cli-design §4.3); `webhook list/create/delete` (board webhooks; CLI never *receives*). |
| **subitem expansion** | Multi-level subitem creation (slipped from v0.2 — Monday's `create_subitem` accepts a parent `subitem`, but the friendly translator only handles single-level today). **Contract-extension item** — not in cli-design §13 v0.3 list today; ships only after a pre-M28 cli-design §13 v0.3 amendment PR adds it (Decision 11 below). If the amendment is rejected, M28 drops the subitem work and ships polish + release prep only. |
| **polish** | Refreshed envelope-snapshot suite for v0.3 surfaces; `output-shapes.md` ToC audit; README quickstart updates; CHANGELOG `0.3.0` entry; version bump. |

**Universal partial-success rule (cli-design §6.1 compliant — same
shape as v0.2).** Multi-target verbs in v0.3 (M25 `item update
--continue-on-error` is the only v0.3 consumer; M27 `notification
send` stays single-recipient per cli-design §4.3) emit one
**success envelope** (`ok: true`) with per-target results in
`data.results: [...]`. Each record carries its own
`{<target_id_field>, ok: true | false, error?}`. The shared
per-target dispatch helper (`dispatchSequential` in
`src/api/partial-success-mutation.ts`, lifted in v0.2) takes the
id-field name as a parameter. The envelope's top-level
`error` slot is reserved for whole-call failure; all-failed-but-
each-call-attempted is still `ok: true` and the agent reads
`data.results` to determine outcomes. v0.3 introduces no contract
revision to §6.1.

**Out of scope (deferred per `cli-design.md` §13 "Explicitly
deferred from v0.1's stable contract"):**

- `item watch <iid>` (v0.4 — polling cadence question still open
  per cli-design §14.4).
- Bulk operations with `--concurrency` (v0.4 — probed against
  Monday's per-account concurrency cap).
- Asset upload (`add_file_to_column`, `add_file_to_update`) — the
  `files`-shaped column write path (v0.4).
- Shell completion (bash / zsh / fish) via commander (v0.4).
- `doc list/get` (v0.4 read-only; full docs CRUD deferred further).
- `team` create/manage (v0.4).
- Boolean filter DSL — v0.3 keeps the v0.1 / v0.2 `--where`
  operator allowlist + `--filter-json`. Pencilled at v0.3 stretch
  / v0.4 firm; only ships when fixture-driven adoption metrics
  show concrete demand.
- `monday item recreate-from-archive` — still no misleading
  `restore` command (cli-design §5.4 stands as a permanent
  non-goal-adjacent decision).

**Permanent non-goals (per cli-design §13 — reminded here so plan
readers don't propose them as v0.3 candidates):**

- Hosting webhooks (CLIs can't expose public HTTP — out of model);
  v0.3's `webhook create` is the *registration* shape against
  Monday, not a receive endpoint.
- App framework / installable monday apps (different surface
  area).
- Real-time GraphQL subscriptions (Monday's endpoint doesn't
  support them).
- Telemetry, update-notifier, analytics — ever.
- Forms (Monday's public-submission feature) — same hosted-
  endpoint shape as webhooks.
- Saved queries / aliases — would silently change behavior across
  machines; shell aliases / shell functions are the established
  UNIX answer.
- `monday undo` (replay-based reversal of recent mutations) —
  Monday's state model is authoritative and concurrent; honest
  substitutes are `--dry-run` (v0.1) and `item history` (v0.3 —
  M24 below).

## 2. Pre-flight: what's already in place

Working at HEAD (`main`, post-`6d93f65`). The post-v0.2 → v0.3
cleanup window closed; **2296 tests passing**; coverage 99.05 /
95.47 / 99.51 / 99.18 (statements / branches / functions / lines)
above the 95/95.45/95/95 floor. `monday-cli@0.2.0` published to
npm 2026-05-08.

What v0.3 inherits from M0–M18:

- **Foundations (M0–M2.5)** — runner / signal handling / typed
  errors / redaction / envelope builders / commander wiring /
  transport seam / retry layer / `MondayClient` / `account`
  commands all live and stable. v0.3 inherits unchanged.
- **Read surface (M3–M4)** — `workspace`, `board`, `user`,
  `update`, `item` reads (incl. filter DSL, cursor pagination,
  NDJSON streaming, projection layer). v0.3's M23 cross-board
  search + M24 history + M27 webhook list lean on these for
  resolution: `--board <bid>` lookup, column resolution, `me`
  token, board metadata cache.
- **Write surface (M5a–M5b + M8–M18)** — every mutation verb
  v0.2 ships, plus the §5.3 writable allowlist (10 friendly
  types: `text`/`long_text`/`numbers`/`status`/`dropdown`/`date`/
  `people` from M5a + `link`/`email`/`phone` from M8) and the
  `--set-raw` escape hatch. **M19's tentative-row work plugs
  into this** — three new translator slots in the same
  dispatcher, one new account-tag directory module, one
  per-board-relation-allowed-boards check.
- **R-class generalised helpers** — `assertResponseFieldPresent`
  (R42, accepts `details: Record<string, unknown>` map +
  `nullHandling: 'caller_handles' | 'throw_not_found'`
  discriminant), `withBoardInvalidation*` (R46, single-leg + fan-
  out wrappers), `findBoardChildOrThrow` (R51), `startNdjsonStream`
  (R52), `buildStreamingTrailerMeta` (R53), per-noun projection
  helpers `projectMutationItem` / `projectMutationUpdate` /
  `projectMutationBoard` / `projectMutationColumn` /
  `projectMutationGroup` (R28 / R37 / R43 / R45 / R48).
  v0.3 mutation surfaces consume these on day one.
- **Cache invalidation discipline (cli-design §8)** —
  `invalidateBoard(boardId)` plus the §8 leg-count split
  (single-leg vs fan-out). v0.3's diagnostics cluster (M22)
  reports cache state through this. **M27 webhook mutations
  are live-only** — webhooks aren't part of §8's cache scope
  (which covers boards / users / schema only) and adding them
  is out-of-scope for v0.3 per the §6 risk register entry.
- **Partial-success envelope** (cli-design §6.1 with v0.2's
  `data.results: [...]` per-target shape) — M25 `item update
  --continue-on-error` is the first v0.3 consumer.
- **Streaming helpers (R52 + R53)** —
  `startNdjsonStream` / `walkPages.onItem` / `paginate.onItem`
  with proper `'drain'` backpressure. M23 cross-board `item
  search` is the first v0.3 consumer (one walker per board, fan
  out across favorites or workspace scope).
- **Dry-run engine** (M5a, cli-design §9.2) — v0.3's M19
  tentative-row friendly translators emit dry-run shapes
  through this engine on day one.
- **Output-shapes contract** (`docs/output-shapes.md`) —
  92 envelope snapshots after M18; v0.3's M28 release prep adds
  ~30+ new snapshots covering cross-board search, history,
  diagnostics, dev verbs, notifications, webhooks.
- **Test infrastructure** — fixture transport, fixture server
  (E2E), redaction-hardening suite, agent-flow E2E, NDJSON
  streaming integration tests. **2296 tests passing**.
- **Tooling** — ESM, NodeNext, strictest TS, ESLint
  `strictTypeChecked + stylisticTypeChecked`, vitest 4. CI runs
  typecheck + lint + test:coverage + build smoke on Node 22 and
  24. Two-AI review (Codex `gpt-5.5`, read-only) per design
  change and per implementation milestone.

What's **missing** that v0.3 needs (each filled by the named
milestone):

- An **account-tag directory** module (M19) mirroring the
  `userByEmail` user-directory pattern in `src/api/resolvers.ts`
  (M5a — there is no standalone `user-directory.ts` module; the
  user-directory cache / lookup / refresh-on-miss machinery lives
  inside `resolvers.ts` lines 192–402). Monday's tags are account-
  scoped (`account_tags` returns the directory; `tags` column
  values reference tag IDs). The friendly translator needs to
  resolve `<tag1>,<tag2>` to tag IDs at boundary; misses surface
  the `tag_not_found` error code (registered pre-M19 at `4c652d5`
  per §8 decision 1).
- A **linked-board enumeration + complexity-budget** path (M19)
  for `board_relation` and `dependency`. Monday's wire shape is
  `{ item_ids: [N1, N2] }` referencing items in the column's
  `settings.boardIds ?? [settings.boardId]`. The friendly
  translator validates input items belong to those allowed
  boards before constructing the wire payload — a per-call
  Monday lookup with complexity-budget interaction (see §6
  risk register).
- A **multi-profile config loader** (M21) — v0.1/v0.2 read
  `MONDAY_API_TOKEN` from env or `.env`. v0.3 adds
  `~/.monday-cli/config.toml` with `[profiles.<name>]` blocks +
  `--profile <name>` global flag. **Selection order is fixed by
  cli-design §7.2:** `--profile` flag > `MONDAY_PROFILE` env >
  `default_profile` in config > `MONDAY_API_TOKEN` env (falls
  back to v1 mode if no config file exists). **Tokens are never
  stored in the config file** — profile entries reference an env
  var name via `api_token_env = "MONDAY_API_TOKEN_<NAME>"` per
  cli-design §7.2.
- A **credentials cache primitive** (M21) —
  `~/.monday-cli/credentials` (mode `0600`) for `monday auth
  login`'s OAuth flow result per cli-design §7.3. Format
  question is open (cli-design §14.5); default leans toward our
  own JSON.
- A **self-test harness** (M22) for the diagnostics cluster —
  mockable transport seam for `monday status`'s probes (so
  status itself is testable without real network), plus a
  `platform_api.daily_limit` + `daily_analytics.by_day` query
  path for `monday usage` (operations per day, NOT
  complexity points — per the M22 pre-flight empirical-probe
  finding that `account.complexity` doesn't exist and the
  daily-budget surface lives under `platform_api`).
- A **per-item event projection layer** (M24) — `item history`
  surfaces Monday's `activity_logs` entries as event objects with
  `created_at`, `actor_id`, `kind`, `before` / `after`. Distinct
  from existing item / update projections; a new §6 envelope
  shape (cli-design §13 v0.3 entry).
- A **workflow-shortcut layer** (M26) over the v0.1/v0.2
  CRUD surface — `dev sprint`, `dev epic`, `dev release`,
  `dev task` are convention-driven helpers (per cli-design
  §2.7 — Monday Dev is convention, not API). They translate
  workflow vocabulary to standard board / item operations.
- An **outbound notification + webhook surface** (M27) —
  `notification send` (Monday's `create_notification` mutation;
  per-user-id targeting), `webhook list/create/delete`
  (`webhooks` query + `create_webhook` / `delete_webhook`
  mutations). New nouns. **Webhooks are live-only for v0.3** —
  not part of cli-design §8's cache scope (boards / users /
  schema only). If webhook caching becomes load-bearing later,
  it lands as a v0.3.x or v0.4 §8 contract-extension PR.
- A **multi-level subitem creation** path (M28) — Monday allows
  `create_subitem` with a parent that is itself a subitem; the
  current friendly translator caps at single-level. Shipping
  multi-level requires hierarchy validation (depth limit per
  Monday's policy — typically 3 levels).

## 3. Milestone sequencing

Ten milestones (M19–M28). Each milestone is a coherent commit
cluster (3–10 commits) ending on a green tree with the listed
exit criteria met. Earlier milestones unblock later ones; do not
start M(N) until M(N-1)'s exit criteria are satisfied. Each
milestone updates `docs/output-shapes.md` for any new command
shapes it lands and adds envelope snapshots for any new emitted
shape.

Order at a glance: **writer-expansion close → time_tracking →
auth foundations → diagnostics → cross-board reads → history →
partial-success bulk → dev namespace → outbound writes → subitem
expansion + release prep**.

```
       M19                                — writer expansion close (slipped tentative row)
       M20                                — time_tracking start/stop verbs
       M21 ─ M22                          — auth foundations (login + profiles) → diagnostics (status + usage)
       M23 ─ M24 ─ M25                    — cross-board reads (search + favorites) → history → partial-success bulk
       M26                                — dev namespace (workflow shortcuts)
       M27                                — outbound writes (notifications + webhooks)
       M28                                — subitem expansion + polish + 0.3.0 release prep
```

### M19 — Writer expansion close (slipped tentative row)

**The opening v0.3 milestone.** Closes the column-type
roadmap's v0.2 carryover: the three tentative-row types
(`tags`, `board_relation`, `dependency`) that slipped from v0.2
at M18 close (per cli-design §5.3 writer-expansion roadmap +
§13 v0.3 entry) get their friendly translators. Mirrors M8's
foundational role for v0.2 — the slipped row was always
intended as the first thing v0.3 picked up, since later v0.3
milestones don't depend on it. The runtime
`unsupported_column_type` errors for these three types
already carry `deferred_to: "v0.3"` (per the M18 round-2 P2
fix); landing the friendly translators flips them to
writable.

**Status: M19 closed.** Six implementation commits + one mid-
milestone-pause docs commit shipped across two sessions; the
v0.2 writer-expansion roadmap's tentative row (`tags` /
`board_relation` / `dependency`) graduated into the friendly
`--set` translator allowlist; `monday account tags` read verb
shipped (closes the §6.5 `tag_not_found.details.hint` forward-
reference). The full M19 post-mortem lands in §11 below.

**Shipped commits (in order):**

  raw-writable comment refresh). Combined to one commit
  because the Codex fixes touch the same docs the close
  sweep touches; splitting would have created two near-
  identical doc-sweep commits.

**Why this goes first.** Three reasons: (1) Continuity —
v0.2's writer-expansion narrative finishes here; agents
reading the changelog see "v0.2 firm row → v0.3 tentative
row" as one connected story. (2) Pre-trodden ground — the
M5a translator dispatcher + the M8 `--set-raw` resolution
path + the R-class generalised helpers (R42's
`assertResponseFieldPresent`, R28's `projectMutationItem`)
all give M19 day-one infrastructure. The risk vector is
the per-type design clearance, not the implementation
shape. (3) No dependency on later v0.3 surfaces — M19 can
land without auth profiles (M21), without the diagnostics
cluster (M22), without `dev` namespace (M26). Any other
v0.3 milestone going first would either depend on an open
design question (auth caching format, complexity-budget
interaction with cross-board search) or strand the v0.2
carryover indefinitely.

**Pre-flight design clearance** (must close before any feat
commit lands; mirrors M16's seven-round pre-flight cadence
that pinned 22 contract bugs before code shipped):

1. **`tags` translator shape.** Input form: `<tag1>,<tag2>`
   (comma-split, trimmed). Output wire shape:
   `{ tag_ids: [N1, N2] }`. Resolution: per-account tag
   directory lookup. New `src/api/tag-directory.ts`
   module mirrors the `userByEmail` user-directory
   pattern in `src/api/resolvers.ts` (M5a precedent —
   account-scoped lookup, cache-aware via §8
   invalidation, exposes
   `resolveTags({ client, input, env?, noCache? }):
   Promise<{ ids, misses, source }>` and
   `loadAccountTags({ client, env?, noCache? }):
   Promise<{ tags, source, cacheAgeSeconds }>` — the
   M19 pre-flight contract diff at `d822982` pinned
   the surface as object-form inputs with a required
   `MondayClient`, mirroring `UserByEmailInputs`
   verbatim). Cache key shape:
   `{ kind: 'account-tags' }` (single-account scope; no
   per-board fan-out). Misses surface as a single
   `tag_not_found` error code with `details: { tags:
   <misses[]>, hint: 'Run \`monday account tags\` to
   list available tags.' }` — array shape per §8
   Decision 1 (multi-miss `--set tags=foo,bar,baz`
   surfaces all missing tags in one envelope, NOT one
   error per miss). The hint hard-pins `monday account
   tags` as a v0.3 read-verb deliverable per the
   §6.5 PR's forward-looking commitment (cli-design.md
   §6.5 entry landed at `4c652d5`).
2. **`board_relation` translator shape.** Input form:
   `<iid1>,<iid2>` (comma-split item IDs). Output wire
   shape: `{ item_ids: [N1, N2] }`. Resolution: per-board
   `column.settings.boardIds ?? [column.settings.boardId]`
   list (already in board metadata via M3); validate that
   each input item ID belongs to one of those allowed
   boards. Mismatched item IDs surface as
   `usage_error` with `details: { item_id: <id>,
   allowed_boards: [...], hint: 'item belongs to board X
   but column accepts only [...]' }`. **Complexity-budget
   interaction:** validating each input item via
   `items(ids: [N])` query would burn complexity per item;
   batch via `items(ids: [N1, N2, ...])` to amortise.
   Decision: batch with a hard cap of 25 items per
   `--set` call (matching Monday's documented
   relation-column item cap); >25 surfaces `usage_error`
   pre-network.
3. **`dependency` translator shape.** Identical wire shape
   to `board_relation` (`{ item_ids: [N1, N2] }`); the
   distinction is that Monday's `dependency` column has
   its own settings and its own per-call quirks. Validate
   against `column.settings.dependencyBoards ?? [...]`
   (Monday's separate `dependency` settings field).
   Otherwise treat as a sibling of `board_relation` —
   same resolution path, same complexity-budget
   interaction, same per-call cap.
4. **Clear payloads** (cli-design §5.3 clear table). All
   three types clear to `{}` via `change_column_value`
   (matching v0.2's expansion of the per-type clear
   table; see cli-design §5.3 "Clearing column values").
   Bulk `item clear --where ... <col>` (already shipped
   in M12) inherits this on day one.
5. **Dry-run shape.** Per cli-design §5.3 dry-run example
   block (lines 2416–2434) — `data: null`,
   `meta.dry_run: true`, `planned_changes: [...]` array.
   Each tentative-row type emits `resolved_ids` (column
   ID resolution) + `diff` (`{from: previous, to: new}`)
   + `details` (resolution-source hint: tag-directory
   match for `tags`; allowed-boards-validated for
   `board_relation` / `dependency`).
6. **`example_set` per writable column** (cli-design §3.2
   `board describe`). All three new types need
   `example_set` strings — `"tag1,tag2"` for `tags`,
   `"<iid1>,<iid2>"` for `board_relation` /
   `dependency`. Update `commands/board/describe.ts`
   `example_set` builder to cover the new types so
   `board describe` is self-sufficient for agents
   constructing M19-class `--set` calls.

**Deliverables**

- `src/api/column-values.ts` — three new tentative-row
  translator slots:
  - `tags` — `<tag1>,<tag2>` (comma-split, trimmed,
    deduplicated) → `{ tag_ids: [N1, N2] }` after
    `resolveTags()` lookup. Empty input rejected with
    `usage_error`.
  - `board_relation` — `<iid1>,<iid2>` (comma-split item
    IDs, trimmed) → `{ item_ids: [N1, N2] }` after the
    allowed-boards check. Empty input clears (consistent
    with v0.2 expansion clear semantics: `{}` via
    `change_column_value`).
  - `dependency` — same shape as `board_relation` against
    Monday's `dependency` settings field.
- `src/api/tag-directory.ts` — new module exporting
  `resolveTags({ client, input, env?, noCache? }):
  Promise<ResolveTagsResult>` and
  `loadAccountTags({ client, env?, noCache? }):
  Promise<LoadAccountTagsResult>` (object-form inputs
  with a required `MondayClient` per the M19 pre-flight
  contract diff at `d822982`).
  `ResolveTagsResult = { ids: readonly number[],
  misses: readonly string[], source: 'cache' | 'live'
  | 'mixed', cacheAgeSeconds: number | null }`
  (Commit 2 amendment to the pre-flight diff —
  `cacheAgeSeconds` added so the translator-source
  aggregation pathway can populate
  `meta.cache_age_seconds` per §6.1; `cacheHitAge`
  preserved for `'mixed'` source to surface worst-case
  staleness alongside the cache + live merge).
  `LoadAccountTagsResult = { tags: readonly AccountTag
  [], source: 'cache' | 'live', cacheAgeSeconds:
  number | null, complexity: Complexity | null }`
  (Commit 2 amendment — `complexity` slot mirrors
  `BoardMetadataLoadResult` so `monday account tags
  --verbose` can report budget like other cache-backed
  reads). Mirrors the `userByEmail` user-directory
  pattern in `src/api/resolvers.ts` (the existing
  precedent — there is no standalone
  `user-directory.ts` module; the user directory cache
  / lookup / refresh-on-miss machinery lives inside
  `resolvers.ts` lines 192–402). New cache-key kind
  `{ kind: 'accountTags' }` (camelCase TS literal,
  matching `schemaVersion`'s convention; on-disk path
  `account_tags/index.json` snake-case mirrors the dir
  name; `CacheEntryInfo.kind: 'account_tags'` snake-
  case matches the dir name + the `cache list`
  per-kind label — Commit 2 amendment to the pre-flight
  diff which used kebab-case `'account-tags'` as a
  placeholder).
  **Misses surface as a single `tag_not_found` error
  with `details: { tags: misses, hint: <string> }`** —
  array shape (NOT one error per miss) so a multi-miss
  `--set tags=foo,bar,baz` surfaces all three missing
  tags in one envelope (per Decision 1 below; cli-
  design §6.5 entry landed at `4c652d5`).
- `src/api/board-relation-validation.ts` — new helper
  `validateBoardRelationItems({ client, itemIds,
  allowedBoards, context, env?, noCache? })` that
  batches a single live `items(ids: [...])` query and
  returns `{ ok: true } | { ok: false, mismatches:
  readonly BoardRelationMismatch[] }`. The M19 pre-flight
  contract diff at `d822982` pinned the
  surface — `MondayClient` is required (no cache
  fallback since item board membership can change
  between calls), and the per-call cap
  (`BOARD_RELATION_MAX_ITEMS = 25` per Monday's
  documented relation-column item ceiling) lives
  alongside the validator. Used by both
  `board_relation` and `dependency` translator slots.
- `src/api/column-types.ts` — `WRITABLE_COLUMN_TYPES`
  grows from 10 to 13. Update `unsupportedColumnTypeError`
  to drop `tags`, `board_relation`, `dependency` from the
  `V0_2_WRITER_EXPANSION_TYPES` set (the runtime
  constant name retains its M8-era spelling per the
  stability comment at `column-types.ts` lines 209–211 —
  renaming would churn every consumer with no wire-shape
  change; the M18 round-2 P2 fix already documents that
  the category-name `'v0_2_writer_expansion'` covers the
  v0.3-deferred tentative row). Those errors will no
  longer fire — the friendly translator handles them
  now. Tentative-row reclassification: the `deferred_to:
  "v0.3"` value-write row in `unsupportedColumnTypeError`'s
  category table loses its three members. **`time_tracking`
  stays in a v0.3 verb-shaped category** (per cli-design §5.3
  + §13 v0.3 — verb-shaped extension, NOT v0.4 / files-
  shaped) — its `unsupported_column_type` error keeps
  `deferred_to: "v0.3"` and grows a `hint:
  'time_tracking is verb-shaped; use monday item time-
  track start/stop'`. M20 lands the verb surface and the
  hint becomes self-fulfilling; M19 pins the verb-shaped
  category boundary in the meantime so `time_tracking`
  never accidentally enters the v0.4 / files-shaped
  partition.
- `src/utils/errors.ts` — `tag_not_found` widens
  `ERROR_CODES` (28 total post-M19; cli-design §6.5
  entry landed at `4c652d5`). Pre-flight contract diff
  lands the union widening + corresponding entries in
  `CODE_RETRYABLE_DEFAULT` / `CODE_TYPICAL_HTTP_STATUS`
  / `exitCodeForError` switch ahead of any throw site,
  so M19 implementation feat commits land throws into
  a stable typed surface.
- **`monday account tags` read verb** — required by
  the §6.5 `tag_not_found.details.hint` forward-
  reference. Lands inside M19 implementation OR as a
  fast-follow v0.3.x sidecar; the call is made at M19
  implementation kickoff. NOT a contract change once
  it ships (the verb just resolves the hint's existing
  commitment); just a deliverable scope question. If
  the call is "M19-fold", it ships alongside the
  `tags` translator + `tag-directory.ts`; if it's
  "v0.3.x sidecar", M19 ships without it and a v0.3.x
  patch lands the verb when the cli-design §4.3
  `account` namespace gets its second verb (currently
  only `whoami` lives there).
- `src/commands/item/set.ts` + `src/commands/item/
  update.ts` — wire the new translator slots through the
  existing dispatcher. Bulk `item update --where` and
  bulk `item clear --where ... <col>` inherit
  automatically (the dispatcher is the seam; no per-
  command edits needed).
- `src/commands/item/create.ts` — `create_item`'s
  `column_values` accepts the new types. Existing test
  cassettes need extension to cover M19-class
  create-with-tags / create-with-relation cases.
- `src/commands/board/describe.ts` — `example_set`
  builder gets the three new types. Snapshot refresh
  follows.
- Output-shapes.md — three new entries (tags-set / tags-
  clear, board_relation-set / -clear, dependency-set /
  -clear). ~6 new envelope snapshots.

**Exit criteria**

- All three friendly translators ship with full per-type
  unit + integration test coverage (happy path + every

- `WRITABLE_COLUMN_TYPES` is 13.
- `tag_not_found` registered in cli-design §6.5 (planning
  doc edit lands as a separate pre-M19 contract-change
  PR with its own Codex review per CLAUDE.md workflow
  rules — NOT in M19's feat commits).
- Coverage holds at or above 95.45% branches; M19 is a
  good candidate for the focused coverage-push that
  recovers toward 95.5% (see §7 release exit checklist).
- 2296 → ~2330 tests passing (M5a expanded ~30 tests per

- Codex pre-flight pass before first feat commit
  (mirrors M16's `c0efab5`); Codex implementation pass
  before M19 close.
- `docs/output-shapes.md` ToC updated; envelope snapshots
  refreshed.

  written at M19 close (mirrors v0.2-plan §10 M8 post-
  mortem).

### M20 — `time_tracking` start/stop verbs

**Closed (documentation-only).** Empirical probe at M20
implementation (2026-05-10, against API version `2026-01`)
confirmed Monday's public GraphQL API does **not** currently
support writing to `time_tracking` columns: `change_simple_
column_value` rejects every candidate value with
`CorrectedValueException`; `change_column_value` rejects every
candidate JSON shape with `InvalidColumnTypeException`; full
mutation-root introspection (152 mutations) found zero
time-tracking-related mutations. The pre-flight contract
assumed `change_simple_column_value` would route through; that
assumption was empirically wrong.

The verbs ship as **forward-compatibility markers** —
registered in the CLI surface so agent scripts targeting
`monday item time-track start/stop` are stable across the
eventual swap when Monday ships API support — and reject every
invocation today with `usage_error` carrying the empirical
probe context (date / API version / Monday's exact error codes
for each candidate wire shape) so an agent reading
`details.hint` can self-verify the limitation.

**Verb-shaped column-type extension.** `time_tracking` is
distinct from M19's value-write surface — it's start/stop
semantics, not a settable JSON value. New surface:
`monday item time-track start <iid> [--column <col>]` /
`monday item time-track stop <iid> [--column <col>]`.
Three-level command depth (`item time-track <verb>`) is
permitted by cli-design §5.2 carve-out 2 (verb-shaped
column-type extensions); the existing `dev` namespace
remains carve-out 1 (workflow namespaces).

**Scope size:** S (documentation-only at v0.3 — no wire
mutation; no cache surface; no board-invalidation fan-out;
no new error codes — Decisions 4.1 / 4.2 reuse `usage_error`).

**Decisions closed at M20 pre-flight (this commit):**

- **Decision 4.1 — `start` against a running column:**
  Closed: `usage_error` (reuses the existing code rather
  than introducing `time_tracking_already_running`).
  `details: { board_id, item_id, column_id, running:
  true, hint }`. Hint points at `monday item time-track
  stop <iid> --column <col>`. Reasoning: agents already
  branch on the verb they invoked, and `details.running:
  true` carries the discriminant — adding a per-state-bug
  ERROR_CODE widens the contract surface for marginal
  benefit. The recommended `--restart` flag mention from
  the original v0.3-plan paragraph dropped — `--restart`
  is a forward-looking flag we haven't designed; promising
  it in the hint commits us to v0.3 scope creep. The
  two-step `stop` then `start` path is the agent's
  recovery surface.
- **Decision 4.2 — `stop` against a non-running column:**
  Closed: `usage_error` (symmetric to 4.1; no
  `time_tracking_not_running` code). `details: {
  board_id, item_id, column_id, running: false, hint }`;
  hint points at `monday item time-track start <iid>
  --column <col>`. Mirrors `start`'s explicit-state
  convention and 4.1's reasoning verbatim.
- **Decision 4.3 — Idempotency:** Closed: BOTH verbs are
  non-idempotent. `start` opens a new history session in
  valid pre-state (each call against a stopped column
  appends a new session); `stop` closes the just-running
  session. Both throw `usage_error` on invalid pre-state
  per 4.1 / 4.2. Trade-off documented: agents needing
  best-effort `stop` swallow the typed error envelope (the
  `details.running: false` discriminant) — `monday item
  get` does NOT surface the time-tracking running flag
  in the v0.3-M20-pre-flight item-read projection (see
  M20-implementation deliverables below for the optional
  projection widening). The alternative `stop` shape
  (no-op-on-stopped, idempotent retry-safe) is defensible
  but breaks symmetry with `start` and hides state from
  agents that branch on the response — Decision 4.3 commits
  to symmetry over no-op convenience.

**Stub deliverables (shipped in pre-flight at `a702af2`):**
`src/api/time-tracking.ts` — module signatures
(`StartTimeTrackingInputs` / `StartTimeTrackingResult` /
`StopTimeTrackingInputs` / `StopTimeTrackingResult` +
the two stub-body verbs throwing `internal_error` until
M20 implementation lands).

**Deliverables shipped at M20 implementation (this close):**

- `src/api/time-tracking.ts` — runtime bodies replace the
  pre-flight `internal_error` stubs with `usage_error`
  rejections carrying the `API_UNSUPPORTED_HINT` (named
  the empirical probe). The four `*Inputs` / `*Result`
  interfaces stay verbatim from the pre-flight — they
  describe the FUTURE shape that materialises when Monday
  ships API support and the rejection bodies swap to real
  wire calls.
- `src/commands/item/time-track/start.ts` +
  `src/commands/item/time-track/stop.ts` — three-level
  command depth (per cli-design §5.2 carve-out 2; uses
  nested `ensureSubcommand` to register `monday item
  time-track start` / `... stop`). Argv shape mirrors the
  future implementation (`<iid>` positional + `--column
  <col>` + `--board <bid>` + `--dry-run`). `--board`
  resolution preserved at v0.3 (mirrors `item set` / `item
  clear` / `item archive`'s standard pattern — invalid item
  IDs surface as `not_found` for agent UX consistency
  across item verbs); `--column` resolution intentionally
  skipped (the api primitive throws regardless of column
  validity).
- `src/commands/index.ts` — registry slots for both verbs
  (commands array grows 70 → 72; the `command_count`
  envelope assertion in the schema snapshot moves
  accordingly).
- `src/api/column-values.ts` — `unsupportedColumnTypeError`
  time_tracking-branch hint update. The hint now names the
  documentation-only verbs by their argv shape AND carries
  the empirical-probe caveat so an agent who hits
  `unsupported_column_type` for a `--set time_tracking=...`
  call gets a copy-pasteable next command alongside the
  honest "the verbs throw too" disclaimer.
- Test coverage: `tests/unit/api/time-tracking.test.ts`
  expanded from 9 surface tests to 16 full-coverage tests
  (rejection code, message naming, input echo into
  details, hint substring assertions for the empirical-
  probe context, symmetry between start + stop).
  `tests/integration/commands/item-time-track.test.ts`
  ships 8 cassettes (— `--board` supplied, `--board`
  omitted with valid item, `--board` omitted with
  invalid item → `not_found`, non-numeric item ID →
  parse-boundary `usage_error`, omitted `--column` echoes
  empty string verbatim, token redaction across error
  envelopes; symmetric coverage for `stop`).

**Deliverables explicitly NOT shipped (scope-creep avoided):**

- **Item-read projection widening for `monday item get`** —
  pre-flight noted as optional; deferred since the verbs
  themselves cannot fire today, so the agent flow that
  would consume the `TimeTrackingValue.{running, started_
  at}` projection (best-effort `stop` per Decision 4.3) is
  also non-functional. Re-evaluate if Monday ships API
  support; tracked at §22 as a v0.3 cleanup-window
  candidate.
- **Wire-level mutation wiring** — see "Closed
  (documentation-only)" block above. The runtime body
  swap is one-sided in `src/api/time-tracking.ts` once
  Monday ships API support; agent-facing argv shape is
  stable.

**Codex implementation review:** Per project workflow, the
close commit ships after a Codex pass. Review prompt
focuses on cross-doc shape consistency (cli-design §4.3 vs
§6.4 vs output-shapes.md vs the runtime emission), the
`unsupportedColumnTypeError` hint-update consistency
across `column-values.ts` + the corresponding test
assertion, the documentation-only framing readability for
agents grepping the envelope's `details.hint`, and whether
any finding from the pre-flight Codex pass (rounds 1+2 at
`a702af2`) drifted under the documentation-only pivot. The
R-class quality refactor candidates from the pre-flight
handoff (`peopleResolution` parity fix,
`unsupportedColumnTypeError` category-table dispatch) all
shipped in prior cleanup windows; nothing surfaces here.

### M21 — `monday auth login` + multi-profile config

**Status: closed.** Part 1 shipped 2026-05-10 across three feat
commits (`5c1f7ac` + `81eec03` + `a4cb5b0`) + drift sweep
(`e767249`); Part 2 shipped this session across four commits
on `main` (`1cd660a feat(m21-pt2): redaction-runtime extension`
+ `3b5cb30 test(m21-pt2): leak-test canary discipline` +
`1c77699 refactor(r-new-1): lift isENOENT into src/utils/fs` +
`e21c166 fix(m21-pt2): apply Codex round-1 P1 + P2 + P3` —
plus this close-docs commit). **M22 unblocked.**

**Part 1** (prior session): runtime bodies for the OAuth flow
(real `bindOAuthListener` + `exchangeCode`), credentials cache
(read/write/delete/resolve with atomic-replace + read-time
`fs.fstat` mode check), profiles config (TOML loader via
`smol-toml`), auth login/logout commands (8-step §7.3.1 flow),
and `cli/program.ts` profile-resolution preAction hook. Floor
adjusted 95.45 → 94.5 with documented rationale; recovered to
95.45 at `7058754` (post-M21 coverage-push session, before M22
pre-flight).

**Part 2** (this session): redaction-runtime extension folding
loaded `access_token` values into `ctx.runtimeSecrets` for the
value-scan filter; M2-style leak-test canary discipline
(`tok-cred-leak-deadbeef-canary` sweep across 10 emission paths);
R-NEW-1 `isENOENT` lift into `src/utils/fs.ts` (3-consumer
trigger fired); Codex 2-round implementation review (1 P1 +
2 P2 + 1 P3 round-1, 0 P1 + 1 P2 close-docs scope + 1 P3
round-2 — all applied inline). See §11 M21 post-mortem.

Full close marker: this status flip + §9 preconditions tick +
§11 post-mortem + §22 R-NEW-1 lift confirmation + 3 new
R-watch-items.

**Foundational v0.3 milestone for everything that follows.**
Multi-profile credentials cache via OAuth flow + new
`~/.monday-cli/config.toml` format with per-profile token,
workspace, timezone, and API-version overrides. New
`--profile <name>` global flag.

**Scope size:** L (new boundary surface — config loader,
OAuth flow, credentials cache primitive, per-profile env
resolution, mode `0600` file primitive, redaction
extension). The biggest contract-touching milestone of
v0.3.

**Decisions to close before kickoff** (Decision 3 closed
in this commit ahead of the M21 pre-flight contract diff
via cli-design §7.4 extension PR with Codex review; the
rest are pinned by cli-design §7.2 / §7.3 already):
- **Auth caching format** (Decision 3 — **closed:
  `3eba714`** via cli-design §7.4). Plain JSON at
  `~/.monday-cli/credentials` with mode `0600`; not
  `gh`/`aws`-style. Per-profile schema with
  `schema_version: "1"` + `{access_token, obtained_at,
  expires_at, scopes, account_id}`; atomic-replace via
  temp-file + `fs.rename` mirroring `src/api/cache.ts`
  `writeJsonFile`; read-time `fs.fstat`-against-open-
  descriptor permission check. Profile config
  (`config.toml`) stays human-editable; credentials
  stay machine-managed. Per-profile token source order
  (also pinned in §7.4.1): credentials cache > profile's
  `api_token_env` > `config_error`.
- **Source priority** (pinned by §7.2). `--profile` flag
  > `MONDAY_PROFILE` env > `default_profile` in config >
  `MONDAY_API_TOKEN` env. M21 implements this exactly.
- **Token storage** (pinned by §7.2). Tokens are NEVER
  stored in `config.toml`. Profile entries reference an
  env var name (`api_token_env = "MONDAY_API_TOKEN_X"`)
  or rely on the credentials cache file. M21 enforces
  this at parse boundary — config-time tokens reject
  with `config_error`.
- **Multi-account vs multi-token-same-account.** Profiles
  are account-scoped — each profile authenticates
  against a (possibly different) Monday account. Tested
  via fixture transport switch.
- **OAuth flow** (pinned by §7.3 — `monday auth login` is
  OAuth + credentials cache, not paste-in). M21 ships
  the full OAuth dance: browser open + localhost
  callback + code exchange + credentials write. **If the
  OAuth dance proves out of session-budget at M21
  kickoff, paste-in becomes a contract-extension PR
  (cli-design §7.3 amendment) BEFORE M21 first feat
  commit, NOT an M21 implementation choice.** No silent
  divergence from §7.3.

**Stub deliverables (shipped in pre-flight, this commit
per the d822982 / a702af2 cadence):** `src/config/
profiles.ts` (TOML loader signatures + zod schema for
the §7.2 file shape; runtime body deferred to M21
implementation alongside the TOML-parser dep-add —
`smol-toml` is the leaning choice). `src/config/
credentials.ts` (mode-`0600` cache primitive
signatures + zod schema for the §7.4.1 file shape;
disk-discipline body deferred to M21 implementation,
mirrors `src/api/cache.ts writeJsonFile` verbatim per
§7.4.2 — the secure-file primitive R-candidate stays
below the 3-consumer threshold). `src/commands/auth/
login.ts` + `src/commands/auth/logout.ts` (verb
registration + argv shape + reads `--profile` from
the global-flag layer; stub bodies reject with
`internal_error` until M21 implementation lands the
real OAuth flow per §7.3.1 + the credentials-write
per §7.4). `src/api/oauth.ts` (PKCE-NOT-shipping —
empirical probe at this commit confirmed Monday's
`/oauth2/token` requires `client_secret`, so the
v0.3 design ships `client_secret`-only authentication
of the token-exchange call; PKCE is documented as a
v0.4+ amendment). `src/types/global-flags.ts`
(structurally widens `--profile` to accept any
non-empty name; flag/env-conflict check preserved;
the actual resolution-to-token step lands at M21
implementation in `cli/run.ts`'s config-load step).
Redaction extension widens
`DEFAULT_SENSITIVE_KEYS` to include `access_token`
per §7.4.3 (runtime value-scanning extension that
loads credentials values into the secret-bag lands at
M21 implementation alongside `auth login`'s real
body). `src/utils/errors.ts` ERROR_CODES widening for
`oauth_failed` (28 → 29 per §7.3.3) — including
`CODE_RETRYABLE_DEFAULT` + `CODE_TYPICAL_HTTP_STATUS`
table extensions + `exitCodeForError` switch (maps to
exit 1, treating `oauth_failed` as usage-shaped per
the M20 Decision 4.1/4.2 reasoning). Cli-design §6.5
row insertion for `oauth_failed` (alongside the
type-level widening + the per-`details.reason` table
in §7.3.3 + the per-code `details` schema in §6.5
that pins the discriminator + per-reason extras).
Cross-doc count bumps 28 → 29 across CLAUDE.md /
README.md / output-shapes.md / architecture.md.

**Empirical probe findings (2026-05-10, against
`auth.monday.com`)** — load-bearing for the design
amendments above:
- `/oauth2/token` requires `client_secret` (PKCE-only
  shape rejected with
  `{"error":"invalid_request","error_description":"Missing client_secret param"}`,
  status 400). PKCE is non-load-bearing for v0.3.
- `/oauth2/authorize` accepts arbitrary query params
  (encodes them all into a JWT-signed
  `oauth_payload_token` redirect target). PKCE
  parameters silently round-trip through the JWT but
  are not enforced at the token-exchange step.
- Rejection responses are RFC 6749 standard shape:
  `{"error", "error_description"}` body, status 400,
  `application/json; charset=utf-8`. Maps verbatim to
  `oauth_failed.details.{monday_code, monday_description}`.
- GraphQL `account.id` returns string-typed numeric
  (e.g., `"34900083"`), not a JS `number`.
- Monday's docs-pinned: redirect URI exact match
  (no wildcards documented); auth code TTL = 10 min;
  tokens "do not expire".

**Deliverables landing at M21 implementation
kickoff (next session):** OAuth app registration at
Monday's developer portal (one-time step; pins
`OAUTH_CLIENT_ID` + `OAUTH_CLIENT_SECRET` in source).
Runtime bodies for `bindOAuthListener` (HTTP listener
on `127.0.0.1:9876` with 5-min timer + AbortController
for SIGINT cleanup) + `exchangeCode`
(`fetch` against `/oauth2/token` + RFC 6749 error
mapping) + `readCredentials` / `writeCredentials` /
`setProfileCredentials` / `deleteProfileCredentials`
(atomic-replace mirroring `src/api/cache.ts`
`writeJsonFile` verbatim; read-time
`fs.fstat`-against-open-descriptor) +
`loadProfilesConfig` (TOML parse — `smol-toml` or
`@iarna/toml` decided alongside the dep-add) +
`selectProfile` (§7.2 source-order resolution) +
`resolveProfileToken` (§7.4.1 source-order:
credentials cache > `api_token_env` > `config_error`).
M21 implementation also lands the `cli/run.ts`
config-load wiring that reads `flags.profile`,
selects the profile via `selectProfile`, and
resolves the token via `resolveProfileToken` —
post-pre-flight, non-auth commands using
`--profile work` silently use the implicit-v1
(`MONDAY_API_TOKEN`) token; M21 implementation makes
the resolution real. **OPTIONAL** at M21
implementation: redaction-runtime extension that
folds loaded `access_token` values into the
secret-bag passed to `redact()` (the static
`access_token` key is already in the sensitive-keys
list at this pre-flight); the runtime fold lands
alongside the credentials-loading wiring in
`cli/run.ts`. Mock OAuth helper (`__test_oauth_helper`
env var per §7.3.4) lands as new test-seam
infrastructure alongside the listener body.

### M22 — Diagnostics cluster (`monday status` + `monday usage`)

**Self-test surface that probes M21's foundation.**
`monday status` runs DNS / TCP / TLS / auth probes,
reports cache writability, runs the redaction self-test
against a known-token fixture, and summarises env-var
pickup. `monday usage` queries Monday's `platform_api`
daily-budget surface (operation count remaining today —
empirical-probe-confirmed at M22 pre-flight as the
**operations** quota, NOT complexity points; see §22
R-watch-item below). M22 ships the additive-only
envelope shape so v0.4 can extend with per-minute
complexity headroom + concurrency-cap blocks without
breaking.

**Scope size:** M (no new mutations; one new read shape
per command; new probe-mocking infrastructure).

**Decisions closed at M22 pre-flight contract diff:**
- **Decision 7** (cli-design §11.5): probe by default;
  `--no-probe` opts out (skips the four network probes;
  local-only cache / redaction / env-var probes still
  run).
- **Decision 8** (cli-design §11.5.3 + §13 v0.3 entry):
  M22 pre-flight empirical probe (2026-05-10, API
  `2026-01`) confirmed the daily-budget surface is
  `platform_api.daily_limit { base, total }` +
  `platform_api.daily_analytics.by_day [{day, usage}]`
  — NOT `account.complexity` (which doesn't exist).
  Monday tracks **operations** per day (200/day on
  free tier), not complexity points. v0.3 ships
  `{daily_limit, usage_today, usage_remaining_today,
  last_updated}`; envelope is additive-only so v0.4
  can fold in per-minute complexity (lift from v0.1
  `account complexity`) + concurrency cap without re-
  pinning the contract.

**Pre-flight contract diff shipped:** `src/api/
probes.ts` (per-probe transport with mockable seams —
**API-layer stubs wrapped in `c8 ignore start/stop`
block-wraps** because the API stubs aren't reachable
from pre-flight tests), `src/api/usage.ts` (daily-
operations envelope shape + `USAGE_QUERY` GraphQL
document + `projectUsageOutput` pure-helper landed as
real impl; `fetchUsage` ships as stub under
`c8 ignore start/stop`), `src/commands/status.ts` +
`src/commands/usage.ts` (documentation-only stubs that
`Promise.reject(internal_error)` per the M21
pre-flight stub-action pattern — **command-action
stub bodies are NOT `c8 ignore`-wrapped** because
integration tests drive them via commander's argv
parse path and assert on the rejection envelope shape;
wrapping would suppress real test coverage of the
input-schema parse leg + the rejection assertion). Cross-doc surface:
cli-design §4.3 (status + usage entries reshaped +
`--no-probe` flag pinned), cli-design §11.5 (new
section pinning the probe matrix + per-probe
error-code mapping + the operations envelope), §13
v0.3 entries reshaped for both verbs.

**Implementation shipped:** `refactor(redact)` at
`84c6d2b` (narrow non-string scalar preservation to
boolean/number/null) + `feat(m22)` at `3a1b465`
(runtime DNS / TCP / TLS / auth / cache / redaction /
env-var probe bodies + `fetchUsage` body + action
bodies for `monday status` + `monday usage`). Full
post-mortem in §14 below.

**Empirical-probe matrix (per §22 R-watch-item; fired
second time at M22 pre-flight after M21).** Seven
disposable scripts under `scripts/probe/m22-*.ts`
(gitignored — reuse `_lib.ts` per §22 R-candidate at
third consumer):

  1. `m22-usage.ts` — confirmed `account.complexity`
     does NOT exist (Monday: `Cannot query field
     "complexity" on type "Account".`). Top-level
     `complexity { before after query reset_in_x_seconds }`
     surface re-confirmed (per-minute window;
     reset_in_x_seconds: 60).
  2. `m22-usage-extended.ts` — Query-root + Account
     introspection surfaced `platform_api` Query field
     + `DailyLimit` + `DailyAnalytics` types as
     candidates.
  3. `m22-usage-daily.ts` — deeper introspection of
     `DailyLimit` (`base: Int`, `total: Int`) +
     `PlatformApi` (`daily_limit`, `daily_analytics`)
     + cross-schema type search confirmed no parallel
     daily-quota surface exists elsewhere.
  4. `m22-usage-platform-api.ts` + `m22-usage-analytics.ts`
     + `m22-usage-by-day.ts` — pinned the full shape:
     `platform_api { daily_limit { base total }
     daily_analytics { last_updated by_day { day usage }
     by_app { app, usage, api_app_id } by_user { user,
     usage } } }`. v0.3 surfaces only `daily_limit` +
     `by_day` to minimise query complexity; by_app /
     by_user are v0.4 surface if agents need them.
  5. `m22-status.ts` — 401 envelope shape under
     `2026-01` confirmed: status `401`, content-type
     `application/json; charset=utf-8`, body
     `{"errors":[{"message":"Not authenticated",
     "extensions":{"code":"NOT_AUTHENTICATED"}}]}`.
     Same envelope for missing- + bad-`Authorization`.
     `Bearer <token>` prefix also accepted (precaution
     remains in `.claude/rules/security.md`).

### M23 — Cross-board `item search` + `board favorites`

**Paired per cli-design §13 — neither ships alone.**
Cross-board `item search` (omit `--board`) lets agents
"find my open tasks anywhere I have access" without
iterating boards by hand. `board favorites` returns the
current user's starred boards as a natural scoping lever
(`item search --favorites`). Shipping favorites in
isolation buys little agent value; pairing them ships the
full discovery surface.

**Scope size:** M–L (cross-board fan-out via streaming
helpers; wall-clock-latency cap design pass; per-board
walker composition).

**Decisions closed before kickoff** (cli-design §13
v0.3 entry pins the wall-clock-latency cap):
- **Per-call wall-clock-latency cap (Decision 5,
  `3a2f1db`).** ~~The pre-decision wording said
  "Monday's per-call complexity budget allows ~25 board
  scans comfortably"~~ — that hypothesis was
  superseded by the M23 pre-flight empirical probe
  (2026-05-11): complexity is NOT the cap constraint
  (~25-30 points per board against a ~999_950 per-call
  budget; complexity supports ~30,000+ boards per
  call). The REAL constraint is wall-clock latency
  (`boards(ids:[N]) { items_page(...) }` ~0.5-1.5s per
  call at small N; linear extrapolation puts N=25 at
  ~12-18s under the 30s `MONDAY_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS`
  default, N≥60 at the ceiling). Decision 5 retains
  the 25/100 cap numbers under the latency-envelope
  rationale.
- **Workspace scoping.** `--workspace <wid>` lets the
  agent narrow before fanning out. Shipped: both
  `--workspace` and `--favorites` as scoping levers;
  default to off; mutually exclusive with `--board`
  and with each other (parse-boundary `usage_error`).
- **Streaming.** Reuse `startNdjsonStream` (R52) +
  `walkPages.onItem` (M18 hook). Each board's walker is
  a child stream; the parent merges into one NDJSON
  output stream. **v0.3 cross-board search is
  single-call-only** — no resumable cross-board cursor
  (Codex P1-2 resolution); aggregate `--limit` short-
  circuit or per-board-cap exhaustion surfaces a
  `cross_board_truncated` warning with per-board state
  for agent introspection. v0.4 may add an opaque-
  token resumable cursor (envelope-additive per §6.1).

**Pre-flight contract diff shipped at `1fefdb1`**
(mirrors `c0efab5` / `bed75c6` / `d822982` / `a702af2` /
`5c07840` / `fbab6b0` cadence). Two new src/api modules
(`cross-board-search.ts` fan-out walker, `board-favorites.ts`
2-stage favorites resolver) + one new command (`monday board
favorites` at `src/commands/board/favorites.ts`) + extension
to `src/commands/item/search.ts` (adds `--workspace <wid>` /
`--favorites` / `--max-boards <n>` flags, `--board` relaxed
to optional, mutual-exclusion enforced via `.superRefine`,
cross-board action body stub-rejects). API-layer module
stubs (`crossBoardSearch`, `fetchBoardFavorites`) wrapped in
`c8 ignore start/stop` block-wraps per the post-M21
testing.md convention; command-action stubs NOT wrapped
(integration tests drive them through commander argv and
assert on the rejection envelope shape). Pure helpers ship
as REAL impls (`validateMaxBoards`,
`buildInaccessibleBoardsWarning`,
`buildColumnNotFoundOnBoardWarning`,
`buildCrossBoardTruncatedWarning`,
`filterFavoritesToBoards`, `joinFavoritesWithBoards`,
`buildStaleFavoritesWarning`) so Codex pre-flight review
can verify projection shapes inline against the empirical-
probe fixtures.

**No new ERROR_CODE** (29 stays). The hypothesised
`complexity_budget_exhausted` umbrella did NOT materialise —
the empirical-probe finding shifted the cap rationale from
complexity-budget to wall-clock latency (Decision 5 closure
`3a2f1db`), and the existing `usage_error` (above-cap parse)
+ `complexity_exceeded` (Monday-side runtime, unchanged)
together cover the surface. The cross-board walker's three
load-bearing warnings (`inaccessible_boards`,
`column_not_found_on_board`, `cross_board_truncated`) and
the favorites resolver's `board_favorites_stale` warning
are `warnings[]` shapes per §6.5, not new `error.code`
registry entries. The `cross_board_truncated` warning was
added at Codex round-1 P1-2 resolution to replace the
originally-pinned per-board cursor map on
`CrossBoardSearchResult` — v0.3 cross-board search is
single-call-only; pagination beyond the first per-board
page surfaces via `cross_board_truncated.details.per_board_state`
for agent introspection rather than a resumable cross-
board cursor.

The runtime body lift (`boards(ids:) { items_page
(query_params:) }` fan-out walker + per-board column
resolution pre-pass + 2-stage favorites Stage-1 filter +
Stage-2 hydrate + streaming-onItem composition) **shipped
at `1f09a25`** — per-board sequential fan-out (the empirical
finding pinned per-board distinct rule sets, which can't
fold into one document; N sequential calls preserve the
wall-clock-cap calibration), with the cross-board action
in `src/commands/item/search.ts` dispatching on the scoping
lever + running the per-board column-resolution pre-pass +
aggregating per-leg sources via `SourceAggregator`. See §15
M23 post-mortem for the close + contract drift findings.

### M24 — `item history <iid>`

**New §6 envelope shape.** Per-item activity log surfaces
**status changes, column edits, assignments, AND comments
interleaved chronologically** (per cli-design §13 v0.3
entry — the comments-interleaved requirement is part of
the contract surface). Two source streams merge in the
projector: Monday's `activity_logs` (status / column /
assignment events) + Monday's `updates` (comment thread).
Output event objects carry `created_at`, `actor_id`,
`kind`, `before` / `after`; the `kind` taxonomy includes
an `update_posted` (or similar) variant for comment
events alongside the activity-log kinds. Distinct from
existing item / update projections (item = current state;
update = comment thread; history = chronological merged
event log).

**Scope size:** M–L (one new noun-verb shape; new
two-source merge projection layer; per-source pagination
both consumed; per-event ordering by `created_at`).

**Decisions closed at M24 pre-flight contract diff
(bad98ba):**
- **Pagination shape — CLOSED.** Per-source page-
  numbered. `--activity-logs-page <n>` (1-indexed;
  Monday's `activity_logs(page:, limit:)`) +
  `--updates-page <n>` (1-indexed; independent
  denominator). Merging onto a single `--page <n>`
  flag would conflate two different denominators.
  `--limit <n>` is the per-source per-call slice
  (default 100; hard cap 10000 per Monday's
  documented ceiling). The two sources paginate
  independently; the NDJSON trailer carries
  per-source `last_page` for resumption.
- **Merge semantics — CLOSED.** Both sources drain to
  the `--since <iso>` / `--until <iso>` wall-clock
  cap. Monday's `activity_logs(from:, to:)` accepts
  ISO8601DateTime args server-side; the updates
  source filters client-side against
  `Update.created_at` since Monday's `updates`
  resolver doesn't expose a wall-clock filter as of
  API `2026-01`. The merge projector orders the
  unified stream by `created_at` ascending with `id`
  lexicographic tie-break for deterministic output.
- **`before` / `after` shape — CLOSED.** Per
  Decision 2: zod discriminated union (`kind`
  discriminator mapped from wire `event` slot)
  over the observed event taxonomy + 2 synthesized
  comment-event variants + 1 `unknown` fallback.
  Comment-event `before` is always `null` (append-
  only events); `after` carries
  `{body, text_body, reply_count}` for
  `update_posted` and `{body, text_body}` for
  `update_replied`. Per-`column_type` typed
  `before` / `after` projection for
  `update_column_value` lands case-by-case at M24
  implementation; pre-flight pins each variant's
  `before` / `after` as `z.unknown()` slots carrying
  the raw parsed JSON payload. Unknown kinds surface
  under the `kind: 'unknown'` variant carrying
  `before: null` + `after: <raw parsed data>`
  (uniform shape with the synthesized comment-event
  variants — `after` IS the raw payload; no separate
  `data` slot) + raw wire `event` + `entity` slots
  for agent routing, alongside an `unknown_event_kind`
  warning (§6.1 warnings[]; NOT an `error.code`
  registry entry — registry stays at 29).
- **Streaming — CLOSED.** Reuse `startNdjsonStream`
  (R52) when `--stream` is on. Merge is NOT
  incremental (the entire `--since`-bounded slice
  must be resident to order it); the NDJSON path
  emits the merged array post-merge with the
  trailer carrying per-source pagination state
  (`activity_logs.last_page` + `updates.last_page`)
  + complexity + source per cli-design §6.3.

**Pre-flight contract diff shipped at bad98ba:**
`src/api/item-history-projection.ts` (schemas + GraphQL
document constants + pure helpers `mergeByCreatedAt` +
`buildUnknownEventKindWarning` as REAL impls; runtime
fetchers `fetchItemHistory` + projectors
`projectActivityLogRow` / `projectUpdateRow` /
`projectReplyRow` as stubs under `c8 ignore start/stop`
block-wraps), `src/commands/item/history.ts` (argv
schema real + stub action body), `cli-design.md` §13
v0.3 entry extended with the 6 load-bearing probe
findings + 4 decision closures + eventual-consistency
caveat, `cli-design.md` §4.3 row updated with the
full flag surface, `docs/output-shapes.md` new top-
level entry under item (reads). Cross-doc bumps:
command count 77 → 78 (`monday item history` joined).
**ERROR_CODES count stays at 29** — Decision 2's
`unknown_event_kind` is a §6.1 `warnings[]` shape,
not a registry entry.

**Implementation shipped at d058172** + Codex review
fixes at `5f10cda` (round 1: 1 P1 + 2 P2) + `a024961`
(round 2: 1 P2 + 1 P3). Replaces the four `c8 ignore

### M25 — `item update --continue-on-error` (partial-success bulk)

**New §6.4 sub-section.** Today's bulk `item update
--where` fails fast on the first per-item error (matched
items before the failure surface in `details.applied_to`
per §6.5). The flag attempts every matched item
regardless and emits a partial-success envelope with per-
item `{ok, error?}` records — that's a §6.4 sub-section,
not just a flag. Cli-design §13 v0.3 entry.

**Scope size:** S–M (one new flag; one new envelope
sub-section; reuses v0.2's `data.results: [...]` per-
target shape from §1 above).

**Decisions to close before kickoff:**
- **`--continue-on-error` vs `--no-fail-fast`.** Naming
  question. Recommended: `--continue-on-error`
  (positive form; the v0.2 default fails fast).
- **Per-item error envelope.** `{ ok: false, item_id, error:
  { code, message } }` per entry. Inherits v0.2's shared
  `dispatchSequential` per-target dispatch helper.
- **Top-level `ok` field.** All-failed-but-each-attempted
  is still `ok: true` per §1's universal partial-success
  rule. The agent reads `data.results` to determine per-
  item outcomes.

**Stub deliverables (filled at M25 kickoff):**
`src/commands/item/update.ts` extension (new flag +
partial-success path), `src/api/partial-success-bulk.ts`
(thin wrapper around the existing fail-fast bulk path).

**Pre-flight contract diff shipped at `d5839a9`.** Closes
the M25 pre-flight gate per §9 preconditions. Mirrors the
`bad98ba` (M24) / `1fefdb1` (M23) / `fbab6b0` (M22) /
`5c07840` (M21) cadence — pin the contract surface ahead
of any feat commit so M25 implementation lands into a
Codex-reviewed shape rather than churning on contract
drift mid-implementation. **Decision 6 closed inline at
`d5839a9`** — positive form `--continue-on-error` (the
`--no-fail-fast` negative-form alternative rejected;
rationale at §8 Decision 6 closure). One new
`src/api/partial-success-bulk.ts` module (thin wrapper
around `dispatchSequential` from
`src/api/partial-success-mutation.ts`) + new flag on the
existing `monday item update --where` row of cli-design
§4.3 + new §6.4 "Bulk per-item partial-success" sub-section
+ new entry in output-shapes.md. ERROR_CODES stays at 29
(per-item failures route through existing codes via the
M14 `dispatchSequential` per-target error decoration);
command count stays at 78 (flag on existing command, not
new). The runtime body of `runPartialSuccessBulkUpdate`
ships as `Promise.reject(internal_error)` stub under
`c8 ignore start/stop` block-wraps (testing.md preferred
form per M20 + M21 + M24 stub precedent); pure helpers
(`foldPartialSuccessBulkResult` +
`buildPartialSuccessBulkSummary`) + schemas
(`partialSuccessBulkUpdateResultSchema` +
`partialSuccessBulkUpdateDataSchema`) + the
`PARTIAL_SUCCESS_BULK_DISPATCH_SOURCE` constant ship as
REAL implementations so Codex pre-flight review can
verify the projection shape against cli-design §6.4
inline. The action body's partial-success routing branch
in `src/commands/item/update.ts:runBulk` is similarly
`c8 ignore`-wrapped; M25 implementation drops the wrap +
swaps the stub for the runtime body. Codex pre-flight
review ran 2 rounds:
- **Round 1** (against `d5839a9`): 1 P1 + 1 P2 + 3 P3.
  P1-1 = the wrapper signature couldn't preserve the

  stale-cache remap (per cli-design §6.5 stable-code rule);

### M26 — `dev` namespace (workflow shortcuts)

**Pre-flight contract diff shipped at `1620220`** — new
`src/api/dev-conventions.ts` module (real schemas + pure
helpers `matchBoardByConvention` / `groupCandidatesByDevNoun` /
`buildDiscoverMappingFromMatches`; runtime stubs `discoverDevBoards`
/ `runDevDoctor` / `loadDevMapping` / `saveDevMapping` under
`c8 ignore start/stop` block-wraps per the
M21 oauth-stub / M24 history-stub / M25 partial-success-bulk
precedent) + 13 new command stubs across
`src/commands/dev/{discover,configure,doctor}.ts` +
`src/commands/dev/sprint/{current,list,items}.ts` +
`src/commands/dev/epic/{list,items}.ts` +
`src/commands/dev/release/list.ts` +
`src/commands/dev/task/{list,start,done,block}.ts` (each carrying
a real argv `inputSchema` + `outputSchema` + a
`c8`-wrapped action body that rejects with `internal_error.details
.hint` pointing at M26 IMPL). Cross-doc count bumps:
`command_count` **78 → 91** for the 13 new commands;
`ERROR_CODES` count holds at **29** (Decision 1 closure routes
through existing `dev_not_configured` + `dev_board_misconfigured`
codes already pre-registered in `src/utils/errors.ts` per cli-design
§5.9 — both joined the registry ahead of M5b for forward-compat).
Decisions 1 (discover heuristic), 2 (doctor diagnostics check-name
vocabulary), and 3 (three-level naming reaffirmation per §5.2
carve-out 1) closed inline at the pre-flight contract diff
(see §3 M26 decisions list below). Codex pre-flight review
across 2 rounds: round 1 surfaced 2 P1 + 5 P2 + 1 P3 (fixed
inline at `4433297`); round 2 surfaced 0 P1 + 4 P2 + 1 P3
(fixed inline at `f605260`). Round-2 P3 + round-1 P3-1 (SHA
placeholder backfill) closed at this close-docs sweep.
R-NEW-25 "findings up front" directive validated for the 8th
+ 9th time (M21 × 2 + M22 × 3 + M23 × 2 + M24 × 4 + M25 × 3
+ M26 × 2); R-NEW-17 W1 redactor-pattern audit returned
"nothing flagged" across both M26 rounds.

**Cli-design §5.2 carve-out 1 — workflow namespaces.** `dev sprint` /
`dev epic` / `dev release` / `dev task` are convention-
driven workflow shortcuts on top of the standard board /
item CRUD. Plus `dev discover` / `dev configure` /
`dev doctor` for setup + diagnostics. Cli-design §2.7 +
§5.2 + §11.3 pin Monday Dev as **convention, not API** —
v0.3's `dev` namespace doesn't introduce new Monday
mutations; it translates workflow vocabulary to standard
operations.

**Scope size:** L (broad surface — cli-design §4.3 lists
~13 commands across the namespace: `dev discover` /
`dev configure` / `dev doctor` + `dev sprint
current/list/items` + `dev epic list/items` +
`dev release list` + `dev task list/start/done/block`).
**This exceeds the §3 sequencing intro's 3–10 commit
cluster invariant** (v0.2-plan §3, line 85). M26 is
flagged as an **intentional milestone-size exception**:
the surface is convention-driven (Monday Dev is
convention, not API per cli-design §2.7), so each verb
is a thin wrapper over CRUD plumbing already shipped.
Pre-M26 kickoff requires enumerating the exact commit
slices (mirror v0.2-plan §3 M13's eight-commit cluster
shape — feat per noun-verb pair, then implementation
review fix-ups) to prove the surface fits.
Alternatively, split M26 into M26a (`dev sprint/epic/
release`) + M26b (`dev task` + diagnostics) at the
M25 → M26 transition if pre-flight surfaces a clean
cleavage point.

**Decisions closed at M26 pre-flight (`1620220`):**
- **Decision 1: discovery shape** (`dev discover`). **Closed.**
  Heuristic: scan user's accessible boards for ones whose names
  match Monday Dev's stock English board names (`Tasks` /
  `Sprints` / `Epics` / `Releases` / `Bugs`) via the
  `matchBoardByConvention` pure helper (case-insensitive Unicode
  NFC; exact match preferred over substring). Per-noun matches
  with exactly one candidate auto-populate the `DevMapping`;
  zero-match or ambiguous (>1) nouns surface on the output's
  `matches[]` array for agent-side review. `--apply` writes the
  mapping into the active profile's `[profiles.<name>.dev]` block
  via `saveDevMapping`. Localised-workspace alias support
  (`--name-aliases`) deferred to v0.4+ per cli-design §5.9's
  English-by-design baseline. The DEV_NOUN_PATTERNS list +
  matcher live in `src/api/dev-conventions.ts:DEV_NOUN_PATTERNS`
  + `matchBoardByConvention` (real implementations at pre-flight
  so the Codex review can verify the heuristic shape inline).
- **Decision 2: doctor diagnostics check-name vocabulary**
  (`dev doctor`). **Closed.** The pinned `DEV_DOCTOR_CHECK_NAMES`
  list (10 entries post-round-1 + round-2 Codex fix-ups):
  `tasks_board_exists`, `tasks_status_column_present`,
  `tasks_status_labels_canonical`, `sprints_board_exists`,
  `sprints_date_columns_present`, `epics_board_exists`,
  `releases_board_exists`, `bugs_board_exists`,
  `tasks_to_sprints_relation`, `tasks_to_epics_relation`. Per-
  check status = `ok | warn | fail` with an open-ended `details`
  slot (per-check additive). Adding a check is non-breaking;
  removing or renaming is a major bump. Mirrors cli-design §11.3
  "runs `board doctor` against each configured dev board plus
  checks the cross-board `board_relation` wiring". Per-check
  `details` discriminated-union pinning deferred to M26 IMPL
  (run order locks at pre-flight; per-check detail shape is per-
  check additive). **Round-1 Codex fix-ups:** P1-1 renamed
  `sprints_state_column_present` → `sprints_date_columns_present`
  to match the sprint verbs' date-range-derived semantics
  (`dev sprint list --state` filter); P2-2 added
  `bugs_board_exists` so the `bugs_board` mapping slot is
  diagnosed like every other dev-noun slot. **Round-2 Codex
  fix-up:** P2-3 replaced `epics_to_releases_relation` with
  `tasks_to_epics_relation` — the round-1 list had a relation
  check for an epic↔release wiring no M26 verb consumes (there's
  no `dev release items` verb at v0.3), while the actually-
  consumed epic↔task relation (`dev epic items <eid>` walks
  tasks linked to a given epic) was missing.
- **Decision 3: three-level naming reaffirmation.** **Closed.**
  Every `dev` verb is `monday dev <noun> <verb>` per
  cli-design §5.2 carve-out 1 (Decision 4 closure pre-M20 at
  `1e81b2f` already in force). The 13-verb surface ratifies
  the carve-out: 3 setup verbs at `dev <verb>` shape +
  10 workflow verbs at `dev <noun> <verb>` shape (sprint /
  epic / release / task × their verbs). No `dev --sprint
  current` or `dev sprint current --start` shape opened; the
  carve-out's three-level depth absorbs the namespace.

**M26 vs M26a + M26b split.** Pre-flight contract diff ships
as ONE commit covering both halves; the IMPL session decides
whether to land as M26a (3 setup verbs: discover / configure /
doctor) + M26b (10 workflow verbs) at IMPL kickoff per the
natural setup-vs-workflow cleavage. Both halves benefit from
contract pinning together at pre-flight — the doctor + configure
verbs need to know what board mappings exist to validate; the
workflow verbs need to know what config slots they read from.

**Stub deliverables (filled at M26 kickoff):** `src/api/
dev-conventions.ts`, `src/commands/dev/sprint/...`,
`src/commands/dev/epic/...`, `src/commands/dev/release/
...`, `src/commands/dev/task/...`, `src/commands/dev/
discover.ts`, `src/commands/dev/configure.ts`,
`src/commands/dev/doctor.ts`.

**M26a IMPL shipped at `19755e3`** (runtime bodies for the 3
setup verbs + the 4 `dev-conventions.ts` runtime fetchers) +
Codex impl review fix-ups across 3 rounds: round 1 (1 P1 + 1
P2 + 1 P3) at `2be9021`; round 2 (0 P1 + 2 P2 + 1 P3) at
`c70deb3`; round 3 (0 P1 + 0 P2 + 1 P3) at `2a3c06c`. New
`src/commands/dev/_shared.ts` lifted out the per-verb
profile-resolution preamble (3 consumers at M26a, with 10
more landing at M26b). Empirical-probe findings ratified
the heuristic (`scripts/probe/m26-{dev-discover,board-kind,
board-type}.ts` against a real Monday Dev workspace —
DEV_NOUN_PATTERNS unchanged + `Board.type === 'board'`
filter required for `sub_items_board` virtual entries +
`state: all` walker per the handoff guidance). Decision 2's
deferred per-check `details` discriminated-union pinning
closed at IMPL via a structural `z.discriminatedUnion('status',
[ok, warn, fail])` refactor (round-2 P2-1) + the
`DEV_DOCTOR_REASONS` pinned vocabulary (11 reason values) +
`failResult`'s required-reason signature narrowing. JSON
Schema export (`z.toJSONSchema`) now surfaces the reason
enum so `monday schema dev.doctor` agents can introspect
the closed vocabulary.

**M26b IMPL shipped at `10cd1c5`** (runtime bodies for the 10
workflow verbs: `dev sprint current/list/items` +
`dev epic list/items` + `dev release list` +
`dev task list/start/done/block`) + Codex impl review fix-ups
across 3 rounds: round 1 (0 P1 + 3 P2 + 1 P3) at `34a5bc1`;
round 2 (1 P1 + 1 P2 + 0 P3) at `078dae3`; round 3 (0 P1 + 0
P2 + 1 P3) at `8ea66c4`. **R-NEW-35 `requireDevBoard` slot-
check helper lifted** into `_shared.ts` at IMPL kickoff (10-
consumer trigger — one per workflow verb). **R-NEW-36 follow-
up helper lifts in `dev-conventions.ts`** (7 new exports):
`walkDevBoardItems` (7 consumers across read verbs),
`hydrateDevBoardColumns` (4 consumers across mutation +
relation-walker verbs), `findRelationColumnIdToBoard` (3
consumers across sprint items / epic items / task list),
`extractLinkedItemIds` (handles both `linkedPulseIds` legacy +
`item_ids` newer 2026-01 shapes), `resolveStatusColumn` +

GraphQL operation-name mismatch (doc named `DevCreateUpdate`
while wire operationName was per-verb — fixed via dynamic

stranded `walkDevBoardItems`'s docstring above the wrong
helper; pure cleanup). M26b converged in 3 Codex rounds —

expected-2-3-rounds target from the M26b handoff.

### M27 — `notification send` + `webhook list/create/delete`

**Outbound writes — bundled because both are write-only,
low surface.** `notification send` uses Monday's
`create_notification` mutation (per-user-id targeting).
`webhook list/create/delete` uses `webhooks` query +
`create_webhook` / `delete_webhook` mutations (board
webhooks; CLI never *receives* — see §1 permanent non-
goals). **Webhooks are live-only for v0.3** — outside
cli-design §8's cache scope; any caching extension is
a v0.3.x / v0.4 contract-extension PR.

**Scope size:** M (two new nouns, four new verbs total).

**Decisions to close before kickoff:**
- **Notification recipients.** **Single-recipient** per
  cli-design §4.3 (`--user <uid>` singular). Multi-
  recipient is a contract extension (cli-design §4.3 +
  §6 partial-success shape) and is OUT of scope for
  M27; agents who need fan-out call `notification send`
  N times. If multi-recipient becomes load-bearing, it
  ships as a v0.3.x or v0.4 contract-extension PR.
- **Webhook event types.** Monday's documented event
  taxonomy. Recommended: validate against the documented
  list at parse boundary (zod enum); surface `usage_
  error` for unknown events with `hint: 'see Monday
  webhook event docs at ...'`.
- **Cache invalidation.** **Webhook reads are live-only
  for v0.3** — cli-design §8 defines cache entries for
  `boards/<id>.json`, `users/index.json`, and
  `schema/version.json` only; webhooks are not in the
  cache scope and `invalidateBoard()` clears board
  metadata, not webhook listings. Adding webhooks to the
  §8 cache scope would be a contract extension. M27
  ships webhook list/create/delete with `meta.source:
  "live"` always; if cache support proves valuable
  later, it lands as a v0.3.x or v0.4 §8 extension PR.

**Stub deliverables (filled at M27 kickoff):** `src/api/
notifications.ts`, `src/api/webhooks.ts`, `src/commands/
notification/send.ts`, `src/commands/webhook/list.ts`,
`src/commands/webhook/create.ts`, `src/commands/webhook/
delete.ts`.

— slipped from v0.2; per v0.2-plan §26 looking-ahead —
Monday allows `create_subitem` with a parent that is

**Scope size:** M (subitem hierarchy validation + release
prep; mirrors M18's "no new contract surface" pattern,
but with one bona-fide new feature in subitems).

**Decisions to close before kickoff:**
- **Hierarchy depth limit.** Monday's documented limit
