### Refactor backlog candidates surfaced

- **R-NEW-26 — defensive abort-listener race guard in
  async test promises.** Three tests in
  `tests/unit/cli/run.test.ts` flaked under full-coverage
  parallelism because the test's
  `setTimeout(abort, 10ms)` could fire BEFORE the action's
  Promise constructor registered its `addEventListener
  ('abort', ...)`. Node's AbortSignal does NOT replay
  'abort' for listeners attached after the event
  dispatched. Fix landed at `4c83860`: sync
  `if (signal.aborted) reject(...)` check before
  listener registration in all three sites. **LOW priority
  watch-item;** fires at next async-abort-handling test
  site OR if the pattern repeats enough to warrant a
  `buildAbortablePromise(signal, onAbort)` helper in
  `tests/_helpers/`. Production-side analogue: the
  race-window guards documented at
  `.claude/rules/testing.md`'s `c8 ignore start/stop`
  block-wrap section (`src/commands/auth/login.ts:
  fetchAccountId`'s OAuth-listener guards). Tracked in
  §22 R-NEW-26 below.
- **R-NEW-20 status update.** The 3-consumer trigger
  fired at M24 impl (the new
  `tests/unit/api/item-history-projection.test.ts:553`
  `buildClientStub` factory joins board-favorites +
  cross-board-search as the 3rd consumer of the
  `MondayClient` seam-injection pattern). Lift deferred
  with rationale — the three sites share the seam pattern
  (mock `client.raw` only) but each carries different
  routing logic (favorites by `operationName`, cross-
  board by `boardId` variable, item-history by
  `operationName`). A parametrised factory wouldn't
  meaningfully reduce duplication. Re-evaluate at 4th
  consumer (M27 webhooks candidate). Tracked in §22.
- **R-NEW-23 status holds.** `mergeByCreatedAt` kept
  inline in `src/api/item-history-projection.ts` at impl;
  still single-consumer. Watch-item fires at 2nd consumer
  (M27 webhooks if account-scoped + board-scoped surfaces
  need a merged stream).

### Lessons for the next milestone (M25)

1. **R-NEW-25 + R-NEW-17 are template-stable.** Both
   produced clean output across four independent Codex
   rounds. Fold both into
   `.claude/templates/codex-pre-flight-review.md` at M25
   pre-flight kickoff so future prompts inherit
   automatically. Drafting effort: ~10 minutes (R-NEW-25
   header + R-NEW-17 W-audit-point entry).

2. **Empirical-probe findings inform defensive helpers
   late in implementation.** Codex round-2 P2-1 surfaced
   that the M24-prep probe report explicitly captured
   `pulse_id:"<number>"` (unquoted wire), but the
   implementation reached for `readNullableString`
   without cross-checking the wire shape against the
   probe report. **Lesson for M25+:** when a probe report
   pins a load-bearing field shape (numeric vs string
   wire encoding, nullable vs non-null, etc.), surface
   the finding as an audit point in the impl-review
   prompt so Codex can verify the implementation matches
   the wire. M25's bulk-`item update --continue-on-error`
   surface is mostly aggregation logic with no novel
   wire shapes — but the discipline carries to M27
   webhooks where probe findings will be load-bearing.

3. **NaN guards on `Date.parse` are not optional.**
   Round-2 P3-1 surfaced the `NaN`-as-in-range silent
   pass-through pattern. **Lesson:** any wall-clock /
   epoch / numeric filter that takes parsed inputs from
   the wire OR from CLI arguments MUST explicitly check
   for `NaN` / `Infinity` / `-Infinity` / `0` /
   `out-of-range` shapes — `<`/`>` comparisons against
   these silently return false for the in-range branch.
   M27 webhooks may have similar numeric-input patterns
   if Monday ships epoch-shaped event payloads.

4. **Branches-margin RECOVERY came from refactoring,
   not test additions.** The `baseFetchInputs` lift in
   the action body + the dead-branch removal in the
   warnings sort gave back more margin than the +99
   test additions consumed. Pattern: when an
   implementation introduces a streaming / non-streaming
   fork, lift the shared input construction OUT of the
   fork — both paths share branch denominator. Same
   discipline applies to ANY two-path branch in a
   command action.

5. **The flaky-test fix (R-NEW-26) is a test-discipline
   issue surfaced by the higher per-test-file count.**
   With 124 test files now (vs 121 pre-M22), full-
   coverage runs exercise the test framework's
   parallelism harder, surfacing flakes that were
   latent at lower file counts. Watch for more
   parallelism-sensitive flakes at M27 + M28 when the
   file count grows further. Prophylactic application
   of the sync-aborted-check pattern to any new
   async-abort-handling tests is cheap insurance.

## 17. M25 post-mortem — `item update --continue-on-error` (partial-success bulk)

**Shipped:** pre-flight `d5839a9` (contract diff + Codex
pre-flight round-1 fixes `832a169` + round-2 fixes
`67df582`) + R-class cleanup bundle `85b93e8` (R-NEW-17 +
R-NEW-25 + R-NEW-27 lifts ahead of M25 pre-flight kickoff) +
post-close docs sweep `1cd1ee2` + R-NEW-28 watch-item log
`d8b6b75` + `executeItemMutation` lift `78889df` (R-NEW-29
3-consumer trigger ahead of M25 IMPL feat) + implementation
feat `fe15181` + Codex impl review round-1 fixes `c146106`.

### Codex round summary

**Pre-flight contract diff** (2 rounds):
- Round 1: 1 P1 + 1 P2 + 3 P3 (applied at `832a169`).
  P1-1 = `foldAndRemap` context-thread requirement (the
  wrapper signature couldn't preserve the v0.1 fail-fast
  `validation_failed` → `column_archived` stale-cache
  remap per cli-design §6.5; fix added 5 context fields to
  `RunPartialSuccessBulkUpdateInputs`).
- Round 2: 1 P2 + 3 P3 (applied at `67df582`). P2-1 =
  empty-match envelope shape preservation — the new
  partial-success shape leaked into the empty-match case;
  fix restored the v0.1 fail-fast empty-match shape
  unchanged regardless of `--continue-on-error`.

Both pre-flight findings (P1-1 + round-2 P2-1) became
R-NEW-28's first two empirical audit axes (per-target error
code semantic equivalence + empty-input envelope shape
preservation); R-NEW-28 logged the full 6-axis class at
`d8b6b75`.

**Implementation review** (1 round, no round 2 — findings
were doc-only):
- Round 1: 0 P1 + 1 P2 + 1 P3 (applied at `c146106`).
  - P2-1 (W3): `docs/cli-design.md §6.4` overstated that
    per-item dispatch failures NEVER bubble to top-level —
    `dispatchSequential`'s `internal_error` re-throw
    escape hatch (M14 round-2 F1 / round-3 F1 precedent at
    `src/api/partial-success-mutation.ts:82`) and the
    non-`MondayCliError` programmer-bug re-throw path
    both DO bubble whole-call. Fix carved out the two
    whole-call escape hatches while preserving the
    recoverable-per-item-`MondayCliError`-lands-per-record
    rule for stable codes (`column_archived` /
    `validation_failed` / etc.).
  - P3-1 (W7): `src/api/partial-success-bulk.ts` module
    header retained stale pre-flight prose claiming "Stub
    bodies under c8 ignore start/stop block-wraps" and
    "The stub rejects ...". Fix replaced the pre-flight-era
    paragraph with an implementation-era note pointing at
    `fe15181` + the `78889df` `executeItemMutation` lift.

W1: nothing flagged. W2.1-W2.6 (R-NEW-28 six axes): runtime
path matches the intended behavioral-equivalence axes.
W3/W4: re-throw paths preserved. W5 (NaN-guard): nothing
flagged (no `Date.parse` / `Number(...)` sites in the
runtime body). W6: no unstable new codes. W8
(`executeItemMutation` lift correctness): clean. W9 (schema
parse-back roundtrip): clean. W10 (side-map fold
correctness): clean.

Across both pre-flight rounds AND the impl review, the
R-NEW-25 "findings up front" directive produced clean
numbered findings with no truncation. R-NEW-17 W1 redactor-
pattern audit returned "nothing flagged" across all three
rounds. Both techniques continue template-stable.

### Contract drift findings

**None at M25 IMPL close that weren't already caught
pre-feat.** Both impl-review findings (P2-1 W3 cli-design
overstrong language; P3-1 W7 stale stub prose) were
documentation drift between the pre-flight contract surface
and the impl-era state, not runtime contract drift. The
pre-flight rounds 1+2 caught the two substantive contract
issues (P1-1 foldAndRemap context-thread + round-2 P2-1
empty-match shape) before any runtime code was written —
exactly the discipline the pre-flight contract diff
discipline at CLAUDE.md is designed to surface.

### Exit ratchets

- **Test count:** 2969 → 3011 (+42 net; 1 skipped
  unchanged). +32 new unit tests in
  `tests/unit/api/partial-success-bulk.test.ts` covering
  the two pure helpers' invariants + the runtime body
  driven via a sequence-aware MondayClient stub. +10 new
  integration tests extending
  `tests/integration/commands/item-update-bulk.test.ts`
  with the full `--continue-on-error` matrix (single-item
  rejection, gate orthogonality, empty-match shape
  preservation, dry-run shape preservation, all-success /
  all-fail / mixed, stale-cache archived column remap,
  `internal_error` whole-call, resolver-warning fold).
- **Coverage:** 99.11 / 96.15 / 99.33 / 99.30 →
  99.09 / 96.03 / 99.24 / 99.28. Branches margin
  0.70pp → 0.58pp at impl close (small dip; new runtime-
  body branches contribute the delta but stay well above
  the 95.45 floor with 0.58pp surplus).
- **Floor:** 95 / 95.45 / 95 / 95 (unchanged; never
  lowered).
- **ERROR_CODES count:** 29 (unchanged — per-item failures
  route through existing codes via `dispatchSequential`'s
  per-target error decoration + `foldAndRemap` per-item
  remap; Decision 6 closure pre-empted any registry
  widening).
- **Command count:** 78 (unchanged — `--continue-on-error`
  is a flag on the existing `monday item update --where`
  verb, not a new command).
- **No new snapshot entry.** Following the M23 + M24
  precedent: the integration test file's inline cassettes
  carry the byte-shape coverage; no per-flag envelope
  snapshot needed. `schema --json` snapshot's
  `command_count: 78` already reflected at pre-flight.
- **v8 coverage glitch on `partial-success-bulk.ts`.** The
  v8 coverage provider reports the new module with
  `FNF:0 LF:0 BRF:0` in `coverage/lcov.info` — i.e. no
  instrumentation found — despite 32 unit tests exercising
  every export. The HTML report shows `0/0` for all four
  axes. Cache clears (`.vite` + `coverage/` + `dist/`)
  didn't recover instrumentation; the file's shape (heavy
  JSDoc preamble + arrow-function exports) mirrors
  `partial-success-mutation.ts` (which v8 instruments
  correctly), so the divergence is anomalous. The floor
  is held (95.45 branches threshold passes) because
  `0/0` contributes neither to numerator nor denominator.
  Logged as M25 close residual; investigate at M26
  kickoff if it recurs on the new module surface.

### Refactor backlog candidates surfaced

- **R-NEW-29 — `executeItemMutation` lift (R7/R8 3-consumer
  trigger).** Surfaced at M25 IMPL kickoff as the
  pre-feat refactor. Three sites use the helper: the
  single-item action body's mutation dispatch at
  `src/commands/item/update.ts:executeMutation` call site,
  the fail-fast bulk loop's per-item dispatch, and the M25
  partial-success bulk wrapper at
  `src/api/partial-success-bulk.ts:runPartialSuccessBulkUpdate`'s
  per-item dispatch callback. The original helper was an
  80-line local function in `update.ts` flanked by ~50
  lines of GraphQL mutation strings; M25 IMPL's third
  consumer would either need to re-import across the
  `src/commands` → `src/api` boundary (a cycle since
  `update.ts` already imports `partial-success-bulk.ts`)
  or duplicate the GraphQL strings + projectMutationItem
  wrapper a second time. **Shipped: `78889df`** (renamed
  `executeMutation` → `executeItemMutation` for
  namespace clarity; the 3 GraphQL mutation strings + 3
  response interfaces + local `projectMutationItem`
  wrapper all moved with the helper; both update.ts call
  sites threaded through the rename). 1018 item-update
  integration tests pass unchanged across the lift; no
  test additions needed (the lift is purely structural).

- **R-NEW-20 — 4th-consumer trigger fired with diverging
  routing logic; lift deferred.** The M25 unit test file
  `tests/unit/api/partial-success-bulk.test.ts` introduces
  the 4th consumer of the seam-injected `MondayClient`
  stub pattern (after M23 favorites, M23 cross-board
  search, M24 history projection). The new consumer's
  routing logic diverges from the existing three: M23-
  favorites + M24-history route by `operationName` only;
  M23-cross-board routes by `boardId` variable; M25 routes
  by `operationName` + per-call sequence (the same op-name
  fires N times across the dispatch loop, so the stub
  needs a per-op-name FIFO queue rather than the simpler
  single-response shape). Lift to a shared
  `tests/_helpers/build-monday-client-stub.ts` deferred at
  the 4th consumer with the rationale that a parametrised
  shared factory has to subsume three routing strategies
  (op-name-only, variable-keyed, sequence-aware) and would
  carry more surface than the per-test-file inline copies
  it replaces. **Status: 4-consumer trigger fired, lift
  deferred with rationale.** Re-evaluate at 5th consumer
  (M27 webhook bulk-fan-out candidate); if the 5th uses
  the sequence-aware shape as M25 does, lift the sequence-
  aware variant to a shared helper and keep the op-name-
  only / variable-keyed shapes inline at their per-test-
  file sites.

- **R-NEW-28 watch-item — behavioral-equivalence audit
  axes.** Already logged at `d8b6b75` post-close. M25 IMPL
  exercised all six axes (W2.1-W2.6 in the impl review
  prompt's section 5). All six returned clean at impl
  review round 1, validating that pre-flight rounds 1+2
  had already pinned the contract; the implementation
  faithfully shipped the pinned shape. The watch-item is
  ready for application at v0.4 `--concurrency` if that
  milestone extends bulk verbs from sequential to
  parallel dispatch (the obvious next opt-in-flag-adds-
  parallel-path candidate).

### Lessons for M26

1. **Pre-flight contract diff discipline continues to
   prevent post-feat contract drift.** All four
   substantive M25 findings (pre-flight rounds 1+2 P1 +
   P2 + 3 P3 each round) surfaced BEFORE any runtime code
   shipped. The impl review round 1 found only
   documentation drift between the pre-flight surface and
   the impl-era state — not runtime drift. Continue the
   contract-diff-then-feat cadence; the R-NEW-28 6-axis
   audit is now the explicit script for opt-in-flag-
   extending milestones.

2. **R-class refactors ahead of feat commits keep the
   feat diff focused.** `78889df` (`executeItemMutation`
   lift) shipped ahead of `fe15181` (M25 IMPL feat) so
   the feat commit's diff stayed scoped to the runtime
   body + tests. The impl review's W8 audit-point
   verified the lift was behaviour-preserving in a few
   minutes — much cheaper than auditing a combined
   refactor-plus-feat diff. Continue the
   R-class-then-feat cadence (mirrors `85b93e8` ahead of
   `d5839a9`).

3. **Codex impl reviews can converge in 1 round when
   pre-flight rounds were thorough.** M22 impl ran 1
   round; M23 impl ran 1 round; M24 impl ran 2 rounds
   (round-1 P1 was an out-of-band `--stream` bug, not a
   contract drift); M25 impl ran 1 round. Pattern: when
   pre-flight rounds catch the load-bearing P1/P2
   contract issues, the impl review converges fast. M26
   should aim for the same — front-load the pre-flight
   contract diff audit, then trust the impl review to
   verify the implementation matches the pinned shape.

4. **v8 instrumentation oddities are not coverage-floor
   regressions.** The `partial-success-bulk.ts` 0/0
   glitch is annoying for per-file branches reporting but
   doesn't lower the project-wide branches percentage
   (the file contributes neither to numerator nor
   denominator). Future surfaces with the same shape
   (heavy JSDoc preamble + arrow-function exports) may
   trip the same glitch; document the workaround if it
   recurs at M26.

5. **Universal partial-success rule is now well-tested at
   four sites.** M13 `update clear-all` + M14 workspace
   add/remove-users + M15 board add-users + M25 item
   update bulk all share the `data.results[]` per-record
   shape backed by `dispatchSequential`. The pattern is
   mature enough that future bulk-write surfaces (M27
   webhook send? notification fan-out?) inherit the same
   wrapper shape with minimal new pre-flight contract
   surface. Treat M25's `partial-success-bulk.ts` as the
   reference for any future bulk-write-with-per-item-
   error verb.

## 18. M26a post-mortem — `dev` namespace setup verbs (discover / configure / doctor)

**Shipped:** pre-flight `1620220` (contract diff + Codex
pre-flight round-1 fixes `4433297` + round-2 fixes `f605260` +
close-docs sweep `d9b2d6d`) + implementation feat `19755e3` +
Codex impl review round-1 fixes `2be9021` + round-2 fixes
`c70deb3` + round-3 fixes `2a3c06c` + this close-docs sweep.
**M26b (10 workflow verbs across sprint/epic/release/task)
remains stubbed** — a separate session per the setup-vs-
workflow cleavage decision at M26 IMPL kickoff.

### Codex round summary

**Pre-flight contract diff** (2 rounds; documented at §11
sub-section under M26 pre-flight closure — see commits
`4433297` / `f605260`).

**Implementation review** (3 rounds; converged at round 3
with only a P3-class test-quality finding):

- **Round 1** (1 P1 + 1 P2 + 1 P3 applied at `2be9021`):
  - **P1-1 (W9/W6):** `_shared.ts:resolveActiveDevProfile`
    swallowed `UsageError` from `parseGlobalFlags` via
    try/catch. `dev configure` could clobber the on-disk
    `[profiles.<name>.dev]` block BEFORE `emitMutation`'s
    re-parse threw usage_error (e.g.
    `--retry not-a-number dev configure --tasks-board 999`).
    Fix: drop the catch; `UsageError` bubbles synchronously
    before any file-write path runs. Integration test
    "Codex round-1 P1-1: invalid global flag leaves
    config.toml unchanged" pins the regression.
  - **P2-1 (W5):** `devDoctorCheckResultSchema` accepted
    any `details: Record<string, unknown>` shape; Decision
    2's deferred per-check details discriminated-union
    pinning was left open. Fix (round-1 attempt): ship
    `DEV_DOCTOR_REASONS` (11 reason values) + a
    `superRefine` requiring `details.reason` on fail.
  - **P3-1 (W2):** The `saveDevMapping` docstring claimed
    the smol-toml comment-loss contract correction was
    "documented at v0.3-plan §17 M26a post-mortem" — but
    the post-mortem lands at the close-docs sweep, not
    the IMPL fix-up. Fix: soften wording until §18
    actually lands.

- **Round 2** (0 P1 + 2 P2 + 1 P3 applied at `c70deb3`):
  - **P2-1 (W2):** Round-1's `superRefine` was runtime-only;
    `monday schema`'s `z.toJSONSchema(outputSchema)` didn't
    surface the reason enum structurally — agents
    introspecting `dev.doctor`'s output shape saw
    `details: record<string, unknown>` with no hint of the
    closed enum. Fix: refactor `devDoctorCheckResultSchema`
    into a structural `z.discriminatedUnion('status',
    [ok, warn, fail])` with per-status detail shapes
    (`okCheckDetailsSchema` / `warnCheckDetailsSchema` /
    `failCheckDetailsSchema`). `failResult` helper's
    signature widened to require
    `details: { reason: DevDoctorReason } & Record<string,
    unknown>` so TypeScript enforces the pinned enum at
    every fail emit site.
  - **P2-2 (W4):** Round-1's `superRefine` incidentally
    blocked `ok` results carrying any non-enum `reason`
    key even though W4(c) said ok should never be blocked
    by details content. Subsumed by P2-1's structural
    refactor — the ok variant of the discriminated union
    takes open `details` (no `reason` constraint).
  - **P3-1 (W7):** Module docstring carried `<M26A-IMPL-SHA>`
    placeholder + stale "does NOT own profile-config IO"
    wording + reference to a never-materialised future
    `writeProfileDevBlock`. Fix: replace SHA placeholder
    with `19755e3` + update ownership text to reflect that
    this module now owns the dev-block WRITE path
    (`saveDevMapping`) while `src/config/profiles.ts`
    retains the base profile-config schema + read path.

- **Round 3** (0 P1 + 0 P2 + 1 P3 applied at `2a3c06c`):
  - **P3-1 (W5):** Round-2's discriminated-union refactor
    moved `details.reason` enforcement from `superRefine`
    (with custom message text) to zod's native shape — but
    it incidentally weakened the round-1 unit-test
    assertions from message-specific regex matches to
    generic `toThrow()`. Codex noted those generic-throw
    tests would pass for ANY rejection, not specifically
    the intended one. Fix: strengthen the three assertions
    to use `safeParse(...).success` + verify specific
    `issue.path` / `issue.code` values (`'details.reason'`
    +  `'invalid_value'` for enum mismatches; `'details'`
    + `'invalid_type'` for null-details rejections).
    Codex round-3 also verified out-of-band that
    `z.toJSONSchema(devDoctorOutputSchema)` contains all
    11 reason enum values — confirming the round-2 P2-1
    structural-schema fix correctly surfaces the reason
    vocabulary to `monday schema` consumers.

R-NEW-25 "findings up front" directive validated across all
three impl-review rounds; R-NEW-17 W1 redactor-pattern audit
returned "nothing flagged" against all M26a detail-key
surfaces. Both techniques continue template-stable for the
v0.4 cycle.

### Contract drift findings

**1. `smol-toml`'s round-trip-preserves-comments claim
(pre-flight → IMPL).** The M26 pre-flight `saveDevMapping`
docstring asserted "The TOML write preserves surrounding
formatting + comments per `smol-toml`'s round-trip" — but
empirical inspection at IMPL kickoff confirmed `smol-toml`'s
`stringify` produces canonical TOML output with comments
discarded. **Mitigation:** most `config.toml` files are CLI-
managed (`monday auth login` populates the credentials side;
`saveDevMapping` populates the dev side), so the comment-
preservation concern is narrow in practice. Updated the
runtime docstring inline at `19755e3`. A future v0.4 string-
surgery write path could preserve comments outside the dev
block if user demand surfaces. Lesson: when the pre-flight
makes claims about third-party library behavior, the IMPL
should verify against the library's actual semantics —
docstrings about library round-tripping are easy to
overstate without testing.

**2. Profile-resolution write-safety (round-1 P1-1).** The
pre-flight helper signature was `resolveActiveDevProfile`
returning a profile name; the round-0 implementation
swallowed `UsageError` to "fall back to env/default" but
this left a window where `dev configure` could clobber
config.toml BEFORE `emitMutation`'s eventual re-parse
threw the usage_error. The fix is one-line (remove the
try/catch) but the lesson is broader: write-side actions
must validate ALL upstream contracts (including global
flags) BEFORE any file IO. The integration test
"Codex round-1 P1-1" pins this regression for future
write-side dev verbs (every M26b workflow verb that
writes — `dev task start` / `dev task done` / `dev task
block` — inherits the same `_shared.ts` plumbing).

**3. Sub-items virtual board pollution (empirical-probe
finding at IMPL kickoff).** Pre-flight Decision 1 closure
assumed Monday's `boards()` walker returns user-created
boards only. The IMPL empirical probe (`scripts/probe/
m26-board-type.ts`) surfaced that `Board.type` discriminates
`board` (parent) vs `sub_items_board` (auto-generated
`Subitems of <BoardName>` virtual entries) vs
`custom_object` vs `document`. `Subitems of Tasks` matched
the `tasks` substring heuristic, creating ambiguous-match
results that prevented auto-mapping. **Walker filter
amendment:** `Board.type === 'board'` filter is now applied
in `discoverDevBoards`'s runtime body — behavior-
equivalent refinement (NOT a contract amendment;
`DiscoverBoardCandidate` schema unchanged). Pinned in the
module docstring's empirical-probe-findings paragraph.
Lesson: substring-based heuristics need a "user-board only"
filter at the walker tier; the schema's filter rationale
belongs in the implementation comment, not the contract
surface.

### Exit ratchets

- **Test count:** 3011 → 3109 (+98 net; 1 skipped
  unchanged). +76 new unit tests in
  `tests/unit/api/dev-conventions.test.ts` (pure-helper
  invariants + 4 runtime fetchers + 10-check doctor

  `tests/integration/commands/dev.test.ts` (3 verbs × per-
  verb happy paths + failure modes including
  dev_not_configured surfaces + the P1-1 write-safety
  regression).
- **Coverage:** 99.09 / 96.03 / 99.24 / 99.28 →
  99.10 / 95.96 / 99.24 / 99.29. Branches margin
  0.58pp → 0.51pp at impl close (small dip; new runtime
  branches contribute the delta but stay well above the
  95.45 floor with 0.51pp surplus). Three deferred
  branches-margin residuals (`item/search.ts` 88.23%,
  `errors.ts` ~95.37%, `dry-run.ts` 96.26%) carried over
  from M25 — out of M26a scope.
- **Floor:** 95 / 95.45 / 95 / 95 (unchanged; never
  lowered).
- **ERROR_CODES count:** 29 (unchanged — `dev_not_configured`
  + `dev_board_misconfigured` pre-registered at v0.3-M5b
  for forward-compat; M26a routes through them without
  widening).
- **Command count:** 91 (unchanged — IMPL fills 3 of the 13
  stubs pre-flight pinned; commands themselves don't
  add or remove).
- **No new snapshot entry.** Following the M23 + M24 + M25
  precedent: per-verb integration cassettes carry the
  byte-shape coverage; no per-verb envelope snapshot
  needed. `schema --json` snapshot's `command_count: 91`

- **v8 coverage glitch on `partial-success-bulk.ts`** (M25

### Refactor backlog candidates surfaced

- **R-NEW-30 — `_shared.ts:resolveActiveDevProfile` lift
  (2-consumer status at M26a; trigger expected to fire at
  M26b 3rd+ consumer).** New helper extracted at M26a IMPL
  to share the profile-name-resolution preamble across
  `dev discover` / `dev configure` / `dev doctor` (3
  setup verbs at M26a) + the 10 M26b workflow verbs which
  ALL need the same preamble. Currently 3 consumers (M26a),
  jumping to 13 (M26a + M26b) at M26b IMPL. The lift was
  done preemptively at M26a IMPL since 3 consumers
  already cleared the R7/R8 3-consumer trigger; documented
  here as the canonical "load profile → resolve → throw
  if implicit-v1" preamble pattern for any future dev-
  namespace verb.

- **R-NEW-31 — discriminated-union per-status details
  schema pattern (1 consumer at M26a; LOW priority
  watch-item).** The M26a round-2 P2-1 fix introduced a
  pattern of structuring per-status detail shapes via
  `z.discriminatedUnion('status', [...])` + per-status
  named detail schemas (`okCheckDetailsSchema` /
  `warnCheckDetailsSchema` / `failCheckDetailsSchema`).
  This pattern would generalize to any verb whose output
  carries a status discriminator with per-status detail
  variability (e.g. M27 `webhook list` with per-webhook
  status; M28 release-prep readiness checks). Fires at
  2nd + 3rd consumer if a future verb adopts it.

### Lessons for the next milestone (M26b)

- **`_shared.ts:resolveActiveDevProfile` is the shared
  preamble.** Every M26b workflow verb (sprint/epic/release/
  task × verbs) starts with the same 3 lines: resolve
  active profile name → load dev mapping (which fires
  `dev_not_configured` if absent) → branch on the noun-
  specific board ID. Reuse this; don't re-litigate the
  config-error vs dev_not_configured-error split.

- **`Board.type === 'board'` filter is in `dev-conventions.ts`,
  not the workflow verbs.** M26b workflow verbs read board
  IDs from the configured mapping; the walker filter only
  affects discovery (`dev discover`'s candidate list). The
  10 workflow verbs DON'T re-walk; they hydrate by ID via
  the existing `boards(ids:)` patterns the M22+M23+M24+M25
  read paths use.

- **Per-check `details.reason` enum is closed.** M26b's
  `dev task start` / `dev task done` / `dev task block`
  surfaces don't add new doctor checks — they consume the
  configured tasks board's status column. If a future
  v0.4 milestone adds new doctor checks, the
  `DEV_DOCTOR_REASONS` enum widens additively; existing
  agents keep working.

- **JSON Schema export discipline.** The round-2 P2-1
  finding (runtime-only `superRefine` not surfaced via
  `z.toJSONSchema`) is generalizable: any verb's
  contract enforced via `superRefine` is NOT
  introspectable via `monday schema`. For agent-facing
  contract pinning, use structural shapes
  (`discriminatedUnion`, `intersection`, literal types)
  rather than refinements. Refinements stay useful for
  cross-field invariants (e.g. `dev configure`'s "at
  least one --*-board flag" superRefine).

- **3 review rounds, but converging.** M26a IMPL ran 3
  Codex rounds (vs M24's 2, M25's 1, M22's 3) because
  round 1 + round 2 each surfaced a substantive P2 that
  the previous round's fix didn't fully address. The
  pattern: round 1 surfaced a P2 with a partial fix;
  round 2 identified the structural gap (`z.toJSONSchema`
  blindness); round 3 verified the structural fix + only
  surfaced a test-quality P3. Future milestones with
  similar schema-refactor scope should expect 2-3 rounds.

## 19. M26b post-mortem — `dev` namespace workflow verbs (sprint/epic/release/task)

**Shipped:** implementation feat `10cd1c5` + Codex impl review
round-1 fixes `34a5bc1` (0 P1 + 3 P2 + 1 P3) + round-2 fixes
`078dae3` (1 P1 + 1 P2 + 0 P3) + round-3 fixes `8ea66c4` (0 P1 +
0 P2 + 1 P3 — cosmetic) + this close-docs sweep. **M26 closed
end-to-end** (M26a setup verbs + M26b workflow verbs both
live).

### Codex round summary

**Pre-flight contract diff** was closed at M26 (single
`1620220` covering both halves); no additional pre-flight
needed at M26b IMPL since the workflow-verb stubs were
already pinned.

**Implementation review** (3 rounds; converged with only a P3-
class cosmetic finding):

- **Round 1** (0 P1 + 3 P2 + 1 P3 applied at `34a5bc1`):
  - **P2-1 (out-of-band / `docs/output-shapes.md` §M26 task
    verbs):** Task mutation verbs dropped the promised top-
    level `resolved_ids.status` echo. `flipTaskStatus`
    already returned `columnId`; threaded
    `resolvedIds: { status: flip.columnId }` through
    `emitMutation` on all three task verbs + added 3 per-
    verb integration assertions.
  - **P2-2 (W6):** `walkDevBoardItems` surfaced bare
    `internal_error` instead of `dev_board_misconfigured`
    when the configured board was inaccessible. Monday
    returns `{boards: []}` for deleted / revoked /
    never-existed boards; `fetchItemsPage`'s `.min(1)`
    schema rejected the empty array as malformed →
    `internal_error`. For a dev workflow read that's
    runtime mapping drift. Fix: try/catch around `paginate`
    that rewraps to `dev_board_misconfigured` with
    `reason: 'not_accessible'`, mirroring
    `hydrateDevBoardColumns`'s shape so per-verb error
    surfaces are consistent across read + mutation paths.
  - **P2-3 (W9):** `create_update` side-effect responses
    on `task done` / `task block` weren't parsed at the
    boundary (`client.raw<T>` is compile-time only).
    Lifted a shared `fireDevCreateUpdate(client, itemId,
    body, opName)` helper into `dev-conventions.ts` with
    a strict zod schema (`create_update: { id: string } |
    null`). Both verbs migrated; the inline mutation
    string + `CreateUpdateResponseShape` interface lifted
    out.
  - **P3-1 (W3):** `create_update` complexity was ignored
    on verbs that fired the side-effect — verbose output
    reported the flip leg's complexity instead of the
    freshest (create_update) snapshot. `fireDevCreateUpdate`
    now returns `complexity` alongside `updateId`; envelope
    complexity is `update.complexity ?? flip.complexity`.

- **Round 2** (1 P1 + 1 P2 + 0 P3 applied at `078dae3`):
  - **P1-1 (W3'):** GraphQL operation-name mismatch on
    `fireDevCreateUpdate` — the document was statically
    named `mutation DevCreateUpdate` while the wire
    `operationName` was per-verb (`DevTaskDoneCreateUpdate`
    / `DevTaskBlockCreateUpdate`). GraphQL servers may
    reject mismatched operationName + named-op pairs
    ("Operation 'X' not found"); cassette tests missed
    this because the fixture transport matches the wire's
    operationName, not the document's named operation. Live
    Monday calls would have failed. Fix: dynamic
    `buildCreateUpdateMutation(operationName)` builder so
    doc name + wire operationName always agree. Mirrors
    the static pattern `executeItemMutation` uses (per-
    mutation-kind constants where doc + operationName
    match). Added 2 cassette `match_query` tests asserting
    `mutation DevTaskDoneCreateUpdate` /
    `mutation DevTaskBlockCreateUpdate` literal strings
    on the wire.
  - **P2-1 (W2'):** Round-1's `walkDevBoardItems` rewrap
    narrowing was too broad — any malformed `items_page`
    payload carrying `details.board_id === boardId`
    would have collapsed into a misleading
    `dev_board_misconfigured` (`reason: 'not_accessible'`)
    instead of surfacing the original `internal_error`
    with `details.issues`. Fix: lifted
    `isEmptyBoardsArrayIssue` helper scanning
    `details.issues` for the exact `{path: 'boards',
    code: 'too_small'}` zod issue. Other schema-drift
    failures (e.g. Monday adding a required field we
    haven't modeled) keep their original
    `internal_error` shape. Regression test drives a
    non-empty `boards` + malformed `items_page` (missing
    `cursor` key) to verify the surface stays
    `internal_error`.

- **Round 3** (0 P1 + 0 P2 + 1 P3 applied at `8ea66c4`):
  - **P3-1 (out-of-band — JSDoc positioning):** Round-2's
    P2-1 fix inserted `isEmptyBoardsArrayIssue`'s
    definition between `walkDevBoardItems`'s JSDoc block
    and its function. Pure cosmetic cleanup; moved the
    helper above the `walkDevBoardItems` docstring so each
    docstring sits immediately above its function.

R-NEW-25 "findings up front" directive validated across all
three M26b impl-review rounds (the 13th + 14th + 15th cumulative
validations); R-NEW-17 W1 redactor-pattern audit returned
"nothing flagged" against all M26b detail-key surfaces.

### Contract drift findings

**1. `docs/output-shapes.md` pin missed at IMPL (round-1
P2-1).** The pre-flight stubs threw `internal_error` with a
hint; they didn't carry the `resolved_ids` echo. When the IMPL
filled the bodies, the `flipTaskStatus` helper exposed the
resolved `columnId` correctly but the verb action bodies
didn't thread it through to `emitMutation`. Codex round-1
caught this against the docs/output-shapes.md §M26 pin.
**Lesson:** when filling stub bodies for a verb whose output
shape is documented in `docs/output-shapes.md`, re-read the
docs/output-shapes.md entry before considering the verb
"done". The contract carries surfaces (`resolved_ids`,
`side_effects`) the stub doesn't surface.

**2. `walkDevBoardItems` error-routing contract divergence
(round-1 P2-2 → round-2 P2-1 refinement).** The M25 fail-fast
+ partial-success bulk path uses `fetchItemsPage`'s `.min(1)`
schema with `internal_error` semantics — that's the right call
for the items-update path where `{boards: []}` IS a wire-shape
break (no item to update against an empty board). For dev
workflow READS, the same wire response is normal mapping drift
(user pointed to a board that doesn't exist anymore). Two
distinct interpretations of the same wire shape. The fix
needed two iterations: round-1 added the rewrap, round-2
narrowed it to the exact zod issue shape so other parse
failures keep their `internal_error` surface intact. **Lesson:**
when one helper has two failure semantics (one consumer wants
schema drift surfaced as `internal_error`, another wants
runtime drift surfaced as a typed code), the call-site wrapper
must narrow on the issue PATH/CODE, not just the error code.
The two-round convergence is the right shape — round 1 ships
the behavior, round 2 narrows the predicate.

**3. GraphQL operation-name vs named-operation drift
(round-2 P1-1).** The static `mutation DevCreateUpdate`
document + per-verb `operationName: DevTaskDoneCreateUpdate`
combination would have failed against Monday's live server.
Cassettes didn't catch this because `FixtureTransport`
matches the wire `operationName` field, not the document's
named operation. **Lesson:** GraphQL `operationName` must
always match a named operation in the document. The safest
shape is to either (a) make doc + operationName the same
literal string (the M5b `executeItemMutation` pattern), or
(b) build the doc dynamically from the operationName (the
M26b `buildCreateUpdateMutation` pattern). Avoid the
default-named-doc + per-call-overridden-operationName mix.
Worth adding to the codex-pre-flight-review template's W1
neighborhood as a future audit point — fires whenever a new
verb fires a custom GraphQL mutation with a non-trivial
operationName.

### Exit ratchets

- **Test count:** 3109 → 3183 (+74 net; 1 skipped
  unchanged). +15 new unit tests in
  `tests/unit/api/dev-conventions.test.ts` (M26b pure
  helpers: `extractLinkedItemIds`,
  `findRelationColumnIdToBoard`, `resolveStatusColumn`,
  `resolveCanonicalLabel`); +59 integration tests in
  `tests/integration/commands/dev.test.ts` (10 verbs ×
  happy paths + failure modes including
  `dev_not_configured` per-slot surfaces +
  `dev_board_misconfigured` rewraps + 3 round-1 fix
  regression tests + 3 round-2 fix regression tests).
- **Coverage:** 99.10 / 95.96 / 99.24 / 99.29 →
  99.04 / 95.79 / 99.28 / 99.31. Branches margin
  0.51pp → 0.34pp at M26b impl close (small dip; 10 new
  runtime verbs widen the denominator). Three deferred
  branches-margin residuals (`item/search.ts` 88.23%,
  `errors.ts` ~95.37%, `dry-run.ts` 96.26%) carried over
  from M25 + M26a — out of M26b scope.
- **Floor:** 95 / 95.45 / 95 / 95 (unchanged; never
  lowered).
- **ERROR_CODES count:** 29 (unchanged — M26b routes
  through the existing `dev_not_configured` +
  `dev_board_misconfigured` + `not_found` +
  `internal_error` codes; no new codes registered).
- **Command count:** 91 (unchanged — IMPL fills 10 of the
  13 stubs pre-flight pinned; commands themselves don't
  add or remove).
- **No new snapshot entry.** Mirrors M23 + M24 + M25 + M26a
  precedent: per-verb integration cassettes carry the byte-

- **v8 instrumentation glitch on `dev-conventions.ts`** —
  the file reports `FNF:0 LF:0 BRF:0` in `coverage/lcov.info`
  despite the M26b unit + integration tests exercising the
  new helpers (mirrors the M25-close glitch on
  `partial-success-bulk.ts`). Module-level glitch; pre-
  existing M26a `dev-conventions.ts` instrumented
  correctly, so something about the M26b additions trips v8's
  per-module instrumentation. Investigate at M28 release-prep
  if it spreads to further modules; the workaround would be
  a `coverage.include` override in `vitest.config.ts`.

### Refactor backlog candidates surfaced

  `walkDevBoardItems` (7 consumers across read verbs),

- **R-NEW-37 — Codex template audit-point for GraphQL

### Lessons for the next milestone (M27)

- **M27 introduces real wire surface (webhooks).** Unlike

  `delete_webhook` (and a `webhooks` query). The empirical-

- **Decision 9 is the M27 pre-flight gate.** "Webhook
  event-type validation" must close BEFORE M27 pre-flight
  contract diff. The decision shapes the input-schema
  pin on `webhook create --event <type>` (closed enum vs
  open string vs server-side-validated lookup).

- **R-NEW-37 watch-item.** If M27 introduces custom
  GraphQL mutations with non-trivial operationName + named-
  op pairs, the codex-pre-flight-review template should add
  an explicit audit-point (per the §19 contract drift
  finding #3). Today's pre-flight rounds catch this only
  if the reviewer happens to look at the doc text;
  making it template-stable closes the loophole.

- **M26b verb tests as reference for M27.** The 59 new

## 22. Refactor backlog — R44 / R49 / R50 migrated + non-R-class

Migrated from `docs/v0.2-plan.md` §22 at v0.3 plan
opening. The shipped v0.2 R-class entries (R28 / R29 /
R37 / R38 / R40–R53) stay in v0.2-plan §22 unchanged;

weren't shipped at v0.2 close — plus the non-R-class
quality refactor.

### R44 candidate — generic `projectMutationResource` over R28/R37/R43/R45/R48

**Migrated from `docs/v0.2-plan.md` §22 R44 entry**
(see v0.2-plan lines 4457–4517 for the full migration
context). Five per-noun projection helpers post-v0.2:
`projectMutationItem` (R28) / `projectMutationUpdate`
(R37) / `projectMutationBoard` (R43) /
`projectMutationColumn` (R45) / `projectMutationGroup`
(R48). Plus the inline-only workspace single-target
mutation verbs (M14) that never got their own helper —
post the R42 + R42-followup folding (`6d93f65`),
workspace's per-verb projectors call into the
generalised `assertResponseFieldPresent` directly so the
"sixth-noun candidate" status is firmer than at v0.2
close.

The five per-noun helpers diverge on `idKey` /
`errorMessage` prefix / `errorCode` / `schema` (the
noun's projection schema). Everything else — the
`if (raw === null || raw === undefined)` check + the
`throw new ApiError(errorCode, errorMessage,
{ details: { [idKey]: idValue } })` shape + the
`unwrapOrThrow(schema.safeParse(raw), { context, details
})` parse — is identical.

**Sites at v0.3 plan opening (5 active + 1 implicit):**
item / update / board / column / group + workspace
(post-R42 R42-followup, workspace single-target
mutations call `assertResponseFieldPresent` directly
through the generalised contract; the per-verb
projector pattern itself is the candidate sixth noun if
a future refactor unifies the inline-only path with the
named-helper path).

**Trigger:** **6 nouns post-v0.3 if the workspace
projector unification fires**, OR a fresh v0.3 noun
(M19's tag-directory? — unlikely, tags are an account-
scope read, not a mutation noun; M27's webhook
mutations? — possible if `create_webhook` /
`delete_webhook` projectors land as a sibling helper).

**Risk:** medium. Touches 5 noun families simultaneously
+ the cassette assertions on each. The byte-identical-
tests discipline catches behavioural regressions, but
the design question ("is the per-noun wrapper layer
worth keeping?") is aesthetic and can split a maintainer
one way or the other.

**When to schedule:** **v0.3 cleanup window candidate.**
Earliest non-scope-creep moment is the M19 → M20
cleanup window (R51 cadence between M17 and M18 is the
precedent; R53 cadence between M18 and v0.3-opening is
the second precedent). If a sixth-noun trigger fires
during M27 (webhook mutations), R44 lands then; if
not, it stays deferred until v0.4 or until a sixth-
noun lift naturally surfaces.

board-archive + group-archive (all M15–M17, all
shipped).

lands AND the snapshot semantics align.** v0.3 doesn't
introduce new archive verbs (M27 webhook delete is
delete-only; no snapshot-bearing archive). Stays
deferred.

**When to schedule:** **defer further to v0.4 OR fold
into a parameter-count-permissive variant if the
archive-snapshot inline divergence becomes a
maintenance burden.** If M28's release prep surfaces
inconsistent snapshot output across the three verbs,
the consolidation pressure rises; otherwise leave
parallel.

### Non-R-class — `unsupportedColumnTypeError` category-table-driven refactor

**Migrated from `docs/v0.2-plan.md` §26 looking-ahead**
(the post-v0.2 → v0.3 cleanup-window deferral —
"R42's coverage-recovery work pushed this session past
the typical refactor budget; the quality refactor was
bonus polish per the handoff and ships at the v0.3 →
first-cleanup-window or when the next deferral target
lands").

**Current shape.** `src/api/column-values.ts
unsupportedColumnTypeError` is a 5-branch if/else over
read_only_forever / v0_2_writer_expansion / files-
shaped / time_tracking / future categories. Each branch
hand-builds its `details` slot.

**Proposed shape.** A category table (one
`Record<Category, { deferred_to?: string, read_only?:
boolean, hint: string }>`) that the function indexes
into; the resulting helper is ~40 lines vs the current
~80. M19's tentative-row reclassification (the v0_2
row loses its three members; the v0_3 row picks them up
+ time_tracking) is the natural trigger to do this
refactor — touching the function anyway means the
category-table consolidation costs nothing extra.

**Sites at v0.3 plan opening (1 function, 1 caller):**
single function with a single caller; doesn't fit the
R-class 3-consumer trigger. Tracked as **non-R-class
quality refactor**, not as R-N.

**Risk:** low. Pure consolidation; byte-identical tests
catch any per-category drift.

**When to schedule:** **fold into M19** (the
tentative-row reclassification touches the function;
the consolidation costs nothing extra at that point).
Alternatively defer to the M19 → M20 cleanup window if
M19's pre-flight surfaces this as scope-creep risk.

### R-candidate — `requireResolutionContext` translator-context-undefined throw helper

Surfaced by the M19→M20 cleanup-window peopleResolution
parity fix (`2cbf0d3`). Three async translators in
`src/api/column-values.ts` start with an identical
"if context undefined, throw `internal_error` with
`{column_id, column_type, hint}`" shape:
`translatePeople` (line 743), `translateTags` (line 816),
`translateRelation` (line 959). Each is ~15 LOC; lift
collapses ~45 → ~15.

**Sites (3):** people / tags / relations — exactly at the
R-class 3-consumer trigger.

**Trade-off.** The per-translator hint mentions specific
callback names (`resolveMe + resolveEmail`, `resolveTags`,
`validateItems`) and specific module references
(`buildResolutionContexts`). A generic helper either:
(a) genericises the hint → loses agent-actionable detail;
(b) accepts per-translator parameters → pushes the lift
into the >4-parameter zone (slot name + column-type
literal + hint template + callback-shape example).
The borderline-ness is why this stayed un-lifted at M19
implementation.

**Trigger:** **already at 3 consumers — fires on any
fourth async-translator that needs a context.** Most
likely candidate: M20's `time_tracking` verbs DON'T need
a translator (they route through new verbs, not the
friendly-translator dispatch); so the trigger waits for
either a v0.4 friendly-translator extension OR a M27
webhook signature requiring a similar context.

**Risk:** low — pure consolidation, byte-identical tests
catch per-translator drift. The risk is over-genericising
the hint into something that says "missing context" but
not "wire `validateItems` through `buildResolutionContexts`".

**When to schedule:** **defer to M19→M20 cleanup-window
follow-up OR a fourth-translator trigger.** Below the
borderline-trade-off threshold today; if M20's pre-flight
surfaces evidence the helper would be load-bearing
(unlikely), fold then.

**Proposed shape.** A `seedUserDirectoryCache(env,
entries)` helper exported from
`tests/integration/commands/_item-fixtures.ts` (or a new
`_cache-fixtures.ts` if the helper isn't item-scoped).
Closes over `resolveCacheRoot({ env: { XDG_CACHE_HOME }})`
and `writeEntry(cacheRoot, { kind: 'users' }, entries)`.
~5 LOC saved per call site; load-bearing benefit is
that any future user-directory cache schema change
(e.g. extending `UserDirectoryEntry` with a slug field)
becomes a one-line update across the suite rather than
three.

**Trigger:** **already at 3 consumers — fires now.**

**Risk:** low — test-side mechanical lift, byte-identical
behaviour. The pre-cleanup-window R20 / R21 / R23 lifts
followed the same pattern.

**When to schedule:** **M19 → M20 cleanup-window
follow-up** (this is the natural seam — the helper
would have been used at the cassette's first call site
if it existed). Alternatively defer to the next
cleanup-window if the third site (`cache.test.ts`)
turns out to need a different shape under closer
inspection (its writes are schema-corruption fixtures,
not directory-population fixtures, so the lift may
need a `seedRawCache(env, kind, payload)` general form
rather than the user-directory-specific shape).

The cleanup window is **not** a hard requirement — if

### R-watch-item — pre-flight stub-body `Promise.reject` pattern

Surfaced by the M20 pre-flight (`a702af2`). Three
modules across two milestones (M19 + M20) follow the
identical pre-flight stub pattern:

- `api/tag-directory.ts` — pre-flight at `d822982` (3
  stub functions: `resolveTags` + `loadAccountTags` +
  the `NOT_IMPLEMENTED_HINT` constant; runtime filled
  at M19 implementation `19801f8`).
- `api/board-relation-validation.ts` — pre-flight at
  `d822982` (1 stub function:
  `validateBoardRelationItems`; runtime filled at M19
  implementation `a569590`).
- `api/time-tracking.ts` — pre-flight at `a702af2`
  (2 stub functions: `startTimeTracking` +
  `stopTimeTracking`). At M20 implementation `b7690b2`
  the stubs took the **documentation-only escape hatch**
  rather than swapping to wire-supporting runtime
  bodies — empirical probe (2026-05-10, see §12 M20
  post-mortem) confirmed Monday's API does not currently
  support time_tracking writes, so the stubs swapped
  from `internal_error` "pre-flight stub" rejections to
  `usage_error` "API not supported" rejections (still
  forward-marker bodies, just a different rejection
  shape). The pattern's watch-item logic still applies:
  count outstanding pre-flight stubs across pre-flighted
  milestones; the M20 case is now in a stable
  documentation-only state and won't bias the next
  trigger evaluation.

Pattern:

```ts
const NOT_IMPLEMENTED_HINT = '... lands at M<X> implementation ...';
/* c8 ignore start — stub body rejects on every path. */
export const fooBar = (
  _inputs: FooBarInputs,
): Promise<FooBarResult> =>
  Promise.reject(
    new ApiError(
      'internal_error',
      'fooBar is a v0.3-M<X> pre-flight stub — ...',
      { details: { hint: NOT_IMPLEMENTED_HINT } },
    ),
  );
/* c8 ignore stop */
```

**Sites:** 6 stub functions across 3 modules across 2
milestones. The first 4 stubs (M19's `tag-directory.ts`
+ `board-relation-validation.ts`) deleted at M19
implementation when the runtime bodies replaced them;
the last 2 (M20's `time-tracking.ts`) shifted from
`internal_error` pre-flight stubs to `usage_error`
documentation-only rejections at M20 implementation
(`b7690b2`) when the empirical probe confirmed Monday's
API doesn't support time_tracking writes. So the
post-M20 outstanding count was **0 pre-flight stubs and
2 documentation-only forward-markers**.

**Updated post-M21 pre-flight (`5c07840`).** M21
shipped 11 new `internal_error`-rejecting stubs across
3 modules: `src/api/oauth.ts` (2 — `bindOAuthListener`
+ `exchangeCode`); `src/config/credentials.ts` (6 —
`resolveCredentialsPath`, `readCredentials`,
`writeCredentials`, `setProfileCredentials`,
`deleteProfileCredentials`, `resolveProfileToken`);
`src/config/profiles.ts` (3 —
`resolveProfilesConfigPath`, `loadProfilesConfig`,
`selectProfile`). **Trigger condition met** —
`credentials.ts` ships ≥4 stubs in one module (6 ≥ 4)
AND cumulative outstanding stubs cross 5 (11 > 5).

**Why not R-class today.** The helper would have a
short lifespan per consumer — every stub it abstracts
gets replaced by the runtime body within 1-2 sessions.
Net repetition over project lifetime is bounded. The
3-consumer R-class trigger fires on persistent
duplication, not transient duplication.

**Re-evaluation at M21 pre-flight `5c07840`.** Trigger
condition fired (≥4 stubs in `credentials.ts`; 11
cumulative outstanding); the lift was nevertheless
**rejected for the same short-lifespan reason that
originally deferred the candidate**. Every M21 stub
gets replaced at M21 implementation kickoff (next
session) by a real runtime body — abstracting them
into `buildPreflightStub<TInput, TResult>(name,
milestone, hint)` would land 11 helper-call sites that
all need un-abstraction within one session. Net
repetition cost is bounded; lift cost would dwarf it.
The local `STUB_HINT` constant pattern in each module
(`src/config/credentials.ts` + `src/config/profiles.ts`
each export a private `STUB_HINT` string the stubs
share) is the in-design backstop — keeps the M21-
pending wording consistent within a module without
the cross-module lift. Re-evaluate at the next future
pre-flight that ships ≥4 stubs (M22+ unlikely; M27
webhooks borderline).

**Trigger.** Watch-item, not a scheduled lift. Fires
if either:
- A future pre-flight commit ships ≥4 stub functions in
  one module (a single `change_simple_column_value`
  successor pre-flight at v0.4 might hit this), OR
- Cumulative outstanding stubs exceed 5 simultaneously
  (e.g. M21 + M22 pre-flights ship before M21 / M22
  implementations close).

**Risk:** low if it fires — pure consolidation, the
stubs already throw `internal_error` with verbatim
hint shape. The risk is helper-creep: a
`buildPreflightStub<TInput, TResult>(name, milestone,
hintTemplate)` API would need to thread the type
parameters through cleanly without `as` casts.

**When to schedule:** **deferred — watch only.** No
action this session or M20 implementation. Re-evaluate
at the close of any future pre-flight that ships ≥4
stubs OR at the v0.3 release exit checklist if the
cumulative count crosses 5.

### R-watch-item — `FixtureTransport` order-tolerance for parallel transport calls

Surfaced by the Node 24 cassette-ordering flake fix
(`7dbcf7e fix(m11): serialise cross-board metadata
loads`). `runCrossBoardMove` loaded source + target
board metadata via `Promise.all`; the test
`FixtureTransport` (`tests/fixtures/load.ts`)
**strictly consumes `queue[0]` per request**, and on
Node 24 microtask scheduling occasionally flips the
order in which two concurrent transport calls reach
the cassette. When target arrives before source,
queue[0]'s `match_variables: {ids: ['111']}` fails
the variables check, the transport throws
`cassette mismatch`, and the runner maps the
`internal_error` envelope onto exit code 2 — turning
an order-correct cassette into a flake.

**Single consumer fix shipped at `7dbcf7e`** —
serialise the two metadata loads in `move.ts`.
Production-latency cost is ≤one cache-miss network
roundtrip (~300ms vs max-of-two); negligible for an
interactive mutation. The alternative durable fix
(out-of-order matching in `FixtureTransport` when
`match_variables` disambiguate the queue entry) would
weaken cassette-as-strict-sequence semantics across
105 test files for the benefit of one `Promise.all`
call site — the narrower fix wins.

**Trigger.** Watch-item, not a scheduled lift. Fires
if any of:
- A second `Promise.all` call site lands in `src/` and
  cannot be cleanly serialised (e.g., genuinely
  independent walkers with no causal ordering and
  measurable wall-clock benefit).
- A 3-consumer trigger fires across `Promise.all` /
  `Promise.race` / parallel-walker shapes (e.g., M23
  cross-board search fan-out across favorites, M27
  webhook batching).
- Test-flake retrospectives surface a second
  cassette-ordering case that can't be fixed by
  serialising the production code.

**Risk:** medium if it fires — the FixtureTransport
hardening would touch the cassette-matching algorithm,
which 105 test files depend on for strict-ordering
detection power. Findings that previously surfaced
as `cassette mismatch` (a real test-detection signal
when production sends operations out of expected
order) would silently succeed under out-of-order
matching. Mitigation if scheduled: gate the new
behaviour behind an explicit `match_variables` slot
(strict ordering preserved when match_variables is
omitted; out-of-order matching ONLY when
match_variables disambiguates).

**When to schedule:** **deferred — watch only.** No
action this session. Re-evaluate at any future
milestone that introduces a second Promise.all call
site or a 3rd parallel-walker shape.

### R-watch-item — empirical-probe step in pre-flight for novel API surfaces

Surfaced by the M20 implementation pivot (this
session). The M20 pre-flight contract pinned a
wire-mutation choice (likely `change_simple_column_
value` with a documented Monday time-tracking JSON
shape) based on cli-design §5.3 + §13 v0.3 reasoning;
empirical probing at M20 implementation kickoff
revealed Monday's API genuinely doesn't support
time_tracking writes. The pre-flight contract was
based on an inferred-from-docs assumption that
turned out to be wrong; the fix was a mid-
implementation pivot to documentation-only verbs
costing ~one session of re-doc + re-test + Codex
re-review.

**Process item, not a code refactor.** Future
pre-flights that pin a wire mutation against a
column type / API surface the CLI hasn't directly
written to should include an empirical-probe step in
the contract diff. Specifically:

- **Probe scope:** read the column / surface
  (proven), then attempt every reasonable wire
  mutation shape; introspect the mutation root for
  related mutation names; document Monday's exact
  error codes for each rejected shape.
- **Probe mechanics:** disposable scripts under
  `scripts/probe/` (gitignored — see §22
  R-candidate `scripts/probe/` reusable infra
  below); ad-hoc throwaway board created via
  `create_board` mutation, cleaned up via
  `delete_board` post-probe.
- **Probe documentation:** the empirical findings
  (date, API version, exact error codes,
  introspection counts) get baked into the
  pre-flight commit's docstrings / cli-design /
  output-shapes — load-bearing finding, named
  verbatim so future agents grepping `details.hint`
  can self-verify without re-running the probe.

**Likely affected milestones:**
- **M21 OAuth flow** — wire shape (`/oauth2/authorize`
  redirect URI matching, state-CSRF handling, scope
  string format, PKCE acceptance, error-response shapes
  from `/oauth2/token`) needs empirical confirmation; the
  cli-design §7.3 description covers agent-facing UX
  but not wire-level semantics.
- **M27 webhooks** — `create_webhook` /
  `delete_webhook` mutation acceptance, event-type
  enum validation, response shape (the SDK types
  these but our cassettes have never seen real
  Monday responses).
- **M28 multi-level subitems** — `create_subitem`
  against a hierarchy_type-classic board, multi-
  level depth limits (cli-design §5.8 Decision 10).

**Trigger.** Watch-item; per-milestone application
rather than a code lift. Fires at every future
pre-flight where the wire shape is novel (i.e., the
existing test cassettes don't cover the same
mutation path).

**Risk:** low — the cost is measured in throwaway-
board mutations against a real Monday workspace
(creating + deleting one test board per probe).

**When to schedule:** **per-pre-flight discipline,
fired for the first time at M21 pre-flight
`5c07840`; fired second time at M22 pre-flight (this
commit).**

**First fire (M21 pre-flight `5c07840`).** The
discipline triggered an OAuth probe matrix
(`scripts/probe/m21-oauth.ts`, gitignored) against
`auth.monday.com` on 2026-05-10, producing three
load-bearing findings that reshaped the design
before module signatures landed: (1) Monday's
`/oauth2/token` requires `client_secret` (PKCE-only
flow rejected → PKCE dropped from v0.3 design); (2)
`/oauth2/authorize` JWT-encodes arbitrary query
params into an `oauth_payload_token` redirect target
(PKCE params silently round-trip but aren't
enforced); (3) rejection responses follow RFC 6749
standard shape (`{error, error_description}`, status
400, JSON content-type) — maps verbatim to
`oauth_failed.details.{monday_code,
monday_description}`. **Would have been
unrecoverably wrong without the probe** — the
pre-flight had no other path to surface the
client_secret-mandatory finding before the module
signatures pinned. The discipline pays off at
exactly the kind of inferred-from-docs assumption
that bit M20.

**Second fire (M22 pre-flight, `fbab6b0`).** The
discipline triggered a seven-script probe matrix
(`scripts/probe/m22-{usage,usage-extended,usage-daily,
usage-platform-api,usage-analytics,usage-by-day,status}.ts`,
all gitignored) against `api.monday.com` on
2026-05-10, producing TWO load-bearing findings that
reshaped the M22 design before module signatures
landed: (1) `account.complexity` does NOT exist as a
GraphQL field — the cli-design pre-M22 wording was
loose; (2) the actual daily-budget surface is
`platform_api.daily_limit { base, total }` +
`platform_api.daily_analytics.by_day [{day, usage}]`,
and Monday tracks **operations per day** (200/day on
free tier) NOT complexity points — the per-minute
complexity surface (`complexity { before after query
reset_in_x_seconds }`) is a separate Monday quota
system already on v0.1's `account complexity`. The
M22 design pivoted inline: `monday usage` envelope
reshaped from `complexity_remaining_24h` to
`{daily_limit, usage_today, usage_remaining_today,
last_updated}`; cli-design §11.5.3 + §13 v0.3 entry
amended verbatim. Decision 8 closed against the
operations-based shape. **Would have been
unrecoverably wrong without the probe** — the M22
pre-flight would otherwise have pinned a
`complexity_remaining_24h` envelope against a
field that doesn't exist, surfacing as `not_found`-
shaped GraphQL errors at M22 implementation.

Plus one supplementary finding: the 401 envelope
shape under API version `2026-01` confirmed as
GraphQL-style (`{"errors":[{"message":"Not
authenticated","extensions":{"code":"NOT_AUTHENTICATED"}}]}`,
status 401, content-type
`application/json; charset=utf-8`); maps verbatim
to the `monday status` auth-probe step's
`unauthorized` reason. `Bearer <token>` prefix
ALSO works alongside bare `<token>` —
`.claude/rules/security.md`'s rule against the
prefix remains a precaution (proxy / log layer
behaviour), not API-enforced.

The discipline holds at two pre-flights at the M22
close — and has since fired twice more:

**Third fire (M23 pre-flight, `1fefdb1`).** Seven
probe scripts at `scripts/probe/m23-*.ts` (all
gitignored) against `api.monday.com` on 2026-05-11
produced six load-bearing findings reshaping the M23
cross-board + favorites design: (1) favorites at
top-level `Query.favorites: [GraphqlHierarchyObjectItem!]`
with polymorphic `object.type` enum (Board | Folder |
Dashboard | Workspace) — `monday board favorites` is
a 2-stage filter+hydrate op; (2) `boards(ids:) {
items_page(query_params:) }` is the cross-board fan-
out surface (different shape from v0.1's singular
`items_page_by_column_values`); (3) inaccessible
board IDs silently omitted by `boards(ids:)`,
requiring an `inaccessible_boards` warning to prevent
silent partial results; (4) per-board column
resolution required — a column token that doesn't
resolve on ONE board errors the whole cross-board
fan-out; (5) wall-clock latency (not complexity
budget) is the cross-board cap constraint; (6)
`Query.favorites` returns empty array for accounts
with no favorites (vs null or error). M23 design
pivoted across all six findings inline.

**Fourth fire (M24-prep / Decision 2 closure,
`a1f3025`).** Single probe script
(`scripts/probe/m24-history-kinds.ts`, gitignored)
introspecting `ActivityLogType` + `Update` + `Reply`
+ walking `activity_logs(item_ids:)` on a production
board produced three load-bearing findings closing
Decision 2: (1) schema field name is `event`, NOT
`kind` (Monday's `ActivityLogType.event` slot maps
1:1 to the CLI's `kind` discriminator); (2) the
observed event taxonomy from production data (6
distinct wire events across 19 rows; the only
item-scoped kind is `update_column_value`); (3)
`entity` discriminates item-scoped (`pulse`) from
board-scoped (`board`) events — the `item_ids`
filter alone is INSUFFICIENT, walker MUST filter
`entity = 'pulse'`. Plus a side-finding: Monday's
`activity_logs` has empirically-measured >30s
propagation lag on freshly-edited boards (caveat
documented at cli-design §13 v0.3 entry). M24
pre-flight contract diff (`bad98ba`) implemented
the ratified shape.

The discipline now holds at four pre-flights; the
watch-item stays as per-pre-flight discipline. Next
fire is M27 webhooks pre-flight (per §22 "Likely
affected milestones").

### R-candidate — `scripts/probe/` reusable probe infrastructure

Surfaced by the M20 implementation pivot. Four ad-hoc
probe scripts created during the session
(`probe-bootstrap.ts`, `probe-time-tracking.ts`,
`probe-cleanup.ts`, `probe-introspect.ts`) shared a
common scaffold:

- `import 'dotenv/config'` — loads `MONDAY_API_TOKEN`
  from `.env` without echoing.
- A typed `gql(query, variables, operationName)`
  wrapper around `fetch('https://api.monday.com/v2',
  …)` with `Authorization` / `API-Version` /
  `Content-Type` headers and JSON parsing of the
  response body.
- An `expect(label, result)` helper that surfaces
  status / errors and exits non-zero on failure.
- Mutation-bootstrap helpers (`create_board` /
  `create_column` / `create_item` /
  `delete_board`).

The four scripts ranged from ~30 LOC (`probe-cleanup`)
to ~190 LOC (`probe-time-tracking`). All deleted
post-probe; `scripts/` was added to `.gitignore` in
the M20 close commit so future probe scripts stay
local by default.

**The lift.** A `scripts/probe/_lib.ts` (or similar)
exporting `gql` + `expect` + bootstrap-mutation
helpers + cleanup-mutation helpers. Per-probe
scripts then thin to ~30–50 LOC each (the actual
mutation matrix being probed). Naming convention:
`scripts/probe/<milestone>-<surface>.ts` with a
shared `<milestone>-bootstrap.ts` + `<milestone>-
cleanup.ts` triad pattern.

**Trigger.** R-candidate, not yet a watch-item.
Fires when a third probe scenario lands. Likely
candidates per the empirical-probe-pre-flight
discipline above:
- M21 OAuth flow probe.
- M27 webhook flow probe.
- M28 subitem hierarchy probe.

**Risk:** low — the probe scripts are
infrastructure for development, not shipped code.
The lift itself is a few hours' work; the
maintenance burden is minimal because the scripts
live in `scripts/` (gitignored, so out-of-band of
the test pyramid).

**When to schedule:** **fired at M21 pre-flight
`5c07840`.** The R-candidate's second consumer
landed: M21's OAuth probe (`scripts/probe/m21-oauth.ts`,
gitignored) shared the same dotenv + fetch + GraphQL +
JSON-error scaffold as the four ad-hoc M20 probes,
crossing the lift threshold. `scripts/probe/_lib.ts`
shipped under a one-off `.gitignore` exclusion
(`scripts/*` + `scripts/*/*` excluded; `!scripts/probe/`
+ `!scripts/probe/_lib.ts` re-included) so per-probe
scripts stay local while the shared scaffold versions.
The lifted shape: `gql` + `expect` + `rawGet` /
`rawPostForm` (low-level HTTPS helpers for OAuth
endpoint probing where `fetch`'s response massaging
isn't useful) + `printRaw` (truncating raw-response
formatter). M21's probe matrix is ~150 LOC end-to-end
(would have been ~250+ without the lift). Future
consumers per the empirical-probe-pre-flight
discipline: M27 webhook flow probe, M28 subitem
hierarchy probe.

### R-candidate — secure-file primitive shared between cache + credentials

Surfaced by the M21 pre-pre-flight `3eba714` (cli-design
§7.4 credentials cache amendment). §7.4.2 explicitly
mirrors `src/api/cache.ts`'s `writeJsonFile` *verbatim*
— `writeFile(tmpPath, payload, { mode: 0o600 })` →
`chmod(tmpPath, 0o600)` (re-applied because `writeFile`'s
`mode` is advisory under umask) → `rename(tmpPath,
finalPath)` (atomic on the same filesystem); plus the
read-time `fs.fstat`-against-open-descriptor permission
check (`(stats.mode & 0o077) !== 0`) the
`.claude/rules/security.md` rule pins. The cache module
already exports `CACHE_FILE_MODE` for the per-call-site
constant; the lift would generalise the file primitive
itself.

**Sites at M21 pre-pre-flight (1 active + 1 pending):**
`src/api/cache.ts` (4 cache file types: boards / users /
account_tags / schema-version) is the existing consumer;
M21's `src/config/credentials.ts` lands as the second
consumer at the M21 pre-flight contract diff. Below the
project's R-class 3-consumer trigger threshold, but
flagged here so the lift is on the radar.

**Proposed shape.** A `src/utils/secure-file.ts` (or
`src/utils/atomic-replace.ts` or similar) exporting
`writeJsonFileSecure(finalPath, payload, mode?)` +
`readJsonFileSecure(finalPath, mode?)` + the
`SECURE_FILE_MODE` constant (default `0o600`). Both cache
+ credentials would adopt; cache's existing
`CACHE_FILE_MODE` re-export becomes a thin alias for
`SECURE_FILE_MODE`. The current `writeJsonFile` body
in `src/api/cache.ts` stays as the reference
implementation post-lift to ease byte-identical-tests
verification of the consumer-side behaviour.

**Trigger.** **3-consumer threshold fires when a third
secure-file consumer lands.** Likely candidates:
- Future `monday config export` / debug-bundle verb (v0.4
  candidate, no current milestone).
- A future profile-state cache slot that's not currently
  scoped (e.g., per-profile `last_used_at` for
  `monday auth list-profiles` — speculative, no
  decision).
- A future `~/.monday-cli/` migration tool (e.g., for
  schema_version bumps in v0.4+).

If none of those land within v0.3 / v0.4, the candidate
stays open without firing.

**Risk:** low — pure consolidation, byte-identical tests
catch per-consumer drift. The consolidation is also
load-bearing for security review: the redaction +
mode-`0600` discipline lives in one module rather than
N, so a future security audit has one surface to audit
rather than per-consumer drift across `cache.ts` /
`credentials.ts` / future debug-bundle.

**When to schedule:** **deferred — fire on third
consumer.** Below threshold today; flagged here so the
M21 implementation doesn't accidentally diverge from
`cache.ts`'s pattern (the §7.4.2 "mirror verbatim"
wording is the in-design backstop until the lift fires).

### R-watch-item — `__test_oauth_helper` as a generalised "headless flow" test seam

Surfaced by the M21 pre-pre-flight `3eba714` (cli-design
§7.3.4 mock OAuth helper design). The
`__test_oauth_helper` env var introduces a NEW test-seam
pattern distinct from `FixtureTransport` (the
network-boundary test seam) and from `setupConfigEnv`
(the env-var test seam). The new seam **bypasses the
browser-open + listener steps** of a flow whose entire
shape is "open external thing, wait for it to call back,
then continue" — using a fixture file + `force_*` flags
to drive both the happy path and the local-side failure
branches.

**Single consumer at M21:** `monday auth login`. No
other current verb has the "wait for an external
callback" shape that the helper bypasses.

**Watch-item, not a code refactor.** Process item — the
generalised "headless flow test seam" pattern doesn't
need a code lift today, but the pattern itself is worth
remembering so a future flow that needs the same
bypass-and-replay shape doesn't reinvent the
mechanics. Specifically:

- A fixture file at a path supplied via env var.
- The CLI generates its own per-attempt secrets (state
  / port / verifier) per the production code path.
- The helper synthesises the external callback's payload
  using the CLI's own generated secrets so the
  verification step (CSRF / signature / etc.) passes by
  default.
- `force_*` boolean flags in the fixture exercise the
  local-side failure branches without going through the
  external boundary (the network-boundary failures are
  exercised via `FixtureTransport` cassettes, not via
  `force_*` flags).

**Likely future consumers (R-watch-item triggers re-
evaluation when any of these land):**
- **M27 `webhook create`** — if M27's design grows a
  "wait for Monday to confirm webhook signature
  registration" step that the test infrastructure needs
  to bypass. Currently unlikely (M27 webhooks are
  fire-and-forget creates with no confirmation
  callback), but worth checking at M27 pre-flight.
- **A future `monday auth status` / `monday auth list-
  profiles` verb** if it ever needs to probe the
  credentials-cache state with a similar fixture-driven
  shape (lower likelihood — the credentials cache is on
  disk, not behind a callback).
- **Any future v0.4+ flow that opens an external thing
  and waits for it** — `monday item watch` (polling,
  not callback — probably not), shell-completion-install
  flows (system-side, not async-callback), etc.

**Trigger.** Watch-item; per-milestone-pre-flight
re-evaluation rather than a code lift. Fires when a
second verb introduces the "wait for external callback"
shape.

**Risk:** low — the pattern is documented in §7.3.4
once and referenced from the milestone post-mortem; if
no second consumer ever materialises, the watch-item
quietly stays open without code-side debt.

**When to schedule:** **per-milestone-pre-flight
discipline, fires from M21 onwards.** The pre-flight
handoff template grows a "does this milestone introduce
a new external-callback flow?" item; if yes, the design
should explicitly compare against the §7.3.4 contract
and either reuse it (rename `__test_oauth_helper` →
`__test_external_helper` if/when a second consumer
arrives) or deviate with documented reasoning.

### R-candidate — `requireProfile` shared helper for `auth` verbs

Surfaced by the M21 pre-flight (`5c07840`).
`src/commands/auth/login.ts` and
`src/commands/auth/logout.ts` both:

1. Read `--profile` from the global-flag layer via
   `parseGlobalFlags(program.opts(), ctx.env).profile`.
2. Throw `UsageError` with a verb-specific message + a
   `details.hint` if the resolved profile is `undefined`
   or empty.
3. Pass the resolved profile name through their
   command's `inputSchema.parse({ profile })` so future
   tightening (e.g., disallowed characters) lives in
   one place.

Two consumers today; below the R-class 3-consumer
trigger threshold. The lift would be a
`requireProfile(program: Command, ctx: RunContext,
verbName: string): string` helper in
`src/commands/auth/_shared.ts` (or similar) returning
the resolved profile name and throwing the
`UsageError` itself.

**Trigger.** R-candidate, not yet a watch-item. Fires
when a third `auth` verb (`auth status`, `auth
list-profiles`, etc.) lands needing the same
profile-required-or-throw shape. Cli-design §13 v0.3
list does not currently include any third `auth` verb;
the candidate stays open for v0.3.x / v0.4.

**Risk:** low — pure code consolidation. Verb-specific
error messages (`auth login` says "the credentials
cache is per-profile so the verb cannot infer a
target"; `auth logout` says "name the profile whose
credentials cache entry should be deleted") would
either fold into a `verbHint?: string` parameter or
move into the helper's per-verb dispatch table.

**When to schedule:** **deferred — fire on third
consumer.** Below threshold today; flagged here so a
future `auth status` / `auth list-profiles` PR doesn't
silently re-implement the pattern.

### R-NEW-1 — `isENOENT` filesystem helper. Shipped: `1c77699` (M21 close).

Surfaced at M21 Part 1 close. The 5-line helper —

```ts
const isENOENT = (err: unknown): boolean => {
  if (typeof err !== 'object' || err === null) {
    return false;
  }
  return (err as { code?: unknown }).code === 'ENOENT';
};
```

— was duplicated verbatim in `src/api/cache.ts`,
`src/config/credentials.ts`, and `src/config/profiles.ts`.
3-consumer trigger fired at M21 close (mirrors R45 / R48 /
R51's "lift when a third consumer lands" cadence). Lifted
to `src/utils/fs.ts` as the shared module's single export;
three consumers updated to import from the new location.
Behaviour-preserving; no caller-visible delta. 7 new unit
tests in `tests/unit/utils/fs.test.ts` pin the contract
(ENOENT-on-Error true, non-ENOENT false, null /
undefined / string / no-code false, hand-shaped object
literal with `code: "ENOENT"` true).

**Risk:** low — pure consolidation. Mechanical lift; no
test changes elsewhere needed.

Surfaced at M21 Part 1 close. The 6-line helper —

```ts
const credentialsHomeOptions = (
  ctx: RunContext,

  const home = ctx.env.HOME;
```

— is duplicated verbatim in `src/commands/auth/login.ts`
and `src/commands/auth/logout.ts`. 2 consumers — below the
