**Trigger.** **Third consumer fires at `monday auth
status`** (v0.3.x candidate per cli-design §13 — auth
status would need the same shape to read credentials for a
profile-status probe). Lift then to
`src/commands/auth/_shared.ts` (or co-locate with
R-candidate `requireProfile`'s eventual home if both lifts
happen together).

**Risk:** low — pure boilerplate consolidation, identical
to R-NEW-1's shape.

**When to schedule:** **deferred — fire on third
consumer.** Pairs naturally with the R-candidate
`requireProfile` lift if both fire in the same `auth
status` PR.

### R-NEW-3 candidate — `wrapFsError` / `wrapAsConfigError` factory

Surfaced at M21 Part 1 close. Two named consumers + two
inline sites share the same error-wrapping shape:

```ts
const cause = err instanceof Error ? err : new Error(String(err));
return new ErrorClass(message, { cause, details });
```

- `src/api/cache.ts wrapFsError` — returns `CacheError`.
- `src/config/credentials.ts wrapAsConfigError` — returns
  `ConfigError`.
- `src/config/profiles.ts loadProfilesConfig` inlines the
  same pattern at two sites (TOML read failure + schema
  failure paths).

Different error classes but identical guard shape.

**Trigger.** **M22 may pull the trigger** if `monday
status`'s per-probe error wrapping (DNS / TCP / TLS /
401 probes each surface their own typed errors) adds a
third NAMED consumer. The factory shape:

```ts
const wrapFsError = <E extends MondayCliError>(
  ErrorClass: new (msg: string, opts: { cause: Error; details: Record<string, unknown> }) => E,
  err: unknown,
  message: string,
  details: Readonly<Record<string, unknown>> = {},
): E => {
  const cause = err instanceof Error ? err : new Error(String(err));
  return new ErrorClass(message, { cause, details });
};
```

Lives in `src/utils/errors.ts` (or `src/utils/fs.ts`
alongside `isENOENT` — depends on whether the factory is
fs-specific or general error-wrapping).

**Risk:** medium — generic factory constraints across
distinct error-class shapes. The current per-module
helpers diverge on `ErrorClass` constructor signature
expectations; the factory needs a constrained generic
that's tight enough to catch shape drift.

**When to schedule:** **deferred — fire on third NAMED
consumer.** Inline call-sites don't count toward the
trigger (they could be converted to use the existing
local helper first); the factory lift fires when a third
MODULE needs its own wrapper.

**M22 close status (post-`3a1b465`).** R-NEW-3 did NOT
fire at M22 implementation. The cache_writability probe in
`src/api/probes.ts` handles fs errors inline by converting
them to `ProbeFail` shapes (returning `{kind: 'fail',
reason: 'dir_missing' | 'mode_insecure' | ...}`) rather
than throwing typed CLI errors. The pattern doesn't match
the `wrapAsConfigError` / `wrapFsError` factory cadence —
those wrappers produce throwable typed errors with `cause`
+ `details`. The probe layer's contract is "return a result
shape, not throw", so the same fs-error narrowing logic
applies but the wrapping pattern differs. R-NEW-3 carries
forward; trigger remains "third NAMED consumer of a typed-
CLI-error fs-wrap factory shape" (currently 2: cache.ts +
credentials.ts).

### R-watch-item — `vi.stubGlobal('fetch', mockFetch)` URL-include boundary mock

Surfaced at M21 Part 1 close. The OAuth-flow tests in
`tests/integration/commands/auth.test.ts` stub `fetch`
globally with a per-URL-discriminating handler:

```ts
const fetchStub = vi.fn((input: RequestInfo | URL): Promise<Response> => {
  const url = typeof input === 'string'
    ? input
    : input instanceof URL ? input.toString() : input.url;
  if (url.includes('/oauth2/token')) {
    return Promise.resolve(new Response(...));
  }
  return Promise.reject(new Error(`unexpected fetch call to ${url}`));
});
vi.stubGlobal('fetch', fetchStub);
```

This is the first appearance of the pattern in the test
suite — the existing transport-mock pattern
(`FixtureTransport` cassettes) doesn't cover code that
bypasses the SDK transport (the OAuth token exchange runs
through `node:fetch` directly because it's not a Monday
GraphQL call).

**Likely future consumers:**
- **M22 `monday status` probes** (DNS / TCP / TLS / 401)
  — second consumer.
- **M27 webhook delivery probes** — third consumer.
- Any v0.4+ verb that talks to a non-GraphQL endpoint
  (Monday's REST surfaces, webhook callbacks).

**Trigger (R-class lift).** When the third NAMED test
file uses the pattern, lift to
`tests/fixtures/fetch-stub.ts` exporting a
`createFetchStub(routes: Record<string, ResponseFactory>)`
helper that wraps `vi.stubGlobal('fetch', ...)` + the
URL-discriminating dispatch + the
`vi.unstubAllGlobals()` afterEach.

**When to log it as a candidate.** Now (M21 Part 2 close)
as a watch-item — promote to R-candidate when the second
consumer (M22) lands.

### R-watch-item — Post-OAuth single-shot GraphQL via fresh-transport

Surfaced at M21 Part 1 close, ratified at M21 Part 2
Codex round-1 (P1 finding promoted the discipline to a
contract concern). `src/commands/auth/login.ts:fetchAccountId`
needs to query `account { id }` with a freshly-issued
OAuth token BEFORE that token lands in
`ctx.env.MONDAY_API_TOKEN`. The current implementation
builds a one-off `FetchTransport` + `MondayClient` for
the single query and **pushes the token into
`ctx.runtimeSecrets` first** so the value-scan
redaction layer can scrub it if the wire response echoes
the Authorization header back (M21 Part 2 Codex round-1
P1 fix).

**Likely future consumers (R-watch-item triggers
re-evaluation):**
- **`monday auth refresh` (v0.4+)** — needs to query a
  validity-probe endpoint with the refreshed token
  before persisting it.
- **`monday auth status`** — if it does a wire probe
  rather than just returning local-state info, same
  fresh-token-before-env-injection shape.
- **`dev login` shortcut** (M26 candidate) — short-form
  for `auth login` that may also exercise the post-
  exchange probe.

**Lift target (when 3 consumers).** A
`postOAuthFreshTransportProbe(ctx, accessToken, op)`
helper in `src/api/oauth.ts` (or a sibling module)
encapsulating: push token to runtimeSecrets, build
transport, build client, run `client.raw(op)`,
return parsed response.

**When to log it as a candidate.** Now (M21 Part 2
close); promote to R-candidate on the second consumer.

### R-watch-item — `/* c8 ignore */` vs v8 branch-coverage friction (tooling)

Surfaced at M21 Part 1 close. v8's branch indicator on
`if` / `?:` lines still counts the branch against the
coverage percentage even when the statements are
ignored. The result: defensive code marked
`/* c8 ignore */` doesn't show up in statement / line
coverage but DOES drag down the branches percentage.
This forced the M21 Part 1 floor adjustment from 95.45
to 94.5 (the new ~600 LOC of boundary-shaped code had
many production-only branches that resist unit-test
reproduction).

**Two recovery paths if it becomes load-bearing:**
1. **Switch coverage provider to istanbul** (vitest
   supports both). Istanbul's branch indicator honours
   the `/* istanbul ignore */` directives more
   consistently than v8 does for `c8 ignore`.
2. **Audit + add `vitest.config.ts` exclusion list**
   for known-defensive line ranges. Less surgical;
   risks hiding real coverage gaps.

**Recovered without a provider switch at `7058754`**
(post-M21 coverage-push session, before M22 pre-flight).
Path taken: seven targeted tests + folding ten broken
multi-line `/* c8 ignore next N */` directives into the
two-line form per testing.md's "c8 ignore syntax gotcha"
subsection. Measured project-wide branches at recovery:
**95.56% with 0.11pp margin** above the restored 95.45
floor.

**Second-order finding (the recovery's load-bearing
discovery):** v8 silently fails to recognise
`/* c8 ignore next N */` when the directive's comment
block spans multiple lines. The M21 Part 1 LOC landed
several multi-line directives that *looked* correct but
v8 did not honour, leaving production-only branches
counted against the percentage. Folding those directives
into single-line `/* c8 ignore next N */` + separate
explanation comment OR — preferred for blocks of
statements — `/* c8 ignore start */` / `/* c8 ignore
stop */` block-wraps fixed the recognition.

**Project default after recovery:** the
`c8 ignore start/stop` block-wrap is now the preferred
form for production-only conditional branches. Reasoning:
the single-line `/* c8 ignore next N */` form has off-
by-one drift in `N` when surrounding code is added or
removed, and a stale `N` silently un-ignores statements
that should still be ignored. The block-wrap form is
self-delimiting and survives refactors. Single-line
`/* c8 ignore next */` (no `N`) on a single line stays
fine for one-line ignores. The testing.md subsection has
been updated to document this.

**Watch-item stays OPEN** on tooling grounds. The
istanbul-provider swap remains a viable alternative if
recovery becomes recurrent (e.g., a future milestone
introduces another large boundary-shaped LOC drop and
the `c8 ignore start/stop` discipline isn't enough).
Promote to closed-decision if the project ratchets the
floor again without provider changes — at that point
the friction is purely a discipline matter, not a
tooling watch-item.

### R-watch-item — Filesystem-state probes for non-ENOENT fs error branches (test pattern)

Surfaced during the post-M21 coverage-push (`7058754`).
Many fs-touching modules carry catch-paths shaped like
`if (isENOENT(err)) { return undefined; } throw
wrapAsConfigError(err, ...)`. The non-ENOENT arm's
historical wisdom was "platform-specific and not
reproducible from a tmp-dir test" → covered by
`/* c8 ignore */`. The recovery session found the
non-ENOENT arm of `readCredentials` reachable from a
plain unit test by a filesystem-state setup trick:
**create a directory at the path where the file is
expected**, so `open(path, O_RDONLY)` rejects with
EISDIR rather than ENOENT and the catch-path's
`throw wrapAsConfigError(...)` arm fires. The probe
needs no mock and runs cross-platform (POSIX + Windows
both reject `open()` on a directory):

```ts
const credPath = resolveCredentialsPath({ home });
await mkdir(join(home, CREDENTIALS_DIR_NAME), { recursive: true });
await mkdir(credPath, { recursive: true });
await expect(readCredentials({ home })).rejects.toBeInstanceOf(ConfigError);
```

**Likely future consumers:**

- `src/config/profiles.ts:loadProfilesConfig` non-ENOENT
  `readFile` arm (currently `c8 ignore start/stop`-wrapped
  per the post-M21 recovery; convertible to a real test
  via the same EISDIR-via-dir-at-path probe).
- `src/api/cache.ts:readEntry` non-ENOENT path (mirrors
  the credentials shape verbatim — readEntry → JSON.parse
  → schema validate, with the same isENOENT guard).
- M22 `src/api/probes.ts` filesystem-touching probes (if
  any land — TBD per M22 pre-flight).

**Sibling shapes in the same family:**

- **ENOTDIR-via-file-at-dir-component**: create a file at
  a path that should be a parent dir, so `mkdir(child, {
  recursive: true })` fails with ENOTDIR. Useful for
  `ensureSecureDir`'s catch-path.
- **EACCES-via-chmod-0000**: drop permissions on a parent
  dir; works on POSIX, Windows behaviour differs. Avoid
  if cross-platform CI matters.
- **EROFS / ENOSPC**: not reachable from a tmp-dir test.
  These remain genuinely production-only.

**When to log it as a candidate.** Now (post-M21
coverage-push close) as a watch-item. The pattern is
self-contained per call-site and doesn't warrant a
shared helper at this scale; if a fourth consumer lands
in v0.3.x or v0.4 (e.g., M22 probes module touches the
fs), evaluate a `tests/fixtures/fs-state-probes.ts`
helper exporting `seedDirAtPath` / `seedFileAtDirPath`
/ `seedReadOnlyDir`. Until then, individual tests stay
inline — three lines of setup is below the lift
threshold.

### R-NEW-4 — `statusOutputSchema` duplication lift. Shipped: `0b5af57` (post-M22 pre-flight drift sweep).

Surfaced at M22 pre-flight close. The
`statusOutputSchema` + `probeResultEnvelopeSchema`
definitions were duplicated character-for-character
between `src/api/probes.ts` and `src/commands/status.ts`
— a clear lift signal. The duplication arose because
the pre-flight diff was written module-first (api/probes
.ts) and command-first (commands/status.ts) in parallel
without one importing from the other.

The post-pre-flight drift sweep removed the duplication:
`src/commands/status.ts` now imports `statusOutputSchema`
+ `StatusOutput` from `src/api/probes.ts`. The
`StatusOutput` type alias in api/probes.ts was also
flipped from a hand-written `interface` to
`z.infer<typeof statusOutputSchema>` per
`.claude/rules/validation.md` "schema-driven types — define
the schema, derive the type" — stops the two from drifting
when a future schema tightening forgets to also update the
interface.

**Risk:** low — pure consolidation. The unit tests that
imported `statusOutputSchema` from api/probes.ts continue
to validate the same schema; the command's `outputSchema`
in the `CommandModule` shape now references the api/probes
.ts export. No caller-visible delta. Coverage unchanged
at 99.12 / 95.57 / 99.36 / 99.26.

**Why the lift fired immediately rather than at M22
implementation.** The drift sweep IS the lift trigger —
when a pre-flight + a command stub ship as separate-but-
identical schemas, the impl session is forced to either
re-import or re-implement, and the latter is the wrong
default. M21 followed the same pattern (the
`profileEntrySchema` lives in `src/config/credentials.ts`;
the auth commands import it, never redefine). M22's drift
sweep restored that pattern.

### R-NEW-5 — `introspectType()` helper in `scripts/probe/_lib.ts`. Shipped: post-M23 focused-refactor session.

Surfaced at M22 pre-flight. The seven probe scripts
(`scripts/probe/m22-*.ts`, gitignored) repeated a stable
introspection pattern across four of them:

```ts
const t = await gql<{
  __type: {
    name: string;
    description: string | null;
    fields: Array<{
      name: string;
      description: string | null;
      type: { name: string | null; kind: string; ofType: ... };
    }> | null;
  } | null;
}>(`query { __type(name: "${typeName}") { name description fields { name description type { name kind ofType { name kind } } } } }`);
```

— used by `m22-usage-extended.ts`, `m22-usage-daily.ts`,
`m22-usage-platform-api.ts`, `m22-usage-analytics.ts`,
and `m22-usage-by-day.ts`. Pretty-print helper
(`probeType(typeName)`) also recurs ~4 times. ~30 LOC
per consumer × 4 = ~120 LOC of duplication across the M22
probe matrix.

**Sites (4):** the four `m22-usage*-{extended,daily,
platform-api,analytics,by-day}.ts` scripts — well above
the R-class 3-consumer trigger, but the consumers are
**disposable + gitignored**, so the lift's value is in
reducing future-pre-flight probe-script LOC, not
preserving the M22 work.

**Trade-off.** The scripts are throwaway: each pre-flight
deletes them after the empirical findings bake into the
contract docstrings. Lifting `introspectType()` would
expand `scripts/probe/_lib.ts` (the one tracked file)
from ~217 LOC to ~280 LOC for a helper that's only used
by gitignored scripts. The lift is value-positive only
if future pre-flights re-use the helper enough to amortise
the carry.

**Trigger.** **Watch-item — fires at the next pre-flight
that needs type introspection.** Likely candidates:
- **M27 webhooks** — the `create_webhook` / `webhooks(board_id)`
  argument shapes + the `Webhook` type fields all need
  introspection at pre-flight to confirm the v0.3 envelope
  shape. The M22 introspection pattern transfers directly.
- **M28 multi-level subitems** — `create_subitem`'s
  argument shape + the `hierarchy_type` field's allowed
  values + the per-board-template subitem-depth
  constraints all need introspection.

If M27 OR M28 pre-flight ships 3+ introspecting probe
scripts, fold the helper into `_lib.ts`. Until then, copy
the pattern verbatim — the disposable nature of probe
scripts makes the per-pre-flight duplication tolerable.

**Risk:** low — the helper is pure (no I/O beyond the
shared `gql()` wrapper); a regression in introspection
output would surface during the pre-flight's own probe
matrix, not at runtime.

**Trigger update — fired at M23 pre-flight (2026-05-11).**
The M22 post-mortem prediction that "M23 doesn't need
introspection" was wrong: the M23 favorites + hierarchy
probes added FIVE new introspecting scripts —
`m23-favorites.ts` (`__type(name: "User")`,
`__type(name: "Board")`, `__type(name: "Query")`),
`m23-favorites-deep.ts` (`__schema { queryType { fields
... } }` deep walk + `__type(name: "Favorite*")` trial-
introspections), `m23-hierarchy-item.ts`
(`__type(name: "GraphqlHierarchyObjectItem")`),
`m23-hierarchy-object.ts`
(`__type(name: "HierarchyObjectID")`), and
`m23-monday-object-enum.ts`
(`__type(name: "GraphqlMondayObject")` enum-introspection).
**Combined with the M22 scripts that's 9+ consumers.**
The R-class 3-consumer trigger has fired and then some.

**Shipped: post-M23 focused-refactor session.** The
trigger fired at M23 pre-flight (`e7cbae5` documented
the 9+ consumers); a focused refactor session between
the M23 implementation close and M24 pre-flight kickoff
landed the lift. ~80 LOC additive into `_lib.ts`
(richer than the originally-estimated ~30 LOC: the
helper carries the union of every selection set the
9+ consumers used, so future pre-flights pay the
full-shape query once and pick the fields they need
without a second introspection call). Carry cost is
minimal — pure helper, no I/O beyond the shared `gql()`
wrapper. M24+M25+M26+M27+M28 pre-flights each save
1-3 introspection blocks of ~30 LOC = ~150 LOC of
probe-script terseness across the remaining v0.3
milestones.

**Final shape (in `scripts/probe/_lib.ts`):**

```ts
export interface IntrospectedTypeRef {
  readonly name: string | null;
  readonly kind: string;
  readonly ofType: { /* up to 3 levels deep */ } | null;
}
export interface IntrospectedField {
  readonly name: string;
  readonly description: string | null;
  readonly args: ReadonlyArray<{ name; description; type: IntrospectedTypeRef }>;
  readonly type: IntrospectedTypeRef;
}
export interface IntrospectedType {
  readonly name: string;
  readonly kind: string;
  readonly description: string | null;
  readonly fields: ReadonlyArray<IntrospectedField> | null;
  readonly enumValues: ReadonlyArray<{ name; description }> | null;
  readonly possibleTypes: ReadonlyArray<{ name: string }> | null;
}
export const introspectType = async (
  typeName: string,
): Promise<IntrospectedType | null> => { /* routes through gql + expect */ };
export const printIntrospected = (
  label: string,
  introspected: IntrospectedType | null,
): void => { /* mirrors the inline probeType/printFields shape */ };
```

The 3-level-deep `ofType` walk (matching the
`NON_NULL<LIST<NON_NULL<NamedType>>>` chain the M23
favorites-deep probe needed) covers every Monday
schema wrap shape. Variables-based query (`$name:
String!`) rather than string-interpolated — defensive
against future type names containing characters that
break inline GraphQL strings. Returns `null` for
"type not on schema" vs `process.exit(1)` via `expect`
for GraphQL errors — preserves the existing per-probe
contract.

**Test obligation:** none — `vitest.config.ts`
`coverage.include: ['src/**/*.ts']` excludes `scripts/`
from the coverage gate. The helper is consumed only by
gitignored probe scripts hitting the live Monday API;
implicit coverage comes from running probe matrices
during future pre-flights, not from the unit suite. The
`gql()` + `expect()` pair the helper composes have the
same disposable-scripts implicit-coverage contract.

**`introspectQueryRootFields()` deferred.** The
originally-scoped `__schema { queryType { fields { ... }
} }` walker (used once in `m23-favorites-deep.ts`) is
SINGLE-consumer today; lifting it would be premature.
Watch-item: fires at the 3rd consumer (likely M27 or
M28 pre-flight if they need root-field enumeration).

### R-NEW-6 — Codex pre-flight review prompt template. Shipped: post-M23 pre-flight round 1.

Surfaced at M22 pre-flight close. The three M22 Codex
review prompts (`.review-prompt.md`, rounds 1/2/3,
gitignored) shared a stable structure:

1. **Header** — `# Codex pre-flight review — vX.Y-MNN
   contract diff [ROUND N]`.
2. **Round-N findings + how they were addressed** —
   per-finding bullets with the round-1 P1/P2/P3 +
   per-finding fix description (round 2+ only).
3. **Scope of review** — file list with NEW / MODIFIED
   tags + test-count + coverage at gate.
4. **Empirical-probe findings the diff is built against**
   — verbatim probe findings with date + API version +
   load-bearing-finding flag.
5. **Audit points (pre-enumerated per M21 post-mortem
   lesson)** — 5-7 numbered watch-items specific to the
   milestone (redaction-runtime contract, additive-only
   envelope, stub discipline, etc.).
6. **Things explicitly OUT of scope for this review** —
   shipped-in-prior-milestones modules + future-milestone
   surfaces + disposable probe scripts.
7. **Output format** — `P1 / P2 / P3 with file + line cite;
   overall verdict in one sentence`.

The pattern repeated at M21 pre-flight + M22 pre-flight
(2 milestones × ~3 rounds each = 6 prompts of the same
shape). M23-M28 pre-flights will follow the same pattern;
the lift opportunity is a `.claude/templates/codex-pre-flight-review.md`
that future pre-flights start from rather than copying
section-by-section from prior `.review-prompt.md` files.

**Sites (6+):** M21 round 1 / M21 round 2 / M22 round 1 /
M22 round 2 / M22 round 3, plus the M19 + M20 pre-flight
reviews (older; same structure). Well above the R-class
3-consumer trigger.

**Trade-off.** The template would live at
`.claude/templates/codex-pre-flight-review.md` (a NEW
directory; the existing `.claude/rules/` is for
auto-loaded path-scoped agent rules, not templates).
Adding the directory + template is ~80 LOC of project
documentation that future pre-flights re-use; the carry
cost is the template-maintenance burden (any cli-design
shape change forces a template refresh).

**Trigger fired at M23 pre-flight round 1.** The round-1
prompt followed the same 7-section shape verbatim (header +
round-N findings + scope + empirical-probe + audit points +
out-of-scope + output format). Template extracted to
`.claude/templates/codex-pre-flight-review.md` post-M23
round 1; future pre-flights (M24+M25+M26+M27+M28) start from
the template rather than copying section-by-section from the
M21/M22/M23 `.review-prompt.md` archive. The template
documents which sections are template-stable
(1 header / 6 out-of-scope / 7 output format) vs
milestone-specific (3 scope / 4 empirical probes /
5 audit points) vs round-specific (2 prior-round findings).
Risk holds at low (documentation-only; regression would
surface at the next pre-flight as a Codex review-quality
drop vs the M22 baseline).

**Risk:** low — template is documentation; a regression
in the template surfaces at the next pre-flight when
Codex's review quality drops vs. the M22 baseline.

**Why not lift now at M22 close.** Without seeing M23's
pre-flight review prompt, the template's milestone-
specific sections (2 — round-N findings; 4 — empirical
probe; 5 — audit points) would be over-fitted to the M21
+ M22 shape. The lift is most valuable when one MORE
prompt confirms the structure is stable across three
milestones (M21 + M22 + M23).

**Why this is a watch-item, not a refactor candidate.**
The pattern improves test coverage without changing
production code. A shared helper would only marginally
reduce the per-test setup line count (three lines → one
call) and adds a dependency edge from every consumer
into a fixture module. The threshold to lift is whether
the pattern grows arms (different fs-error-shapes
beyond EISDIR), not just consumer count.

- `src/config/credentials.ts:142`


Three sites continue to call with the same `stats.mode`

stub didn't include the format-mode call sites — the

inside the `feat(m22)` commit because the lift is independent

### R-NEW-8 candidate — set-delta `missingByDifference` helper

Surfaced at M23 pre-flight (`1fefdb1`). Two warning-builders
now compute the same set-difference shape — "given a
requested set of IDs and a returned set, which requested
IDs are missing":

```ts
// src/api/cross-board-search.ts:428 (buildInaccessibleBoardsWarning)
const returnedSet = new Set(returnedIds);
const missing = requestedIds.filter((id) => !returnedSet.has(id));

// src/api/board-favorites.ts:303 (buildStaleFavoritesWarning)
const hydratedSet = new Set(hydratedIds);
const missing = favoritedIds.filter((id) => !hydratedSet.has(id));
```

Both are 2-line, deterministic-order set-differences. The
order-preservation matters for cassette stability (`missing`
follows the input-list order, not Set iteration order).
Both producers feed a warning's `details.missing_*_ids`
slot.

**Sites (2):** `src/api/cross-board-search.ts` +
`src/api/board-favorites.ts`. Below the R-class 3-consumer
trigger.

**Trigger.** **Watch-item — fires when a third producer
needs the same set-difference shape.** M24 item history
projector might compute "events present in `activity_logs`
but missing from `updates`" or similar; M25 partial-success
bulk surfaces "items requested vs items mutated" deltas.
If either lands an inline set-difference, lift to
`src/utils/sets.ts` (a new utility-module home — `utils/`
has `fs.ts` for fs helpers; `sets.ts` would be the
collection-helper home).

**Recommended shape:**

```ts
// src/utils/sets.ts (new module if lifted)
export const missingByDifference = (
  requested: readonly string[],
  returned: readonly string[],
): readonly string[] => {
  const returnedSet = new Set(returned);
  return requested.filter((id) => !returnedSet.has(id));
};
```

**Priority: LOW — watch-item until the 3rd consumer surfaces.**
Both current sites are simple 2-line filters; lifting now
would create a new utility module home for marginal gain.
The pattern is so trivial (3 lines including the Set
construction) that copy-paste is tolerable; the lift becomes
valuable only when the carry cost amortises across 3+
producers + future fan-out shapes.

**Risk:** trivial. Pure helper, no behaviour change. The
order-preservation contract (input order, not Set order) is
the only behavioural invariant; lift must preserve it.

### R-NEW-9 watch-item — 2-stage GraphQL filter+hydrate resolver shape

Surfaced at M23 pre-flight (`1fefdb1`). The M22 + M23
diagnostics + favorites verbs share a "2-stage
GraphQL filter+hydrate" shape:

- **M22 `monday usage`** (`src/api/usage.ts`):
  Stage 1 — `platform_api.daily_limit` query.
  Stage 2 — `platform_api.daily_analytics.by_day` (same
  call as Stage 1 in this case via the combined query;
  conceptually 2-stage but Monday surfaces both in one
  request, so the "stages" collapsed at implementation).
- **M23 `monday board favorites`** (`src/api/board-favorites.ts`):
  Stage 1 — `Query.favorites` → filter to
  `object.type === Board`.
  Stage 2 — `boards(ids: [filtered])` → hydrate name +
  workspace_id + state + url.
  Stage-1/Stage-2 count delta → `board_favorites_stale`
  warning.
- **M24 `item history`** (planned per §3 M24 + §8
  Decision 2): Stage 1 — `activity_logs(...)` page-walked.
  Stage 2 — `updates(...)` cursor-walked.
  Merge sort by `created_at` ascending across both
  sources.

**Sites (2 confirmed; STAYS at 2 post-M24 + post-M25
pre-flight):** M22 (close shape) + M23 (Stage-1 filter +
Stage-2 hydrate + count-delta warning). M24 `item history`
shipped at `d058172` as a TWO-SOURCE CHRONOLOGICAL MERGE
(`activity_logs` page-walk + `updates` page-walk + merge by
`created_at`) — a DIFFERENT shape than filter+hydrate (no
Stage-1 filter; no Stage-2 hydrate by id). The
M24-prep empirical probe at `a1f3025` (Decision 2 closure)
ratified the merge shape ahead of impl. M25 `item update
--continue-on-error` shipped at pre-flight `d5839a9` as a
PER-ITEM DISPATCH LOOP (fan out one `change_*_column_value`
mutation per matched item) — also NOT filter+hydrate. The
planned-third-consumer slot M23 close projected onto M24
never materialised; the next plausible filter+hydrate fire
is M27 webhooks if `webhook list --workspace <wid>`
filter+hydrates webhook IDs to verbose webhook records.

**Trajectory.** The R-class lift candidate is a
`twoStageGraphQLResolver` shape:

```ts
// hypothetical src/api/two-stage-resolver.ts (lifted form)
interface TwoStageResolverConfig<S1Raw, S1Filtered, S2Raw, Out> {
  readonly stage1: { query: string; vars?: ...; parse: ZodSchema<S1Raw>; filter: (raw: S1Raw) => readonly S1Filtered[] };
  readonly stage2: { query: string; vars: (s1: readonly S1Filtered[]) => ...; parse: ZodSchema<S2Raw>; };
  readonly join: (s1: readonly S1Filtered[], s2: S2Raw) => readonly Out[];
  readonly buildStaleWarning?: (s1: readonly S1Filtered[], s2: S2Raw) => StaleWarning | null;
}
```

But: each 2-stage today has custom filter / join / warning
logic. The lift's value is in the SCAFFOLDING (parse + I/O
sequencing + Stage-1/Stage-2 count-delta detection), not the
projection logic.

**Priority: LOW — STAYS at 2 consumers; re-evaluate at
M27 webhooks impl if `webhook list --workspace <wid>`
materialises as a filter+hydrate.** With only 2 confirmed
consumers and the two future candidates (M24, M25)
both diverging away from filter+hydrate, the lift would be
over-fitted to the existing two consumers. The
"empirical-probe-step in pre-flight" R-watch-item should
continue to force M27 to probe the wire shape upfront so
the resolver-shape decision rides with empirical findings.

**Risk:** medium — premature abstraction is the failure
mode here. The M22 `usage` and M23 `favorites` resolvers
look superficially similar but differ in load-bearing
ways (M22's "Stage 1" + "Stage 2" are colocated in one
GraphQL call; M23's are genuinely two round-trips). A
lifted helper that assumed two-call sequencing would
force M22 to refactor for a marginal gain.

### R-NEW-10 watch-item — stable warning-builder factory

Surfaced at M23 pre-flight (`1fefdb1`). Six warning-builder
helpers now follow the same shape:

```ts
export const buildXWarning = (
  ...inputs
): TypedWarning => ({
  code: '<stable_warning_code>',
  message: `<rendered english>`,
  details: { ...structured_fields, hint: '...' },
});
```

**Sites (6):** `buildCapWarning` (walk-pages.ts, v0.1),
`buildNoncanonicalWarning` (board/column-create.ts, M16),
`buildInaccessibleBoardsWarning` +
`buildColumnNotFoundOnBoardWarning` +
`buildCrossBoardTruncatedWarning` (cross-board-search.ts,
M23), `buildStaleFavoritesWarning` (board-favorites.ts,
M23). Well above the R-class 3-consumer trigger
numerically.

**Why this isn't a lift candidate (yet).** The "shape" is
SO trivial (a return statement projecting structured
fields into `{code, message, details}`) that a lifted
factory would marginally reduce the per-warning line count
(8-12 lines → 4-6 lines) but require every caller to import
+ name the factory. The per-warning shape currently
self-documents — reading `buildInaccessibleBoardsWarning`'s
body shows the warning's full surface inline. A lifted
factory would hide the message + hint behind a config
object, harder to grep.

**Trigger.** **Watch-item — fires if a future milestone
ships 3+ warning-builders with IDENTICAL `details` shapes
(not just identical CODE+MESSAGE+DETAILS triple-shape).**
For example, if M24 history projector ships
`buildEventsTruncatedWarning` + `buildMergeStaleWarning`
+ `buildKindUnknownWarning` and all three share the same
`details: { reason, count, hint }` shape, then a lifted
`buildPartialResultWarning(code, reason, count, hint)`
factory becomes value-positive. Until the inner-details
shapes converge, the lift is over-engineering.

**Priority: LOW — re-evaluate at M24 implementation
close.** Watch-item; do not lift speculatively.

### R-NEW-11 watch-item — pre-flight stub factory

Surfaced at M23 pre-flight (`1fefdb1`). Every milestone
pre-flight ships a near-identical
`Promise.reject(internal_error)` stub at each API-layer +
command-action surface (M19 `tag-directory.ts` +
`board-relation-validation.ts`; M20 `time-tracking.ts` +
`item/time-track/{start,stop}.ts`; M21
`oauth.ts`/`credentials.ts`/`profiles.ts`/`auth/{login,logout}.ts`;
M22 `probes.ts`/`usage.ts`/`commands/{status,usage}.ts`;
M23 `cross-board-search.ts`/`board-favorites.ts`/
`commands/board/favorites.ts`). The shape is:

```ts
const stubReject = (): Promise<T> =>
  Promise.reject(
    new ApiError(
      'internal_error',
      `<surface> is a v0.X-MNN pre-flight stub — runtime ... lands at MNN implementation.`,
      { details: { hint: 'MNN implementation lands ...' } },
    ),
  );
```

Plus the command-action variant (throw vs Promise.reject;
identical envelope shape).

**Sites (15+ across M19-M23 pre-flights):** every shipped
or pending pre-flight has 2-4 stub sites. The pattern is
extremely consistent.

**Why this isn't a lift candidate.** Each stub's
*surface name* + *milestone tag* + *details.hint* are
load-bearing — they tell agents which milestone owns the
surface, what the runtime will do, and how to work around
the stub today (e.g., M23's "supply --board <bid> to use
the v0.1 single-board path" hint). A lifted factory like
`preFlightStub('M23', 'monday board favorites', { hint:
'...' })` would marginally reduce per-stub LOC (~6 lines →
~3 lines) but the hint is the load-bearing payload; the
factory just wraps the boilerplate. Most stubs already use
~3-4 lines of actual surface-specific content.

**Trigger.** **Watch-item — fires if a future milestone's
pre-flight ships 5+ stub sites all sharing identical hint
shapes.** M27 webhooks (`webhook list/create/delete` =
3 verbs + 1-2 src/api modules) and M28 multi-level
subitems are the likely candidates. Until then, copy the
pattern verbatim — the per-stub variation is the value, not
the structure.

**Priority: LOW — watch-item; do not lift speculatively.**

  — THREE sites in one file, fired the 3-consumer trigger

- `src/api/raw-write.ts:125, :134` (M8, 2026-05-02 — TWO
  sites)

- `src/commands/board/column-create.ts:338` (M16)

Plus `src/api/probes.ts:335` defines a LOCAL `errorMessage`
function — the M22 developer needed the helper and wrote it
locally rather than discovering the existing 9+ inline sites

**Trigger fired at M6 (2026-05-01, `504dfd6`)** — exactly 11
days before this session's audit. Missed by every milestone

(where MondayCliError / ApiError / ConfigError already live).
Replace all 11 inline sites + the local `src/api/probes.ts`

change.

### R-NEW-16 — `errorCode(err)` helper. **Shipped: `5a7c88d`** (post-M23 audit). At-the-trigger threshold at M22.

Surfaced at post-M23 refactor-opportunity audit. Lower-volume
but adjacent to R-NEW-1's `isENOENT` lift — extracts the
Node `err.code` property in a type-safe way:

```ts
const code = (err as { code?: unknown }).code;
// typeof code === 'string' ? code : undefined
```

**Sites (3 occurrences):**

- `src/utils/fs.ts:29` (R-NEW-1's `isENOENT` — calls
  `(err as { code?: unknown }).code === 'ENOENT'`)
- `src/api/probes.ts:331` (M22 local helper — returns
  `string | undefined`)
- `src/api/transport.ts:198` (extracts the code for the
  errors.ts mapping)

**Trigger at the 3-consumer threshold.** Lift would
generalise R-NEW-1's `isENOENT` into a more general
`errorCode(err): string | undefined` + keep `isENOENT` as
a one-liner on top:

```ts
// src/utils/errors.ts (or src/utils/fs.ts — debatable home)
export const errorCode = (err: unknown): string | undefined => {
  if (typeof err !== 'object' || err === null) return undefined;
  const code = (err as { code?: unknown }).code;
  return typeof code === 'string' ? code : undefined;
};

// src/utils/fs.ts (existing R-NEW-1 lift refactored)
import { errorCode } from './errors.js';
export const isENOENT = (err: unknown): boolean =>
  errorCode(err) === 'ENOENT';
```

**Priority: MEDIUM — ship alongside R-NEW-14 + R-NEW-15.**
The lift is bigger-than-trivial because it touches the
existing R-NEW-1 lift's home (`utils/fs.ts`). The
2-character path swap (`fs.ts` → `errors.ts`) is mechanical;
the rationale (errorCode is general; isENOENT is the
ENOENT-specific specialisation) holds.

**Risk:** low. `isENOENT`'s callers see the same boolean
return; `errorCode` is additive.

Surfaced at M23 pre-flight round-2 (`fa27b60`). Codex
P1-1 introduced the registry-facing `outputSchema` for
`monday item search` as
`z.union([itemSearchOutputSchema,
crossBoardSearchOutputSchema])` — covers both the v0.1
single-board projection and the v0.3 cross-board projection
in one schema so `monday schema item.search` reflects the
runtime output across both branches.

**Sites (1):** `item/search.ts`'s
`itemSearchCommandOutputSchema` is the first consumer.

**Trigger.** **Watch-item — fires when a 2nd + 3rd command
needs a cross-cutting v0.3/v0.4 output-shape extension that
preserves the v0.1 surface.** Likely candidates:
- ~~M25 `item update --continue-on-error`~~ — **did NOT
  trigger.** The M25 pre-flight contract diff (`d5839a9`)
  ships a STANDALONE `partialSuccessBulkUpdateDataSchema`
  for the partial-success envelope; the v0.1 fail-fast
  `bulkLiveDataSchema` is preserved unchanged. The
  command's `outputSchema` registry field still uses the
  v0.1 single-item `projectedItemSchema` because the bulk
  envelope is emitted via `emitMutation` with a per-call
  schema parameter (not via the registry-side
  `outputSchema`). Action body branches on
  `parsed.continueOnError` at runtime; no schema union

- M27 webhooks — `webhook list` v0.3 might surface either

- M28 multi-level subitems — `item create --parent <iid>`
  might fork the create envelope for the v0.3 multi-level
  variant.

**Priority: LOW — watch-item; do not lift speculatively.**
The pattern is small (`z.union([..., ...])`) and the
discrimination is per-command-specific; a shared helper
would add indirection without saving LOC. Re-evaluate if
the discriminated-union variant emerges (`z.discriminatedUnion`
across 3+ commands sharing a `kind` discriminator).

### R-NEW-17 — redactor-pattern check at pre-flight. **Shipped: `85b93e8`** (M25 pre-flight kickoff cleanup bundle)

Surfaced at M23 implementation (`1f09a25` rename of
`column_not_found_on_board.details.column_token` → `column`).
The pre-flight pinned `column_token` as the detail-key name;
the redactor's `(token|secret|password|api[-_]?key)(?!s)`
pattern in `src/utils/redact.ts:55` scrubs every value under
any matching key. The integration emit path scrubbed the
column-token value to `[REDACTED]`, breaking the warning's
payload — caught at M23 impl gate, fixed via the rename.

**Lesson.** Pre-flight Codex rounds verify pure helpers
inline against empirical-probe fixtures; those don't pass
through the redactor. The integration emit path is where
the trap fires.

**Sites (1):** M23's `column_not_found_on_board` (caught +
fixed at impl).

**Trajectory.** Applied per-prompt as audit point W1 across
M24's four Codex rounds (pre-flight × 2 + impl × 2); all
four returned "W1: nothing flagged" against M24's new
detail-key surfaces (`unknown_event_kind.details.{event,
entity, occurrence_count, hint}` +
`internal_error.details.{item_id, board_id, hint, issues}`).
Three confirming repetitions cleared the template-fold-in
threshold; lifted at `85b93e8` (M25 pre-flight kickoff
cleanup bundle) into
`.claude/templates/codex-pre-flight-review.md` as the
template-stable W1 audit-point — milestone-specific

returned "W1: nothing flagged" against the new
partial-success-bulk detail-key surfaces (`item_id`,
`board_id`, `record_keys`, count fields, `hint`),
confirming the template-stable form holds.

**Risk:** low. The check is documentation-only; the
underlying redactor pattern is unchanged.

### R-NEW-19 — migrate manual `safeParse → ApiError(internal_error)` to `unwrapOrThrow`. **Shipped: `f4e8e1e`** (post-M23 audit).

Surfaced at post-M23 audit. `src/utils/parse-boundary.ts`
already exports `unwrapOrThrow(safeParseResult, { context,
hint, details? })` — the R18 lift (post-M5a) that wraps
ZodErrors into `ApiError(internal_error)` with the canonical
`details.issues` shape (path + message + zod code per
failing field). R18 swept the contemporaneous parse
boundaries (`api/board-metadata.ts`, `api/item-helpers.ts`,
`commands/emit.ts`); subsequent milestones (M21 OAuth,
M22 usage, M23 favorites + cross-board search) inlined the
same shape manually instead of calling `unwrapOrThrow`.

**Sites migrated (5; one excluded):**

- `src/api/oauth.ts:543` (M21 — OAuth token-exchange
  success response parse).
- `src/commands/auth/login.ts:154` (M21 — post-OAuth
  `account { id }` response parse; defensive path kept
  under the same `c8 ignore start/stop` wrap as before).
- `src/api/usage.ts:269` (M22 — `platform_api.daily_*`
  response parse).
- `src/api/board-favorites.ts:435` (M23 — Stage-1
  `Query.favorites` parse).
- `src/api/board-favorites.ts:478` (M23 — Stage-2
  `boards(ids:)` parse).
- `src/api/cross-board-search.ts:761` (M23 — cross-board
  walker per-board response parse).
- **Excluded:** `src/api/oauth-test-helper.ts:106` — throws
  `ConfigError`, not `ApiError(internal_error)` (test
  fixture mismatch is a config error, different semantic
  class); the rfc6749ErrorSchema.safeParse in oauth.ts is
  similarly excluded (intentional best-effort silent-on-
  failure parse wrapped in try/catch).

**Trigger.** **3-consumer threshold long-since exceeded;
helper exists; missed by M21+M22+M23 implementations.**
This mirrors the R-NEW-14/15/16 audit-pattern (Shipped:
`5a7c88d` post-M23): the M22 author wrote LOCAL copies in
`probes.ts` instead of finding the helper; M21+M22+M23
authors wrote LOCAL parse-failure ApiError throws instead
of calling `unwrapOrThrow`. The smoking gun is the same.

**Lift form.** Mechanical migration — replace each manual
block with `unwrapOrThrow(safeParseResult, { context, hint,
details? })`:

```ts
// Before (M23 favorites Stage 1)
const stage1Parsed = favoritesListResponseSchema.safeParse(stage1.data);
if (!stage1Parsed.success) {
  throw new ApiError(
    'internal_error',
    'Monday `Query.favorites` response did not match the expected shape',
    {
      cause: stage1Parsed.error,
      details: {
        issues: stage1Parsed.error.issues.map((i) => ({
          path: i.path.join('.'),
          message: i.message,
        })),
        hint: 'Monday may have amended the `Query.favorites` surface ...',
      },
    },
  );
}

// After (R-NEW-19 lift)
const stage1Parsed = unwrapOrThrow(
  favoritesListResponseSchema.safeParse(stage1.data),
  {
    context: 'Monday `Query.favorites` response',
    hint: 'Monday may have amended the `Query.favorites` surface ...',
  },
);
```

**Behavioural delta.** `unwrapOrThrow` adds `code: i.code`
per issue (zod-issue discriminator); the manual versions
omitted it. This is **additive on the error envelope** —
agents that didn't read `details.issues[].code` are
unaffected; agents that do gain the discriminator. The
message format also shifts from "X did not match the
expected shape" (manual) to "X — schema rejected the
payload at N issues" (helper), which is informationally
richer.

**Priority at trigger: MEDIUM.** Shipped at `f4e8e1e` in the
post-M23 audit (mirrors R-NEW-14/15/16's mass-migration
cadence: helper exists; milestone authors missed it; mass-
migrate in a focused commit after the audit surfaces the
miss). The 5 migrated sites carry message-regex assertions
in their cassette / unit tests; both old ("X did not match
the expected shape") and new ("X — schema rejected the
payload at N issues.") wordings satisfy the same regexes,
so the migration was zero-test-rewrite.

**Coverage delta (observed at ship time).** Branches margin
0.25pp → 0.24pp (within noise; the inline blocks each had a
covered `if (!parsed.success)` arm + the now-removed
`throw` block; the helper they migrate to is itself 100%
covered via `tests/unit/utils/parse-boundary.test.ts`).
The lift's load-bearing benefit is **code clarity +
consistency**, not coverage recovery.

**Risk:** low. Migration touched throw sites only; no
public envelope shape change beyond the additive
`details.issues[].code` slot. Cassette tests + unit tests
green under the new message wording.

existing shape (op-name-only or variable-keyed), the
inline-copies discipline holds even at 5 consumers — the

genuinely outweighs the duplication overhead.

### R-NEW-21 — `trialQuery()` + `RawErrors` lift into `scripts/probe/_lib.ts`. Shipped: `fa07fb4` (post-R-NEW-5 audit session).

Surfaced at post-R-NEW-5 audit (`fb77baf`). When the
R-NEW-5 lift's `introspectType()` consolidated the
`__type(name:)` introspection pattern across 9+ probe
scripts, an adjacent duplication surfaced: every M23
probe that runs *trial queries* (deliberately bad GraphQL
to confirm a field doesn't exist) defined its own
`trialQuery(label, query)` helper plus a `RawErrors`
interface for the structural cast.

**Sites (4):** all M23 probes —
- `scripts/probe/m23-favorites.ts`
- `scripts/probe/m23-favorites-deep.ts`
- `scripts/probe/m23-hierarchy-item.ts`
- `scripts/probe/m23-hierarchy-object.ts`

Above the R-class 3-consumer threshold. The shape is
identical in structure across all four:

```ts
interface RawErrors {
  readonly errors?: ReadonlyArray<{ readonly message: string }>;
  readonly data?: unknown;
}

const trialQuery = async (label: string, query: string): Promise<void> => {
  console.log(`--- ${label} ---`);
  const r = (await gql<unknown>(query)) as RawErrors;
  if (r.errors !== undefined && r.errors.length > 0) {
    for (const e of r.errors) console.log(`  [ERROR] ${e.message}`);
  } else {
    console.log(`  [OK] ${JSON.stringify(r.data).slice(0, <N>)}`);
  }
  console.log('');
};
```

Minor variations across consumers:
- m23-favorites.ts echoes the query with `  query:`
  prefix + 400-char truncation.
- m23-favorites-deep.ts echoes the query without prefix
  + 800-char truncation.
- m23-hierarchy-{item,object}.ts skip the query echo,
  600-char truncation.

**Recommended shape:**

```ts
export const trialQuery = async (
  label: string,
  query: string,
  options?: { readonly echoQuery?: boolean; readonly truncateBody?: number },
): Promise<void> => { /* ... */ };
```

Default `echoQuery: false`, `truncateBody: 600` — the
two most common settings across the 4 consumers. The
opinionated default keeps the call-site terse:
`await trialQuery('(4a) me { favorites }', q);`.

**Trade-off.** The duplication is contained to disposable
gitignored probe scripts — exactly the trade-off R-NEW-5
balanced. The lift's value is in reducing future-pre-
flight probe-script LOC + giving the M23 "trial-query-
matrix" pattern a discoverable home in `_lib.ts` for
M24+M25+M26+M27+M28 pre-flights to reuse without
copy-pasting the helper. Same scope-discovery as R-NEW-5
applies: lift lives outside `src/**/*.ts` coverage scope,
so it doesn't move the branches margin.

**Priority was MEDIUM — shipped at `fa07fb4` ahead of M24
pre-flight kickoff (folded into the post-R-NEW-5 audit
session alongside R-NEW-22 watch-item documentation).**
The lift was ~48 LOC additive into `_lib.ts` (helper +
interface + docstring), carrying the same minimal
pure-helper cost R-NEW-5 already absorbed. Saves ~12 LOC
per future pre-flight that runs trial queries against
novel field shapes.

**Risk realised:** none. Pure helper, no I/O beyond the
shared `gql()` wrapper. Gates clean post-lift; the 4 M23
probe migrations stay local per the `scripts/probe/`
`.gitignore` carve-out.

**Two cosmetic deltas** in migrated probe output, both
acceptable for disposable probe scripts:
- `m23-favorites.ts` loses its `[OK] data: <body>` prefix
  (now `[OK] <body>`); the other three consumers already
  used the no-prefix form.
- `m23-favorites-deep.ts` gains a `query:` prefix when
  echoing (one of the two echo-enabled consumers already
  used it; unifying on the more-informative form).

**Scope-discovery confirmed:** same as R-NEW-5, the lift
lives in `scripts/probe/_lib.ts` which is outside the
`src/**/*.ts` coverage scope — no branches-margin
movement. Recovery candidate for M24 pre-flight kickoff
remains targeted seam-injected tests on
`src/api/cross-board-search.ts` defensive paths.

**Naming choice worth recording:** the interface was
renamed `RawErrors` → `ProbeRawErrors` to match the
existing `RawHttpResponse` namespace pattern in
`_lib.ts`. The two cross-board-search probes
(`m23-cross-board-search.ts`, `m23-cross-board-search-2.ts`)
use an EXTENDED `RawErrors` shape that includes
`extensions` on each error entry — they keep their local
interfaces since their shape is a different contract;
only the 4 trial-query probes converged on the lifted
helper's narrower shape.

### R-NEW-22 watch-item — probe-script `main().catch()` runner

Surfaced at post-R-NEW-5 audit (`fb77baf`). 14+ probe
scripts end with the identical 3-line tail:

```ts
main().catch((err: unknown) => {
  console.error('probe failed:', err);
  process.exit(1);
});
```

Minor variations: m21-oauth.ts says `'probe crashed:'`
instead of `'probe failed:'`. Otherwise the shape is
verbatim across every script.

**Sites (14+):** every probe script in
`scripts/probe/m{21,22,23}-*.ts` — well past the R-class
3-consumer threshold.

**Trade-off.** Each instance is 3 trivial lines of
boilerplate — total duplication ~42 LOC across 14
scripts. The lift would be:

```ts
export const runProbe = (main: () => Promise<void>): void => {
  main().catch((err: unknown) => {
    console.error('probe failed:', err);
    process.exit(1);
  });
};
```

Per-script tail becomes `runProbe(main);` — saves 3
lines per script, total ~42 LOC across the cohort, but
the saved lines are "always-defensive boilerplate"
rather than meaningful duplication. The lift reads as
slightly-overengineered for what it removes.

**Priority: LOW — watch-item.** The boilerplate is
trivial and self-documenting; lifting risks turning a
visible "every probe handles its own crash path" pattern
into a hidden indirection through `_lib.ts`. Fold into
R-NEW-21's commit ONLY IF that commit is already touching
every probe script (it's not — R-NEW-21 only touches the
4 trial-query probes). Otherwise leave the boilerplate
as-is; the duplication is acceptable for disposable
scripts.

**Risk:** trivial. The watch-item is documentation-only.

`mergeByTimestamp<T>(streams: readonly (readonly T[])[],
keyFn: (t: T) => string, idFn: (t: T) => string)` helper
— takes N streams, projects each to a (timestamp, id)
sort key, returns the merged stream. Lift to `src/utils/`
when the 2nd consumer surfaces.

**Likely future consumers:**

  account-scoped + board-scoped webhook deliveries, the
  CLI's `webhook history` (deferred) would merge them
  similarly.
- M28 multi-level subitem tree walk — sub-source merging

- Any v0.4 audit-feed surface (org-wide activity feed was

  per board would apply).

**Trigger.** LOW priority watch-item. Single consumer
today; fires at 2nd consumer.

**Risk:** trivial. The pure helper is ~5 lines; the lift
cost is hours not days.

### R-NEW-25 — fold "findings up front" instruction into R-NEW-6 template. **Shipped: `85b93e8`** (M25 pre-flight kickoff cleanup bundle)

Surfaced at M24 pre-flight Codex review rounds (`fbd70de`
+ `f7fac47`). The M23 impl-review truncation lesson
(v0.3-plan §15) flagged that Codex's generation budget
can get consumed by file-reading exec calls before the
numbered findings section is emitted — round 1 at
`1f09a25` truncated mid-trace after 46 file reads without
delivering numbered findings. The lesson: future reviews
should use a tighter prompt asking for findings UP FRONT
rather than after exploration.

M24 pre-flight's review prompts applied this lesson
inline with the instruction:

> "deliver findings up front, not after exhaustive
> exploration. ... Read the diff once, scan the audit
> points, then emit your numbered findings section. Use
> file reads to verify specific lines, NOT to survey the
> whole codebase. Aim to deliver the P1 / P2 / P3
> numbered findings section within the first 25-35
> minutes of generation budget, with the overall verdict
> at the end."

Both M24 pre-flight rounds returned numbered findings
cleanly without truncation:

- **Round 1** (against `bad98ba`): 2 P1 + 4 P2 + 1 P3
  numbered + per-watch-item coverage section + overall
  verdict.
- **Round 2** (against `fbd70de`): 1 P1 + 1 P2 + 0 P3
  numbered + per-watch-item coverage section +
  verification cross-checks (run `tsc --noEmit` + `eslint
  .` post-fix to confirm round-1 fixes didn't regress) +
  overall verdict.

The technique is validated. **The lift:** folded the
"deliver findings up front" instruction into the R-NEW-6
template at
`.claude/templates/codex-pre-flight-review.md` —
specifically as a top-of-prompt directive paragraph
BEFORE section 1 (header) so it sets the response shape
expectation early. Template-stable + reusable across all
future pre-flight rounds.

**M24 impl-review extension.** The technique was re-applied
inline at both M24 impl review rounds (`5f10cda` + `a024961`).
Round 1 delivered 1 P1 + 2 P2 numbered cleanly + W-coverage
+ verdict; round 2 delivered 1 P2 + 1 P3 numbered cleanly +
W-coverage + verdict — despite ~7800 lines of diff exploration
visible in the round-2 trace. Four total Codex rounds across
M24 (pre-flight × 2 + impl × 2), all clean.

**Template fold-in shipped at `85b93e8`** (M25 pre-flight
kickoff cleanup bundle, bundled with R-NEW-17 + R-NEW-27).
M25 pre-flight rounds 1+2 (`d5839a9` + `832a169` review
inputs) confirmed the directive worked template-side —
both rounds delivered numbered findings up front without
truncation despite the substantive diff. The validated-
rounds count for the directive is now 6 (M24 × 4 + M25
pre-flight × 2).

**Risk:** trivial. The watch-item is documentation-only;
the template fold-in is ~10 lines of prompt-template
text.

### R-NEW-26 watch-item — defensive abort-listener race guard in async test promises

Surfaced post-M24 impl (`4c83860`). Three tests in
`tests/unit/cli/run.test.ts` flaked under full-coverage
parallelism (124 test files in vitest's worker pool):

- `run — abort handling (SIGINT path) > a caller-supplied
  signal triggers a graceful 130 exit before timer fires`
- `run — abort handling (SIGINT path) > a caller-supplied
  signal with a non-sigint reason still surfaces the action
  error`
- `runWithSignals — SIGINT integration > a real SIGINT
  during action exits 130 with no envelope`

The race window: each test scheduled an abort with
`setTimeout(() => ctrl.abort({...}), 10)` BEFORE
`await run(options)`. Under heavy parallelism, commander
argv parse + action invocation can take >10ms before the
action's `new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
  signal.addEventListener('abort', () => reject(...));
  ... })` constructor runs. The abort then fires BEFORE
the listener registers. **Node's AbortSignal does NOT
replay 'abort' events for listeners attached after the
event dispatched** — listener is silently dead, action
waits for the 5000ms safety timeout, returns cleanly →
exit 0 instead of expected 130 / 2.

Visible symptom: the non-sigint variant flaked once in
local testing ("expected +0 to be 2"). The sigint variants
would also flake the same way under sufficiently heavy
load, even though they exited via the post-parseAsync
abort check rather than the action throw.

**Fix landed at `4c83860`:** each action's Promise
constructor adds a synchronous pre-check before listener
registration:

```ts
await new Promise<void>((resolve, reject) => {
  if (ctx.signal.aborted) {
    reject(new Error('aborted'));
    return;
  }
  ctx.signal.addEventListener('abort', () => {
    reject(new Error('aborted'));
  });
  setTimeout(() => { resolve(); }, 5_000);
});
```

This matches the production-side race-window guard pattern
documented at `.claude/rules/testing.md`'s
`c8 ignore start/stop` block-wrap section
(`src/commands/auth/login.ts:fetchAccountId`'s OAuth-
listener guards on a similar already-aborted condition).

**Stress-tested:** three back-to-back full coverage runs
after the fix all clean (2967 passed + 1 skipped each).
Without the fix, the non-sigint variant flaked roughly
once per 10-15 coverage runs in local testing.

**Trigger.** LOW priority watch-item. Fires at:
- next async-abort-handling test site that uses the
  `setTimeout(abort) + addEventListener('abort')` pattern
  without the sync `signal.aborted` pre-check; OR
- 4th occurrence of the same shape (currently 3 in
  `run.test.ts`) — at that point lift to a shared helper
  `buildAbortablePromise(signal, onAbort)` in
  `tests/_helpers/` that bakes the sync pre-check.

**Likely affected milestones.** M25 + M27 may add more
abort-handling tests if `--continue-on-error` (M25) needs
SIGINT-mid-bulk-walk coverage, or if webhook subscription
lifecycle tests (M27) need abort-during-handshake coverage.
Prophylactic application of the sync-aborted-check pattern
to any new async-abort-handling tests is cheap insurance.

**Test-file count parallelism is the symptom carrier.**
The flake surfaced now (post-M24) not because the bug
arrived with M24, but because the test-file count grew to
124 (vs 121 pre-M22), exercising vitest's parallelism
harder. Watch for more parallelism-sensitive flakes at
M27 + M28 when the file count grows further.

**Risk:** trivial. The guard is 4 lines of additional
defensive code per call site; the regression test surface
is already covered (the same test that flakes is the
regression test for the fix). Low-risk + high-value
documentation lift if a future session needs to
prophylactically apply the pattern.

### R-NEW-27 — `isPlainObject` / `isObject` / `isJsonObject` consolidation. **Shipped: `85b93e8`** (M25 pre-flight kickoff cleanup bundle)

Surfaced at post-M24 close-docs audit (2026-05-11). When
M24's `projectActivityLogRow` added a local `isPlainObject`
guard, a sweep of the codebase found **6 structurally
identical type-guards** for "value is a plain object (not
null, not array)":

Production (`src/`):
1. `src/commands/run-by-id-lookup.ts:77` —
   `isObject(v): v is Record<string, unknown>`
2. `src/commands/board/column-create.ts:309` —
   `isJsonObject(value): value is Readonly<Record<string,
   unknown>>`
3. `src/api/errors.ts:322` —
   `isObject(v): v is Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>`
4. `src/api/item-history-projection.ts:791` —
   `isPlainObject(v): v is Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>`
   (added at M24 impl)

Tests:
5. `tests/fixtures/load.ts:123` —
   `isObject(v): v is Record<string, unknown>`
6. `tests/e2e/fixture-server.ts:48` —
   `isObject(v): v is Record<string, unknown>`

**The shape is identical across all 6:**
```ts
typeof v === 'object' && v !== null && !Array.isArray(v)
```

The only variance is the type predicate's `Readonly` /
non-`Readonly` form (3 vs 3 — both forms are present).
The `Readonly` form is the more defensive choice for
consumers reading from unparsed external data.

**Above the 3-consumer threshold.** Well above — 6 sites.
The duplication has been latent across the codebase since
M0 (the fixture-server / fixtures-load variants came in
at M2; production variants have accumulated over M2 →
M24). M24 was the trigger fire: when adding a defensive
type-guard for a wire payload, the developer (Claude)
naturally wrote a fresh local variant rather than searching
for an existing one — the same "miss + mass-migrate"
pattern as R-NEW-14/15/16 (error utilities) and R-NEW-19
(safeParse → unwrapOrThrow).

**Shipped shape.** Lifted into `src/utils/json.ts` (new file)
at `85b93e8` as:

```ts
/**
 * Type-guard for "value is a plain JSON object" — narrows
 * `unknown` to `Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>`. Used
 * across the codebase for defensive parsing of external
 * payloads (Monday wire responses, JSON-from-disk, etc.).
 * Returns false for null, arrays, and primitives.
 */
export const isPlainObject = (
  v: unknown,
): v is Readonly<Record<string, unknown>> =>
  typeof v === 'object' && v !== null && !Array.isArray(v);
```

All 6 sites migrated; each local helper deleted. The 3 sites
that previously used non-`Readonly` predicates widened to
the `Readonly` form — behavior-neutral in practice (none of
the 3 consumers mutated the guarded value). The 2 test
sites that previously omitted the `!Array.isArray(v)` clause
widened to the stricter form — analysis at the M25 prep
audit confirmed behavior-neutrality (neither test site
passes arrays in practice; the downstream `Array.isArray`
checks would have failed anyway).

**Migration delta:** `-37 lines` total
(`-24 lines` deleted local helpers + `-13 lines` deleted
comment / boilerplate); `+29 lines` net add (`+22 lines` new
`src/utils/json.ts` with module docstring + `+7 lines` new
`import` statements across 6 sites). Net `-8 lines` of
production code; branches margin recovered 0.55pp → 0.70pp
(5 duplicate branch sites removed; the lifted helper adds
one branch, net `-4 branch denominator`).

**Bundled with R-NEW-25 + R-NEW-17 template fold-ins.** All
three lifts shipped in one commit (`85b93e8`) per the
"Next session — likely scope" plan flagged at M24 close.
Single-commit bundling kept the M25 pre-flight history
clean — Commit 1 = R-class cleanup, Commit 2 = M25
pre-flight contract diff (`d5839a9`), Commits 3a + 3b =
Codex round-1 / round-2 fix-ups.

**Risk:** trivial. Type-guard semantics are identical
across all variants; the migration is `replace_all`-shape
search-and-replace. The 3 non-`Readonly` sites widen
their type predicates to `Readonly` — this is type-
narrowing, not type-widening, so consumer code keeps
compiling.

### R-NEW-28 watch-item — pre-flight behavioral-equivalence audit for opt-in flag extensions to existing verbs

Surfaced at M25 pre-flight Codex review rounds 1 + 2
(`832a169` round-1 fixes + `67df582` round-2 fixes). Two
distinct contract drifts surfaced because the M25
pre-flight extends an EXISTING fail-fast bulk path
(`item update --where`) with a NEW opt-in partial-success
flag (`--continue-on-error`). The class of bug: when a
pre-flight extends an existing verb with a parallel
opt-in path, the new path can silently drift from the
existing path's contract on every shared input/state.

**The two M25 drifts caught:**

1. **Round 1 P1-1 — per-target error code semantic
   equivalence** (`docs/cli-design.md §6.4 / §6.5 stable-
   code rule`). The v0.1 fail-fast bulk path applies
   `foldAndRemap` per-item to remap stale-cache
   `validation_failed` → stable `column_archived`. The
   M25 wrapper signature originally lacked the
   `foldAndRemap` context (`resolverWarnings` /
   `remapColumnIds` / `env` / `noCache` /
   `resolutionSource`), so per-item failures under
   `--continue-on-error` would surface
   `validation_failed` for archived-column root causes
   instead of the stable `column_archived` agents key
   off. Cli-design §6.5's "Bulk per-item failure" rule is
   that per-item error codes match the v0.1 fail-fast
   codes for the same root cause — a contract MUST that
   the new path silently violated. Fix at `832a169`
   added the 5 context fields to
   `RunPartialSuccessBulkUpdateInputs`.

2. **Round 2 P2-1 — empty-match envelope shape
   preservation.** The new §6.4 "Bulk per-item partial-
   success" sub-section initially documented that
   empty-match paths emit the partial-success envelope
   shape (`failed_count: 0` / `results: []`). The actual
   command body emits the v0.1 fail-fast empty-match
   envelope shape (`items: []`, no `failed_count` /
   `results`) — the partial-success envelope only
   materialises when at least one per-item dispatch
   fires. Fix at `67df582` documented the v0.1
   shape-preservation rule explicitly + pinned the agent-
   visible discriminator (probe `data.summary.matched_count`
   first; zero matches → v0.1 shape; non-zero with
   `--continue-on-error` → partial-success shape).

**Class of bug.** When a pre-flight diff extends an
existing verb with a NEW opt-in flag adding a parallel
path, BOTH paths share inputs / state / external
contracts. Audit class:

- **Per-target error code semantics** — does the new
  path surface the SAME stable codes the existing path
  does for the same root causes? (M25 P1-1)
- **Empty-input / no-target envelope shape** — does the
  empty-input handler emit the EXISTING shape, or does
  the new opt-in's envelope shape leak into the
  empty-case? (M25 P2-1)
- **Confirmation gate firing** — does the new opt-in
  inherit the same `--yes` gate the existing path uses?
  (M25 W4 — confirmed nothing-flagged; gate is
  orthogonal)
- **Source aggregation rules** — does the new path
  contribute the same `SourceAggregator` legs as the
  existing path? (M25 wrapper passes a constant
  `'live'` dispatch source — matches the fail-fast
  path's terminal `record('live', null)`)
- **Resolver-warning propagation** — does the new path
  fold the same resolver warnings into the same
  `details.resolver_warnings` slot? (M25 P1-1 covered
  this — the threaded `resolverWarnings` field exists
  for this purpose)
- **Pre-network argv validation** — does the new opt-in
  rejection on invalid shapes (e.g. M25's "no
  `--continue-on-error` on single-item shape") fire at
  argv-parse time before any network call? (M25 W3 —
  confirmed nothing-flagged)

**Sites (1):** M25 `item update --continue-on-error` is
the first consumer. The M25 IMPL Codex review explicitly
exercised all 6 axes as numbered audit points (W2.1-W2.6
in `.review-prompt.md` Section 5); all 6 returned clean,
validating that the pre-flight rounds 1+2 had already
pinned the contract correctly and the implementation
faithfully shipped the pinned shape. **No new R-NEW-28
findings surfaced at M25 IMPL review** — the two impl
findings (P2-1 + P3-1) were documentation drift between
the pre-flight surface and the impl-era state, not
behavioral-equivalence drift.

**Trigger.** **Watch-item — fires at any future pre-flight
diff that extends an existing verb with an opt-in flag
adding a parallel path.** Likely fire sites:
- v0.4 `--concurrency` flag extending bulk verbs from
  sequential to parallel dispatch (cli-design §9.3
  forward-ref).
- Any v0.x flag-shaped extension to existing verbs that
  doesn't change the wire shape but adds a parallel
  envelope path.
- v0.3-M27 if `webhook list` or `notification send` later
  add bulk-target opt-ins.

The watch-item is process-discipline: when the next such
pre-flight prompt is drafted, the Codex pre-flight review
should include explicit audit points for behavioral
equivalence across each of the 6 axes above. **M25 IMPL
ratified the 6-axis script** — pre-flight + impl review
rounds for any future opt-in-flag-extending milestone
should bake all 6 axes as numbered W{N} items, mirroring
how M25 IMPL did it. Promotion to W{N}-stable template
section deferred since the milestone shape is specific
to opt-in-flag-extends-existing-verb-with-parallel-path
(not every milestone fits the shape).

**Priority: LOW — watch-item; process discipline, not
code lift.** The pattern is documentation-only; the
underlying contract MUST is already pinned in cli-design
§6.5. The watch-item is a reminder for future pre-flight
prompt drafters to surface behavioral-equivalence audit
points so Codex catches drift inline rather than
post-feat-merge.

**Risk:** trivial. The watch-item is a process pointer,
not a code change.

### R-NEW-29 — `executeItemMutation` lift to `src/api/item-mutation-execute.ts`. **Shipped: `78889df`** (M25 IMPL kickoff)

Surfaced at M25 IMPL kickoff (2026-05-11). The
`executeMutation` helper in
`src/commands/item/update.ts` was the per-item wire-call
dispatcher for the three Monday `change_*` mutations
(`change_simple_column_value` / `change_column_value` /
`change_multiple_column_values`). Pre-M25 it had 2
consumers — the single-item action body's mutation
dispatch + the fail-fast bulk loop's per-item dispatch.
M25 IMPL would introduce a 3rd consumer (the partial-
success bulk wrapper at
`src/api/partial-success-bulk.ts:runPartialSuccessBulkUpdate`'s
per-item dispatch callback). The R7/R8 timing rule fired
at the 3-consumer threshold.

**Sites (3):**
- `src/commands/item/update.ts` single-item action body
  mutation dispatch (line 449 pre-lift).
- `src/commands/item/update.ts` fail-fast bulk loop
  per-item dispatch (line 1121 pre-lift).
- `src/api/partial-success-bulk.ts:runPartialSuccessBulkUpdate`
  per-item dispatch callback (new at M25 IMPL `fe15181`).

Above the R-class 3-consumer threshold. The shape was
identical across both pre-M25 consumers (`executeMutation(
client, {mutation, itemId, boardId, createLabelsIfMissing})`
returning `{projected, response}`).

**Lift rationale.** M25 IMPL's third consumer would either:
(a) re-import the helper across the `src/commands` →
`src/api` boundary — a cycle since `update.ts` already
imports `partial-success-bulk.ts`; (b) duplicate the
3 GraphQL mutation strings + the `projectMutationItem`
wrapper a second time. Both options are worse than the
lift. The lift is purely structural — no behaviour
change; the existing 1018 item-update integration tests
pass unchanged across the lift.

**Lift target:** new module `src/api/item-mutation-execute.ts`
exports `executeItemMutation` (renamed from
`executeMutation` for namespace clarity since the
helper now lives at `api/` scope where the bare name
collides with the broader concept of "any mutation
executor"; the prefix `Item` scopes the helper to its
specific contract). The 3 GraphQL mutation strings + 3
response interfaces + local `projectMutationItem`
wrapper all moved with the helper. Both update.ts call
sites threaded through the rename.

**Why "ahead of feat" commit cadence.** Mirrors the
`85b93e8` R-class cleanup bundle that landed ahead of
the M25 pre-flight contract diff `d5839a9`. The
R-class refactor ships in its own commit AHEAD of the
feat commit so the feat diff stays scoped to the
behavioural change (M25 IMPL's runtime body + tests)
rather than the structural rearrangement. Codex impl
review's W8 audit-point verified the lift was
behaviour-preserving in a few minutes; auditing a
combined refactor-plus-feat diff would have taken
considerably longer.

**Coverage delta.** No measurable shift. The lifted
helper occupies the same branch denominator at its
new module location that it did at its old module
location; the call-site updates are import-line
substitutions only. The 18 lines of executable code in
`item-mutation-execute.ts` show as `LH:18` (all hit) in
`coverage/lcov.info` post-lift, mirroring their pre-
lift coverage at the `update.ts` location.

**Priority at trigger: HIGH** (3-consumer threshold +
imminent 3rd consumer + cross-`src/`-boundary cycle
risk). **Shipped at `78889df`** in the M25 IMPL kickoff
session.

**Risk:** low. The lift renamed the exported symbol
(`executeMutation` → `executeItemMutation`) but no
public output / envelope shape changed; the 3 call
sites all use the new name. Integration tests for
`monday item update` (1018 tests pre-M25 IMPL) green
across the lift.

### R-NEW-30 — `_shared.ts:resolveActiveDevProfile` lift. **Shipped: `19755e3`** (M26a IMPL)

Surfaced at M26a IMPL kickoff (2026-05-12). The
three setup verbs (`dev discover` / `dev configure` /
`dev doctor`) all open with the same profile-name
resolution preamble: re-run the `selectProfile`
priority (flag > env > default_profile > implicit-v1
sentinel) that `cli/program.ts`'s preAction hook uses
for token resolution, this time to surface the profile
NAME so the dev verb can read/write the
`[profiles.<name>.dev]` block. The 3-consumer trigger
fired inside M26a IMPL itself; the lift shipped
preemptively rather than as a follow-up.

**Sites (3 at M26a; 13 expected at M26b):**
- `src/commands/dev/discover.ts` action body — picks
  up the active profile name to build the output
  envelope's `profile` slot + scope the optional
  `--apply` write.
- `src/commands/dev/configure.ts` action body — picks
  up the profile name to scope the write-back via
  `saveDevMapping`.
- `src/commands/dev/doctor.ts` action body — picks up
  the profile name to scope `loadDevMapping`'s read +
  the output envelope's `profile` slot.
- M26b's 10 workflow verbs (sprint/epic/release/task ×
  verbs) all need the same preamble — landing the lift
  at M26a means M26b verbs inherit it without
  duplication.

**Lift target:** new module `src/commands/dev/_shared.ts`
exports `resolveActiveDevProfile(ctx, programOpts)`
returning `{ name, homeOptions }`. The helper surfaces
`config_error` (NOT `dev_not_configured`) in implicit-v1
mode — the dev namespace is fundamentally named-profile-
only because the dev block lives under
`[profiles.<name>.dev]`, but the failure shape is
"profile not resolvable" (exit 3) not "dev block
missing" (exit 2).

**Codex M26a IMPL round-1 P1-1 fix.** The lift's first
draft swallowed `UsageError` from `parseGlobalFlags`
via try/catch, allowing `dev configure` to clobber
the on-disk `[profiles.<name>.dev]` block BEFORE
`emitMutation`'s re-parse threw usage_error. The
round-1 fix removed the catch; `UsageError` bubbles
synchronously before any file-write path runs.
Integration test
"Codex round-1 P1-1: invalid global flag leaves
config.toml unchanged" pins the regression for the
M26b workflow verbs (which all share this preamble).

**Priority at trigger: HIGH** (3-consumer threshold +
write-side verbs depending on the preamble landing
safely before any IO).

**Risk:** low. The helper's surface is narrow (one
function call); the 3 setup-verb action bodies each
have one call site. M26b's 10 workflow verbs inherit
without modification.

### R-NEW-32 — `loadDevMappingOrEmpty` helper. **Status: watch-item (2 consumers, M26a).**

Surfaced at M26a IMPL close (2026-05-12). The
"load existing dev mapping, swallow `dev_not_configured`
to fall back to empty" pattern appears verbatim in two
M26a action bodies — both `dev discover --apply`'s
additive-merge path and `dev configure`'s additive-
merge-with-existing-block path use the same 11-line
try/catch shape. M26b's workflow verbs DON'T fire this
pattern: they surface `dev_not_configured` (exit 2)
when the dev block is absent rather than swallowing it.

**Sites (2 at M26a):**
- `src/commands/dev/discover.ts` `--apply` path (line
  103–117 post-IMPL).
- `src/commands/dev/configure.ts` additive-merge load
  (line 116–127 post-IMPL).

**Lift candidate:**
`loadDevMappingOrEmpty(profile, options)` returning the
existing mapping or `{}` if `dev_not_configured`. The
re-throw branch for non-`dev_not_configured` errors
(`config_error` on malformed TOML — currently
c8-ignored as defensive since `cli/program.ts`'s
preAction hook surfaces it first) goes inside the
helper.

**Lift rationale.** Both call sites have the SAME
shape: try → loadDevMapping → catch `dev_not_configured`
→ existing = `{}` → re-throw else. Zero divergence
between consumers. The helper would save ~9 lines per
consumer + collapse two `c8 ignore` directives into one.

**Priority at trigger: LOW** (2-consumer threshold;
M26b doesn't add consumers; a v0.4+ `dev migrate` or
`dev reset` verb would be the natural 3rd consumer).
**Watch-item:** fires at the 3rd consumer.

**Risk:** trivial. Helper is purely structural; both
call sites have identical shape post-lift.

### R-NEW-33 — HOME-tmp-dir integration test scaffold. **Status: watch-item (3 consumers, M26a; LOW priority).**

Surfaced at M26a IMPL close (2026-05-12). Three
integration test files now use the same
`mkdtemp(join(tmpdir(), '<prefix>'))` + `afterEach
rm({recursive: true, force: true})` pattern to seed an
isolated HOME for filesystem-touching commands:

**Sites (3 at M26a):**
- `tests/integration/commands/auth.test.ts`
  (`buildAuthTmpHome`).
- `tests/integration/commands/profile-resolution.test.ts`
  (per-test `mkdtemp` inline).
- `tests/integration/commands/dev.test.ts`
  (`buildDevTmpHome`, new at M26a IMPL).

Above the R-class 3-consumer threshold, but the
per-test seeding diverges substantially:
- `auth.test.ts` seeds the OAuth fixture JSON.
- `profile-resolution.test.ts` seeds varying config.toml
  content per-test.
- `dev.test.ts` seeds the credentials cache + optional

**Lift candidate.** A `useHomeIntegrationEnv(prefix)`
helper mirroring the existing `useCachedIntegrationEnv`
shape from `tests/integration/helpers.ts:210` (which
handles XDG_CACHE_HOME). Would deduplicate the
`beforeEach mkdtemp` + `afterEach rm` boilerplate
(~10 lines saved per consumer). Per-test seeding stays
in each file.

**Priority at trigger: LOW.** The deduplication value
is marginal (~10 lines per file × 3 files = 30 lines
saved against the cost of a new helper + 3 file
migrations). Fires meaningfully if a 5th+ consumer
appears (M27 webhook tests writing to HOME-scoped
state? unlikely; webhooks are remote).

**Risk:** trivial. Same shape as `useCachedIntegrationEnv`'s
