### R-NEW-31 — discriminated-union per-status detail schema pattern. **Status: watch-item (1 consumer, M26a; LOW priority).**

Surfaced at M26a IMPL round-2 P2-1 fix (2026-05-12).
The round-1 superRefine approach to enforcing
`details.reason` on `status: 'fail'` was structurally
invisible to `monday schema`'s `z.toJSONSchema(...)`
export — agents couldn't introspect the closed
`DEV_DOCTOR_REASONS` enum. The round-2 fix refactored
`devDoctorCheckResultSchema` into a structural
`z.discriminatedUnion('status', [ok, warn, fail])`
with per-status detail schemas
(`okCheckDetailsSchema` / `warnCheckDetailsSchema` /
`failCheckDetailsSchema`), each carrying its own
required/optional `reason` field shape + open-ended
extras via `.loose()`.

**Sites (1 at M26a):**
- `src/api/dev-conventions.ts:devDoctorCheckResultSchema`
  (M26a IMPL `c70deb3` round-2 P2-1 fix).

**Lift candidate (future).** A generic "per-status
detail discriminated union" helper would help any
verb whose output carries a status discriminator with
per-status detail variability. M27 webhook list (per-
webhook status?) or M28 release-prep readiness checks
might surface 2nd + 3rd consumers.

**Priority at trigger: LOW.** Single consumer at
M26a; pattern is well-encapsulated within
`dev-conventions.ts`. The lift would create more
surface than it saves at 1 consumer.

**Risk:** trivial. Watch-item only; nothing to ship
yet.

**Generalizable lesson.** Per the round-2 finding,
agents introspecting via `monday schema` rely on
STRUCTURAL zod constructs (`discriminatedUnion`,
`intersection`, literal types, enum schemas) rather
than refinements (`.refine`, `.superRefine`).
Refinements stay useful for cross-field invariants
(e.g. `dev configure`'s "at least one --*-board flag"
superRefine), but contract surface that agents
should introspect MUST live in the structural shape.

### R-NEW-35 — `_shared.ts:requireDevBoard` slot-check helper. **Shipped: `10cd1c5`** (M26b IMPL)

Surfaced at M26b IMPL kickoff (2026-05-12). Every M26b
workflow verb opens its action body with the same 6-line
slot-check shape: pull `mapping[<noun>_board]`, check
`undefined`, throw `dev_not_configured` with
`details.slot` + `details.profile` + `details.hint`
pointing at `monday dev configure --<noun>-board <bid>`
or `monday dev discover --apply`.

**Sites (10 at M26b IMPL):** one per workflow verb —
`dev sprint current/list/items` + `dev epic list/items`
+ `dev release list` + `dev task list/start/done/block`.
Each verb consults a noun-specific slot (`tasks_board` /
`sprints_board` / `epics_board` / `releases_board`); the
sprint-items / epic-items / task-list-with-sprint verbs
consult two slots (their primary noun + the secondary
`tasks_board` for the board_relation walker target).

**Lift rationale.** 10 consumers crosses the R7/R8
3-consumer threshold by a wide margin; the inline
duplicate would have shipped 10× the same 6-line shape
across the verb files. The helper centralises the
`dev_not_configured` envelope shape so any future
addition (e.g. a `details.available_slots` echo) lands
once. Per round-1 P2-4 closure (M26 pre-flight): only
the doctor + workflow verbs surface
`dev_not_configured`; discover + configure swallow it as
a first-write case. R-NEW-35 lands the workflow side
of that closure.

**Risk:** trivial. Pure helper; one assertion per
consumer; no behavioral divergence.

### R-NEW-36 — `dev-conventions.ts` workflow-verb helpers cluster. **Shipped: `10cd1c5` + `34a5bc1`** (M26b IMPL feat + round-1 P2-3 lift)

Surfaced at M26b IMPL kickoff. Seven new helpers landed
in `src/api/dev-conventions.ts` to consolidate the
wire-call shapes the 10 M26b workflow verbs share. Each
helper crosses the R7/R8 3-consumer threshold.

**Helpers + consumers:**
- `walkDevBoardItems` — paginates `items_page` on a dev
  board, projects rows via the M4 `projectItem`
  contract. **7 consumers:** sprint current / sprint
  list / sprint items / epic list / epic items /
  release list / task list. Encapsulates the paginate
  + `fetchItemsPage` + `parseRawItem` + `projectItem`
  composition.
- `hydrateDevBoardColumns` — single `boards(ids:)` call
  returning a board's columns + complexity. **4
  consumers:** sprint items / epic items / task list
  with `--sprint` (the three relation-walker verbs) +
  `flipTaskStatus` (mutation hydrate). Surfaces
  `dev_board_misconfigured` with `reason: 'not_accessible'`
  when the configured board is inaccessible.
- `findRelationColumnIdToBoard` — scans columns looking
  for a `board_relation` whose `settings_str` parses to
  contain the target board ID. **3 consumers:** sprint
  items / epic items / task list with `--sprint`.
  Reuses the doctor's `parseBoardRelationTargets`
  parser (no duplicate parser).
- `extractLinkedItemIds` — handles both
  `linkedPulseIds` (legacy) + `item_ids` (newer
  2026-01) wire shapes for board_relation column
  values. **3 consumers** (same set as above).
- `resolveStatusColumn` — finds the status / color
  column on a board + builds a case-folded labels map.
  **3 consumers:** the three task mutation verbs via
  `flipTaskStatus`.
- `resolveCanonicalLabel` — case-insensitively matches
  a canonical Monday Dev label ("Working on it" /
  "Done" / "Stuck") against the column's actual
  labels; returns the exact stored case. Throws
  `dev_board_misconfigured` (`reason: 'no_status_column'`)
  when the canonical label isn't present. **3
  consumers** (same set).
- `flipTaskStatus` — composes `hydrateDevBoardColumns`
  + `resolveStatusColumn` + `resolveCanonicalLabel` +
  `executeItemMutation` into one wrapper that flips a
  task's status to the supplied canonical label.
  Returns `{projected, columnId, label, complexity}`.
  **3 consumers:** task start / task done / task
  block.
- `fireDevCreateUpdate` (round-1 P2-3 lift) — fires
  Monday's `create_update` mutation with a strict zod
  parse boundary (`create_update: { id: string } |
  null`) + dynamic operation-name builder (round-2 P1-1
  fix). **2 consumers** (task done with --message; task
  block always). Strictly speaking this is below the
  3-consumer threshold but the parse-boundary
  discipline matters at every site; the lift is
  justified by the Codex P2-3 finding that
  `client.raw<T>` is compile-time only and the
  malformed-response surface needed structural parsing.

**Lift rationale.** The 10 M26b workflow verbs would
have shipped ~150 lines of inline duplicate wire-call
plumbing without these helpers. The cluster lift keeps
each verb's action body focused on the verb-specific
logic (state filter, side-effect, etc.) while the
wire-call discipline (parse boundary, error-rewrap,
complexity threading) lives one module away. Mirrors
the M25 R-NEW-29 (`executeItemMutation`) precedent —
shared wire-call surface lifts to `src/api/*`, verb
files stay thin.

**Risk:** low. Each helper is purely structural; the
test suite (15 new unit tests in
`dev-conventions.test.ts` covering the pure helpers +
59 new integration tests in `dev.test.ts` covering the
verb compositions) verifies the lift's behavior
end-to-end.

### R-NEW-37 — Codex template audit-point for GraphQL operation-name / named-operation parity. **Status: watch-item (1 consumer at M26b round-2 P1-1; LOW priority).**

Surfaced at M26b IMPL round-2 P1-1 fix (2026-05-12).
The `fireDevCreateUpdate` helper shipped (round 1) with
a statically-named `mutation DevCreateUpdate` document
+ per-verb wire `operationName` override
(`DevTaskDoneCreateUpdate` / `DevTaskBlockCreateUpdate`).
GraphQL servers may reject mismatched
`operationName` + named-op pairs ("Operation
'DevTaskDoneCreateUpdate' not found"); live Monday
calls would have failed. Cassettes missed this because
`FixtureTransport` matches the wire's `operationName`
field, not the document's named operation.

**Lift candidate.** A new template W audit-point asking
the reviewer to verify, for every `client.raw` call:
1. The document's first `mutation X(...)` /
   `query X(...)` named-operation matches the
   `operationName` passed to `client.raw`.
2. If the document has multiple named operations
   (rare), `operationName` matches one of them.
3. Anonymous documents (no `mutation NAME` /
   `query NAME` prefix) pair with NO `operationName`
   parameter.

Each rule is fast to verify by reading the document
text + the call's `operationName` argument; the
template currently relies on the reviewer happening to
look at the document. Making it template-stable closes
the loophole.

**Priority at trigger: LOW.** Single-occurrence find at
M26b; the existing codex-pre-flight-review template
(R-NEW-6 / R-NEW-25 ratified) already catches most of
the W-audit-point class. Fires at the next milestone
introducing a new custom GraphQL mutation — M27
webhook surface (`create_webhook` / `delete_webhook`)
is the candidate. Add to the template after a
confirming repetition.

**Risk:** trivial. Template is documentation; the
template's regression surface is the next pre-flight
review's quality.

**Sites (1 at M26b):** `fireDevCreateUpdate` in
`src/api/dev-conventions.ts`. Fix shipped at
`078dae3` via the `buildCreateUpdateMutation` dynamic
builder.
